# Twangle — Roadmap archive

Finished phases, moved here from [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) so the roadmap stays short (phases 0–8 on 2026-10-11, 9–10 on 2026-10-12, 11–14 on 2026-10-13, 15–17 on 2026-10-14, 18–20 on 2026-10-14, 24 and 21 on 2026-10-15, 22, 25–27, 28, 29, 30 and 35 on 2026-10-16, 31, 32, 36, 33, 34 and 37 on 2026-10-17, 38 on 2026-10-19, 41 and 45 by 2026-10-26, 43, 47, 49 and 48 on 2026-10-26, 54 on 2026-10-27, 55 on 2026-10-28, 46 and 50 on 2026-10-29, 60 and 59 on 2026-10-30, 62 and 61 on 2026-10-31, 63, 64 and 65 on 2026-11-01, 66, 67, 68, 69 and 70 on 2026-11-02, 71, 72, 73 and 75 on 2026-11-03, and 78, 74, 77 and 80 on 2026-11-05, 79 and 76 on 2026-11-06 (on the `v2` branch), 83 on 2026-11-07, 82, 81 and 85 on 2026-11-08 (81 and 85 with their owner checks still open), with the *Music tools* overview and the finished part of Phase 23, more of it on 2026-10-24; finished owner items on 2026-10-16). The text is kept as it was when each item was ticked, so a phase still open names it by its old number: the open phases were renumbered on 2026-10-26 (44 → 46, 49 → 47, 50 → 48, 47 → 49, 46 → 50, 42 → 51, 23 → 52, 51 → 53, 48 → 54, 39 → 55, and the same day 49–55 → 50–56 when a new Phase 49 went in; on 2026-10-27, 54, 55, 56 and 58 → 55, 56, 58 and 59 when the website went in as Phase 54; on 2026-11-02, in the 2.0 part, 74 → 68 and 68–73 → 69–74 when No tour moved up to right after One look; on 2026-11-06, 81 → 82 when the TalkBack fix went in as Phase 81, before More languages; on 2026-11-07, 82 → 84 when the microphone fallbacks went in as Phase 82, right after it, 83 being the finished website makeover; later that day 84 → 87 when the tuning guide (85) and a high-contrast look (86) went in before it; on 2026-11-08, 56 → 89 and 58 → 90 when the watch app's launch went in as Phase 88, right after Phase 53). Phase 23's finished part stays here under that number; its open rest became Phase 52.

Status legend: ☐ todo · ◐ in progress · ☑ done

The ☐ items below are live tests the owner does by hand. They are tracked under **Owner checks** in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md).

## Phase 0 — Scaffolding
Goal: an empty multi-module project that builds and launches a blank Compose screen.

- ☑ Docs: `CLAUDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ROADMAP.md`
- ☑ Folder structure and Gradle module skeletons (`:app`, `:feature:tuner`, `:core:audio`, `:core:dsp`)
- ☑ Version catalog `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
- ☑ GitHub repo connected; branch → PR → squash-merge workflow (see CLAUDE.md); `.gitattributes` for line endings
- ☑ Install Android Studio (bundled JDK) and the Android SDK (API 37); generate the Gradle wrapper (9.8.1, checksum-pinned)
- ☑ Bump dependency versions to latest stable (AGP 9.4.1, Kotlin 2.4.21, Compose BOM 2026.09.00)
- ☑ `MainActivity` + `TwangleApplication` + `AppContainer` + Material 3 theme (dynamic color) + placeholder tuner screen + adaptive launcher icon
- ☑ `./gradlew assembleDebug test lint` passes
- ☑ CI: GitHub Actions workflow running `test`, `lint` and `assembleDebug` on every PR
- ☑ App verified on a device: Samsung Galaxy S26 (Android 16), launches cleanly in dark mode

## Phase 1 — DSP core (`:core:dsp`, pure JVM)
Goal: reliable pitch detection, proven by unit tests, with no device involved.

- ☑ `NoteMapper`: Hz ↔ MIDI ↔ note name/octave/cents, with configurable A4 (415–466 Hz); round-trips 60–1500 Hz within 0.01 cent
- ☑ `Tuning` model + presets: Standard, Drop D, Half-step down, Full-step down, DADGAD, Open G, Open D, Open E
- ☑ Nearest-string lookup for Auto mode (compared in cents; allocation-free; ties go to the lower string)
- ☑ `FrameAssembler` ring buffer (window 4096, hop 1024, no allocations per frame; accepts chunks of any size)
- ☑ `SignalGate`: RMS / dBFS level and DC removal (opens at −50 dBFS, 6 dB hysteresis)
- ☑ `YinPitchDetector`: difference function, CMNDF, absolute threshold, parabolic interpolation, confidence (plus 4 kHz low-pass and adaptive least-squares refinement, see ARCHITECTURE §3)
- ☑ `PitchSmoother`: median filter, octave-jump rejection, EMA on cents, note-change hysteresis (median 5, α 0.3, snap > 50 c, ±15 c hysteresis, octave confirm 3 frames)
- ☑ Test-signal generator (sine, saw, square, weak fundamental, noise, decaying pluck): band-limited, seeded; `testFixtures` so `:core:audio` can reuse it
- ☑ Tests: ±1 cent for 60–1500 Hz synthetic signals; no octave errors on the weak-fundamental case (measured worst: 0.1 c sine, 0.5 c harmonic-rich, < 1 c at 20 dB SNR)
- ☑ Microbenchmark: under 2 ms per 4096-sample frame on the JVM (`./gradlew :core:dsp:benchmark`: median 1.1 ms for YIN and for gate + YIN + smoother, JDK 17, dev laptop)

## Phase 2 — Audio capture (`:core:audio`)
Goal: live microphone frames reach the DSP pipeline.

- ☑ `AudioRecorder` interface + `AndroidAudioRecorder` (48 kHz, mono, PCM float, `Flow<FloatArray>`; pool of 4 reused chunk arrays, checked on the S26 by instrumented tests)
- ☑ Audio source selection: `UNPROCESSED`, then `VOICE_RECOGNITION` as fallback (also falls back if `UNPROCESSED` fails to open)
- ☑ Native sample-rate detection, plus handling for errors and a busy mic (`SampleRateSelector`; typed `AudioRecorderException`: permission missing, init failed, mic busy, read failed)
- ☑ `RECORD_AUDIO` permission flow: rationale, denied, permanently denied → settings (all paths checked on the S26)
- ☑ `FakeAudioRecorder` for tests (`testFixtures`; plays synthetic signals at mic pace, can loop or fail, tracks whether the mic is open)
- ☑ Temporary debug screen showing raw Hz, confidence and dBFS (S26: 440 Hz tone read as 439.98 Hz; mic released in the background)

## Phase 3 — Tuner MVP (`:feature:tuner`) → **v0.1 (internal)**
Goal: a usable guitar tuner.

- ☑ `TunerViewModel` + `TunerUiState` (PermissionRequired / Listening / Detected / Error; smoothing via `TunerInterpreter`, sticky target string in Auto mode)
- ☑ Needle gauge (−50…+50 cents) drawn on a Compose `Canvas`, with animated needle (spring; pinned at the edges beyond ±50)
- ☑ Big note name + octave, frequency, cents readout (S26: 194 Hz test tone shown as G3, 16 cents flat, 194.1 Hz); debug screen removed
- ☑ String row for the selected tuning; detected string highlighted (S26: 111 Hz tone lit A₂, 16 cents sharp)
- ☑ Auto (guitar) mode vs Chromatic mode toggle (segmented "Guitar / Chromatic"; switching keeps the mic open and the layout still)
- ☑ "In tune" state: |cents| ≤ 5 for ≥ 300 ms → color change (green needle, note, text and string; released past 7 cents; S26: 110 Hz tone turns green, 111 Hz doesn't)
- ☑ Mic is released whenever the screen is not visible (`MicLifecycleTest` on the S26: released ~5 s after leaving, reopened on return)
- ☑ Tested on at least 2 physical devices with a real guitar (owner, 2026-10-16)

## Phase 4 — Settings & tunings → **v0.2**
- ☑ DataStore-backed settings repository (`:core:settings`: A4, tuning, notation, theme, dynamic color, keep screen on; falls back to defaults on unknown or damaged data)
- ☑ A4 calibration (415–466 Hz, 1 Hz steps): new settings screen behind a gear button (slider, ±1 Hz buttons, reset); "A4 = 442 Hz" shown on the tuner; S26: saved across restarts
- ☑ Tuning picker (presets): "Standard ▾" above the string row in guitar mode, menu shows each preset's notes; saved (S26: Open G kept after restart)
- ☑ Manual string selection (lock target string instead of auto-detect): tap a string to lock it (outer ring, "Play the A2 string"), tap again for auto; released on tuning change
- ☑ Notation: sharps ♯ / flats ♭ / solfège (Do-Re-Mi): fixed-do with Si; note, strings, tuning menu and hint follow it (S26: "Sol♯₂" fits the string circle)
- ☑ Theme: system / light / dark, and dynamic color on Android 12+ (applied instantly; status-bar icons follow the app theme)
- ☑ Keep screen on while tuning (toggle, default on; S26: `FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON` set only while the tuner is shown)

## Phase 5 — Polish → **v0.9 (beta)**
- ☑ Haptic tick on reaching in-tune (one click from the vibration motor, switch in settings, default on; the same note doesn't tick again within 1.5 s; S26: plays even with the phone's touch vibration off)
- ☑ Accessibility: TalkBack live region ("E, 3 cents flat"), content descriptions, large-font layout, color-blind-safe in-tune indicator (shape as well as color) (throttled: new note after 400 ms, cents at most every 2 s; check mark beside the cents; everything reachable at 2× font; S26 accessibility tree reads "A 2, in tune")
- ☑ Landscape and tablet layouts (two panes once the window is ≥ 600 dp wide and wider than tall: gauge + note beside the controls, gauge shrinks to fit a phone on its side; settings in a centred column ≤ 600 dp; S26: landscape, 2× font and a simulated 800×1280 dp tablet in both orientations, locked string and mic survive rotation)
- ☑ Performance: profile CPU and battery; switch YIN to FFT-based difference if needed; Baseline Profile (S26: direct YIN took ~7 ms CPU/frame, 69 % of the app's cycles, so it now uses an FFT difference: JVM 1.05 → 0.48 ms/frame, app CPU with a note sounding ~45 % → ~20 % of one core, silence 7.8 % → 5.6 %; silent frames no longer wake the main thread; Baseline Profile generated on the S26 and committed, cold start ~89 ms with or without it; battery ≈ 0.8 mAh per hour of listening, CPU only)
  - ☑ Main thread recomposes the tuner on every frame while a note sounds (~11 % of one core on the S26, although only 4–8 frames a second change on screen): try rounding the readout to what is shown, or reading the fast-changing values only in layout/draw (readings that look the same are dropped before the main thread, the screen recomposes only when its text changes, the needle turns its own layer; S26 main thread with identical synthetic input: guitar mode 11.6 % → 5.1 %, chromatic 15.8 % → 12.2 % while drawing ~110 instead of ~75 frames/s, ≈ 2.1 → 1.1 ms per frame)
- ☑ Edge cases: Bluetooth headset mic, very noisy room, harmonics on high strings, sustain decay (synthetic, whole chain: right note at 0–10 dB white noise, ≤ 1 cent at 10 dB; hum 10 dB below the string ≤ 3 cents, hum alone shows nothing; strong-2nd-harmonic high strings no longer read an octave up; long decays stay on the note and the gate closes once; any rate 8–96 kHz within 1 cent (low rates upsampled); built-in mic pinned while a Bluetooth headset is connected)
  - ☐ Owner check with a real Bluetooth headset connected: the tuner still listens through the phone's mic and reads a string correctly (can't be tested without a headset)
- ☑ Localization scaffolding (English, Dutch): every text in Dutch, follows the phone or the Android 13+ per-app language; `LocalizationTest` guards missing translations and hard-coded text
- ☑ Visual redesign with seven styles the user picks from: Prehistoric, Middle Ages, Classic, Retro, Contemporary, Futuristic, Space Age
  - ☑ Style system (`:core:designsystem`): each style has a light and a dark palette (WCAG-checked), its own lettering (six bundled OFL fonts), shapes, a background and a hand-drawn dial; style cards at the top of settings; a change fades in (S26: all seven checked in light and dark)
  - ☑ Tuner screen redesign: note in the style's lettering (shrinks to fit), cents in a pill that fills when in tune, glow and pop on reaching pitch, style-shaped string chips with a backing, palette button opening the style cards on the tuner (S26: all seven styles with a live note from short laptop test tones; landscape, tablet and 2× font via the UI tests)
  - ☑ Wave view: the string's wave against the target's, drawn in each style, with the advice in words ("A little low – tune up"); a Settings switch goes back to cents (S26: every style rendered off-pitch and in tune, light and dark; UI tests for words, TalkBack, landscape and 2× font)
  - ☑ Calmer wave view: a low, frameless sound ribbon that pulses when off and settles into one smooth glowing band in tune, in each style's own marks (S26: every style rendered off, nearly there and in tune, light and dark)
  - ☑ Wave view without the tips in words: the dial already shows how far off, so the pill stays empty, also in tune (TalkBack still hears the cents)
  - ☑ Wave looks: pick the ribbon, stripes, dots, fireflies, a loop or ripples in Settings, with a preview card for each (S26: every look live, off pitch and in tune)

## Phase 6 — Release preparation
Goal: a signed, shrunk release build that is ready for the store, without publishing it yet.
- ☑ App icon, adaptive icon, splash screen (icon checked inside the safe zone with its monochrome layer; opening screen with the icon on gold, or on dark brown in dark mode)
- ☑ R8 minify + resource shrinking; release signing config (keystore kept out of the repo) (release APK 9.3 MB → 1.8 MB; signed with the upload key kept in `~/.twangle-keys/`; checked on the S26)
- ☑ Privacy policy (no audio stored or transmitted) (`PRIVACY.md` in English and `PRIVACY.nl.md` in Dutch, linked from the README; checked against the merged manifest: only microphone and vibrate permissions, no internet)

## Phase 7 — Clearer tuning
Goal: beginners know which way to turn the peg, and experienced players get the precision and numbers they expect. From the UX review of 11 October 2026.
- ☑ Direction hint: "Tune up ↑" or "Tune down ↓" under the note while it is off pitch (in the empty pill, with the needle and with the wave view), nothing extra once in tune; TalkBack says it too ("E 2, 12 cents flat, tune up"); English and Dutch; a Settings switch "Show tune up/down hint" (default on) turns off both the visible hint and the spoken "tune up/down" (S26: "Tune up ↑" shown in the wave view for a short flat test tone; UI tests for both views, in tune, switched off and TalkBack)
- ☑ Remember the mode: Guitar or Chromatic is saved in settings and comes back at the next start (an unknown saved value falls back to Guitar) (S26: switched to Chromatic, force-stopped, reopened in Chromatic from the first frame; repository and ViewModel tests)
- ☑ Chromatic readout: cents and frequency stay visible in chromatic mode, also with the wave view on, in the space the string row uses in guitar mode (switching modes still doesn't move anything) (with the needle they stay under the note; the slot now takes the taller mode's height, so a switch moves nothing at 2× font either. S26: "20 cents flat / 434.9 Hz" under "Tune up ↑" in the wave view for a short test tone, guitar and chromatic screens differ only in the switch, hint and slot; 11 new UI tests)
- ☑ In-tune precision setting: Relaxed (±5 cents, today's behaviour and the default) or Precise (±2 cents, released past 3); `InTuneDetector` tests for both
- ☑ String lock is easy to discover: a one-time tip above the string row ("Tap a string to tune only that one"), gone after tapping it or locking a string, and remembered so it doesn't come back; TalkBack reads it (the tip takes the tuning picker's place until dismissed, because an extra row pushed the strings off the S26 screen; S26: tip shown, gone after tapping it and after locking a string, still gone after a restart; ViewModel, repository and UI tests incl. TalkBack, 2× font, landscape and mode switch) (changed in the last Phase 7 step at the owner's request: the tip is now a speech balloon in the style's shape and colours that floats over the gap above the tuning picker with its pointer at the strings, or under the strings in landscape; the picker stays visible and usable, nothing moves when it comes or goes, and on an upright phone it steps aside while a note sounds)
- ☑ UI tests for the hint, the tip and the chromatic readout (TalkBack, landscape, 2× font); S26 check with short test tones in a few styles (101 tuner UI tests pass on the S26, each with a 60 s timeout; new ones cover TalkBack for "tune up/down" and the readout, Precise, the hint switched off, landscape, 2× font and the combinations. Fixed along the way: the screen moved half a line whenever a note started or stopped with the hint on, and on a phone on its side the hint pushed the frequency out of view. S26: Futuristic dark, Classic light and Retro light and dark with a 4 s tone 20 cents flat: "Tune up ↑", and "20 cents flat / 325.9 Hz" in chromatic mode; the balloon tip in all three)

## Phase 8 — Note names and visual polish
Goal: sharps, flats and tunings look and read the way musicians write them, and no style has small layout clashes. From the UX review of 11 October 2026.
- ☑ ♯ and ♭ look the same everywhere: smaller and raised like in sheet music, in a face that has the signs, in the big note, string chips, tuning menu, settings and dial labels, in all seven styles (today Classic draws a ♯ as big as the letter, and the menu and settings show tiny, sunken ones)
- ☑ Each preset tuning uses the spelling players know, e.g. Half step down = E♭ A♭ D♭ G♭ B♭ E♭ instead of D♯ G♯ C♯ F♯ A♯ D♯, in the menu, string row and readout (Do Re Mi follows: Mi♭ …); the notation setting still decides chromatic mode; tests in `:core:dsp`
- ☑ Waiting screen: the note placeholder no longer looks like a grey loading bar (the dash renders as a block in some faces) — now a faint ♪
- ☑ Style clashes fixed: Classic's frame line no longer runs through the mode switch; Futuristic's corner brackets no longer cover settings text when scrolled; the mode switch uses each style's main accent (Futuristic shows purple beside its cyan); screenshot check of every style, light and dark (the check also pulled the Middle Ages border in and gave the settings list 24 dp at the bottom, clear of every frame and Retro's stripes)
- ☑ Settings in three groups with headings: Tuning (A4, note names, in-tune precision), Look (style, light or dark, wallpaper colours, wave view and wave look), While tuning (keep screen on, vibrate, tune up/down hint)
- ☑ About section at the bottom of Settings: app version, the "free forever" promise, the privacy statement (Phase 6), font and open-source licences; the version number brought in line with the beta (it still says 0.0.1) (now 0.9.0-beta, versionCode 900; each font and library opens its licence text from inside the app; 7 new UI tests incl. 2× font and a phone on its side; S26: all seven styles with the About group and a licence open)
- ☑ Dutch TalkBack says note names the Dutch way: cis, des, es … instead of "C kruis", "D mol" (one helper for every spoken note name: readout, string chips, locked-string hint, tuning menu and the notation setting; English now also says the signs as words in the menu, hint and setting; Do Re Mi keeps "kruis"/"mol"; octave said as the number on screen, "cis 4"; unit tests for every spelling, UI tests in Dutch; S26: uiautomator dump with the app in Dutch)
- ☑ English and Dutch texts; UI tests at 2× font and in landscape for the new settings rows and the About section; S26 check in every style (three Dutch wordings made more natural: "Marge voor zuiver", "Kort trillen zodra de noot zuiver is", "Tik op een naam …"; 5 new UI tests check every heading, note-name choice, About row and credit is reachable and not cut off at 2× font, on its side and in Dutch, and the licence dialog at 2× font; S26: tuner, About and a licence in all seven styles, light and dark, plus Dutch and on its side; nothing else needed fixing)

## Phase 9 — Strobe tuner display
Goal: a strobe view as a third display next to the needle and the wave, for very fine tuning.
- ☑ Strobe model in `:core:dsp`: the pattern's drift speed and direction from the cents deviation, standing still when in tune (tests on synthetic signals, no allocations per frame). Still within ±0.5 cent, 0.4 stripes/s per cent beyond, at most 4 stripes/s (`StrobeMotion`)
- ☑ Display picker in Settings: needle, wave or strobe (replaces the wave-view switch; old setting carried over). Wave on → wave, off → needle; the strobe shows plain stripes in every style for now
- ☑ Strobe drawn in each of the seven styles, light and dark; the pattern moves in the draw phase only, so the screen doesn't recompose every frame (UI test steps frames and counts recompositions: none)
- ☑ TalkBack, landscape, tablet and 2× font for the strobe view (UI tests); S26 check in every style. TalkBack skips the stripes and hears the reading in words, unchanged while they move

## Phase 10 — Compact landscape mode
Goal: on its side, the phone shows only the meter and what is essential, big and easy to read from a distance.
- ☑ Compact layout in landscape: the meter fills the screen, with the note name, the cents and the in-tune signal; tuning picker, mode switch and buttons hidden. On by default (`TunerContent(compactLandscape)`); the gauge is ~450 dp wide on the S26, over twice the two-pane size; palette and settings buttons stay in the corners
- ☑ The target string still shows (small) in guitar mode; tapping the screen briefly brings back the controls. A small string row under the note; a tap (or TalkBack's "Show controls") shows the two-pane layout for 5 s, longer while touched or while the tuning menu is open
- ☑ Settings switch to keep the full two-pane landscape layout instead. "Full layout on its side" at the end of the Look group, off by default; saved in DataStore and followed live by the tuner
- ☑ In each style, light and dark, on phone and tablet, with TalkBack and 2× font (UI tests); S26 check (`CompactLandscapeStylesTest`, 28 tests: in all seven styles, on a phone and a tablet on their side, the needle, wave and strobe in guitar and chromatic mode fit beside a meter at least half the width, nothing overlaps or sits under the corner buttons; 2× font reachable; the meter drawn in light and dark; a tap and TalkBack's "Show controls" bring back the controls. No layout problems found. S26 on its side: Contemporary and Retro dark, Retro, Futuristic and Prehistoric light, the controls on a tap and gone after 5 s, a short test tone in Prehistoric)

## Phase 11 — A wave that helps you tune
Goal: the wave view gives the same "getting closer" cue as the strobe instead of being eye candy. Standing still means in tune; the drift speeds up the further off the note is; the shape still shows how far off from a distance.
- ☑ One motion for every wave look: the drift follows the strobe's speed rule (`StrobeMotion.driftSpeed`: still within ½ cent, faster per cent off) from the exact cents, not the in-tune flag, so the last few cents still show. The shape keeps showing distance up to 50 cents (deeper, more pulses further off) and smooths out close to the note. JVM tests for the drift at 0, ±1, ±3, ±5 and ±30 cents
- ☑ Temporary Settings switch "In tune: breathe / hold still", so the owner can compare both on the phone (English and Dutch, saved in settings); the losing option and the switch go after the owner check above (Settings → "When in tune", under the wave looks)
- ☑ Each look carries the cue in its own way, still in tune and drifting at strobe speed off pitch: stripes drift (not only lean), ripples spread out (sharp) or draw in (flat), fireflies drift and line up, dots scroll, the loop tumbles at the beat rate and settles into a ring. The Settings previews move the same way. Stripes only got the drift: the strobe look replaces them in the next step. Ripples swell unevenly off pitch, the loop spins like a coin with its near side sweeping the way the note is off, fireflies glide into an even line on the note; JVM tests for direction, stillness on the note and no jumps
- ☑ The strobe becomes one of the wave looks, so "Display" is just Needle or Wave; a saved Strobe display opens as Wave with the strobe look. **Owner decided:** 6 looks in the 3×2 grid, the strobe replacing the stripes look (final six: strobe, ribbon, dots, fireflies, loop, ripples). The strobe card comes first; a saved stripes look also opens as the strobe. Its marks now fade in and out at the ends, it rests at half height without a note, and on the note it holds still or swells gently with the temporary switch; the separate strobe view is gone (JVM tests for every old saved value)
- ☑ Every look checked in each style, light and dark, with TalkBack, on a phone and tablet on its side (also compact mode) and at 2× font; ARCHITECTURE.md updated; S26 check with short test tones. Screenshots of all six looks in all seven styles found two things to fix: the look names broke mid-word at 2× font in Dutch ("Strobo-scoop"; now two cards to a row from 1.3× and names shrink to fit one line), and the small looks were dim off pitch on dark backgrounds (brighter tint and fireflies). New UI tests: every look stays inside its box in every style, light and dark; TalkBack skips every look; the look cards are radio buttons in English and Dutch; names fit at 1.3× and 2× font

## Phase 12 — How well the tuner hears you
Goal: show how well the guitar comes through without adding clutter. The display itself looks crisp when the sound is good and faint when it isn't, and a tip appears only when the player can do something about it. **Owner decided (2026-10-12):** no separate meters; signal bars only as an optional extra in Settings.
- ☑ Noise-floor estimate in `:core:dsp`: tracks the room's background level between notes, plus a signal-to-noise figure while a note sounds (synthetic tests, no allocations per frame) — `NoiseFloorTracker`: minimum of note-free frames over 2 s; floor within 0.5 dB, SNR within 1 dB from 0 to 40 dB
- ☑ Clarity figure: how clean the pitch is (from YIN's confidence), so the meter shows how hard the tuner has to work — `Clarity.of`: 1 at 20 dB SNR or cleaner, ≈ 0.5 at 6 dB, ≈ 0 at 0 dB, the same for every pitch and timbre
- ☑ A steady hint line, first, because the tips build on it: a message must stay for a minimum time before it changes, and switching between "in tune", "Tune up ↑" and "Tune down ↓" uses a margin (hysteresis) so it no longer flickers on and off near the note. **Owner reported:** the tune-up / tune-down hint flickers on and off close to in tune. JVM tests that feed a reading wobbling around the in-tune edge and check the hint changes at most once — `SteadyHint` (each message settles 0.2 s for a direction, at once for in tune, then stays ≥ 0.5 s) after `TuningCue` (direction kept inside the in-tune window); the shown hint also decides "in tune", so colour, tick and hint agree
- ☑ The display shows how well it hears: the needle and every wave look are drawn crisp and solid with a strong, clean signal and turn faint and soft when it is weak or noisy, in every style, light and dark. This must never be confused with the tuning cue (motion and shape stay the tuning cue) and must not flicker (smoothed, JVM tests for the mapping from volume, noise and clarity to one reception figure) — `ReceptionTracker`: lowest of level (−50…−35 dBFS), SNR (0…20 dB) and clarity, each eased fast up (0.15 s) and slowly down (0.5 s); needle and wave fade to 0.62 opacity with up to 2 dp blur, the resting look unchanged
- ☑ Tips in the existing hint line, only when the player can fix something: too soft ("Play a bit louder"), too noisy ("Too noisy here"), too loud ("Too loud, move the phone back"). They use the steady hint line (no flicker when the level hovers at a limit: separate on and off limits plus a minimum time shown), never hide the tune-up / tune-down cue while a clean note sounds, English and Dutch, read out politely by TalkBack — `TipCue` + its own `SteadyHint`: too loud from a clipping peak (−0.5 / −3 dBFS), too soft from the note's loudest level (< −44 dBFS, so a fading tail never asks), too noisy from the room (−38 / −42 dBFS) or the note's best SNR (< 8 dB); tips settle 0.5–1 s, stay 3 s and hold through the gap between plucks. A tip takes an existing place (the "Play a string" line, the wave's pill, or the frequency under the needle, so the direction stays) on one line, so nothing moves
- ☑ Optional signal bars, for the curious: a Settings switch "Show signal bars" (off by default) adds a small reception icon with bars near the note, like a phone's signal bars; tapping it shows the volume, noise and clarity figures. Its bars change slowly enough not to flicker. English and Dutch, TalkBack — four bars in the free slot at the right of the note's pill (nothing added, nothing moves), lit from the reception figure with a 0.08 margin; between notes the last count stays, dimmed. The tap panel shows volume, background (dB) and clarity (%), updated 4× a second while open
- ☑ Checked in landscape (also compact mode), at 2× font, in every style light and dark (UI tests); S26 check in a quiet and a noisy room with short test tones — compact-landscape tests in all seven styles, light and dark: the bars sit beside the meter, stay reachable at 2× font and are clearly drawn; the longest tip fits one line at 2× font. On the S26 hiss brought "Too noisy here" in place of "Play a string" and cleared after it; a laptop note (−46 dB at the phone) brought "Play a bit louder", one bar, and the figures panel updating live. The laptop can't make the mic clip, so "Too loud" is checked by JVM tests only (owner check above)

## Phase 13 — A meter with a wow factor
Goal: the meter above the note (the half-circle dial and its needle, shown with both displays) is the first thing anyone sees and the app's main selling point, so it should look stunning. Players pick one of six meter looks, the way they pick a wave look. Every look must still read at a glance from arm's length (left = flat, right = sharp, centre = in tune), move smoothly at the screen's full frame rate, and give a calm, satisfying moment when the string reaches the note.

**Owner decided (2026-10-12):** after two rounds of moving mock-ups (Lock-on, Pendulum, Eclipse, Sunrise and Balance scale dropped), the six looks in this order, with Classic as the default for new installs:
1. **Classic**: today's dial, polished with depth, a glass shine, a needle shadow and a little mechanical overshoot when a new string is plucked
2. **Light arc**: a curve of glowing segments that light up from the centre out to the reading with a soft halo and a fading trail
3. **Spirit level**: a bubble in a curved glass tube that floats to the side the note is off and settles; in tune it rests between the two marks
4. **Rolling scale**: the scale is printed on a turning drum seen through a window and rolls past a fixed centre mark, like a ship's compass
5. **Comet**: a bright comet runs along the arc with a glittering tail that settles and softens on the note
6. **Studio meter**: a backlit meter like a recording desk's, with a real needle and a small lamp that softly lights on the note

Also decided: the in-tune moment must be smooth and not gimmicky (a soft glow that eases in, holds gently and fades, no bursts, sparks or flashes), and all six looks share **one art style, as if made by the same artist** (the same lines, numbers, glass, light and glow). The looks take only colours (and at most the font) from the current style; each style's own finishing touches for the meter wait for the theme redesign in Phase 14.

- ☑ Preview for the owner before anything is built: moving mock-ups in three styles (Prehistoric, Contemporary, Space Age), light and dark, as a page the owner opened on the phone; the owner chose the six above
- ☑ Meter look setting: `MeterLook` with the six looks, saved in settings (key `meter_look`; an unknown saved value gives Classic), JVM tests. One shared frame for every look so nothing else on the screen moves: the same 2:1 box and ±50 cents, readings beyond ±50 pinned at the edge, the "in tune" colour and check mark, the reception fade and blur from Phase 12, hidden from TalkBack. Motion lives in the layer and draw phases only, with no allocations per frame (paths and brushes made once), like the needle today. The shared art language (line weights, numbers, glass, light, colour roles) written down in ARCHITECTURE.md — `MeterFrame` hands every look one `MeterReading`; until a look's own art is built it shows the Classic dial, and the meter picker comes next
- ☑ Meter picker in Settings ("Meter", in the Look group next to the wave looks): a 3×2 grid of cards with moving previews, radio buttons for TalkBack, English and Dutch names that fit on one line at 2× font — `MeterLookPicker` above the display choice, shown with both displays; each card draws the look's real art (`MeterLookArt`, moved to `:core:designsystem` `meter/` so tuner and settings share it) through one gentle demo loop, so a look's own art appears in its card as soon as it is built; shares `LookCardGrid` with the wave picker (two to a row from 1.3× font); UI tests for six cards, each tap, TalkBack and names at 2× font; on the S26 the cards move and a pick survives a restart
- ☑ Looks 1 and 2 (Classic polished, Light arc) in the shared art style, coloured by all seven styles, light and dark — the shared art pieces (plate, recessed glass, marks and numbers, needle, marker shadow, glow, colour roles) in `meter/MeterArt.kt` for every look; Classic's needle and shadow only turn in their layers, on a slightly underdamped spring (overshoots about 9 % after a pluck); Light arc's 41 segments light from the centre to the reading with a halo and a fading trail; each look's own spring (`meterFeel`); the in-tune colour change as one eased amount (`MeterReading.tuned`), the glow comes later; JVM tests for the springs and the trail, UI tests in all seven styles light and dark; checked on the S26 with short test tones (Prehistoric light, Space Age dark)
- ☑ Looks 3 and 4 (Spirit level, Rolling scale) in the shared art style, coloured by all seven styles, light and dark — Spirit level: a curved glass tube tinted by the accent, the bubble drawn once and only moved, turned and stretched by its layer (it stretches a little while it moves fast), resting between two marks that fade to "in tune"; Rolling scale: a drum printed from −60 to +60 seen through a window, each mark placed, narrowed and faded by how far round the drum it is (numbers measured once), rolling past a fixed centre line with pointers, the zero bar on the side the note is off; JVM tests for the bubble's place and the drum's maths, UI tests in all seven styles light and dark; checked on the S26 with short test tones (Prehistoric light, Space Age dark)
- ☑ Looks 5 and 6 (Comet, Studio meter) in the shared art style, coloured by all seven styles, light and dark — Comet: the dial with a dotted path, a head drawn once and only moved by its layer, and a tail of the last 0.55 s (a preallocated ring stepped only while there is a tail) that thins, fades and shimmers gently, shortening to nothing as the head settles on the note; Studio meter: the same plate, glass, marks and needle on a face backlit in the accent, a flatter scale around a hidden pivot under a small cover, and a lamp that blends to "in tune" with the in-tune amount (its glow comes with the in-tune moment); JVM tests for the trail and the needle and lamp maths, UI tests for all six looks in all seven styles light and dark; checked on the S26 with short test tones
- ☑ The in-tune moment: each look's soft glow eases in when the note is reached, together with the existing vibration tick, holds gently while the note rings and fades when it leaves; it never restarts while the note holds and never flickers near the edge (it follows the steady hint's in-tune decision). JVM tests for when it starts and ends. It follows Android's "Remove animations" setting (no glow motion then, just the colour change) — `InTuneGlowRule` lights it with the tick and holds it 600 ms through a dropout of the same note; `MeterReading.glow` eases 0.4 s in / 0.6 s out; one shared `TuneGlow` (the mock-up's `tuneGlow`: "in tune" colour, 32 % dark / 16 % light, radial) drawn once per look under its marker and faded only by its layer's alpha, plus the studio lamp's halo; with "Remove animations" colour and glow snap; JVM tests for the rule and the easing, UI tests for every look in all seven styles light and dark; checked on the S26 with short test tones
- ☑ In-tune effect switch in Settings: "Calm" (default, the soft glow) or "Bold" (a stronger, larger glow, still smooth and without bursts), two choices next to the meter picker, saved in settings with an unknown value giving Calm, English and Dutch, TalkBack, JVM tests — `InTuneEffect` (key `in_tune_effect`), "In-tune effect: Calm / Bold" ("Effect bij gestemd: Rustig / Krachtig") as radio rows right under the meter picker, and the cards preview it; Bold is the same eased glow at 1.7 × the alpha (capped at 52 %: 52 % dark, 27 % light) and 1.35 × the size, the studio lamp's halo 1.7 × (capped at 85 %); both follow "Remove animations"; JVM tests for saving, the setters and the Bold numbers, UI tests for the choice (TalkBack, 2× font) and every look glowing inside its box in Bold, more than in Calm; checked on the S26 with short test tones
- ☑ Smooth on the phone: frame times measured on the S26 for every look in chromatic mode (meter moving all the time), no dropped frames at 120 Hz and main-thread work no higher than the needle's today (see the table in ARCHITECTURE.md) — `scripts/meter-frames.sh` (`MeterFramesBenchmark`; the benchmark build hears a synthetic ±30-cent sweep instead of the mic, and release builds never contain it); the meter now asks for 120 Hz while it moves (Android 16 gave it 60 Hz); every look at 119–120 frames/s with at most 0.5 % late frames (most repeat runs none), main thread 17–20 % and 1.4–1.7 ms per frame, against 22 % and 1.8 ms for the old needle on the same build and input; table "Meter looks (Phase 13)" in ARCHITECTURE.md
- ☑ Every look checked in each style, light and dark, with TalkBack, on a phone and tablet on its side (also compact mode, where the meter is biggest) and at 2× font (UI tests: inside its box, clearly drawn, skipped by TalkBack); screenshots of all six looks in all seven styles for the owner; ARCHITECTURE.md updated; S26 check with short test tones — `CompactLandscapeStylesTest` now checks every look in all seven styles on a phone and a tablet on their side (compact mode) and at 2× font, each size light and dark: clearly drawn inside its box, skipped by TalkBack, every other part of the screen unmoved and unchanged; `MeterLookScreenshots` (opt-in) and `scripts/meter-look-screens.sh` make 168 pictures (20 cents flat, and in tune with the Bold glow) on a contact sheet in `build/owner-screens/meter-looks/`; the pictures showed Space Age's spirit-level numbers almost touching, so close scale numbers are now set slightly smaller to keep 8u apart; checked on the S26 with short test tones

## Phase 14 — New theme styles
Goal: a fresh set of visual styles for the whole app, designed together so every screen, the wave looks and the six meter looks feel like one product. The current Prehistoric → Space Age line may stay, change or be replaced; the owner decides. **Owner decided (2026-10-12):** this phase comes straight after the meter looks.
- ☑ Preview for the owner before anything is built: moving mock-ups of the tuner screen (with a meter look and a wave look) and Settings, light and dark, in [docs/design/1.x/theme-styles-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/theme-styles-preview.html). Its top comment is the art language the build follows. **Owner decided (2026-10-13):** six styles, in this order: **Ancient** (Prehistoric and Middle Ages merged), **Classic**, **Retro**, **Contemporary**, **Futuristic** (the favourite, polished), **Space Age**. Contemporary stays the default for new installs. Each style has its own hue family around the colour wheel. The style is picked in Settings like the meter look and wave look; the tuner's palette icon and the style preview cards go. The meter is bigger, with no plate or frame, drawn straight on the background; its in-tune glow is never cut off at the meter's edges. No "A4 = 440 Hz" line on the tuner, and no fixed borders at the screen edges
- ☑ Style setting moves to the new set: every old saved style opens as its closest new one (Prehistoric and Middle Ages → Ancient; JVM tests for every old saved value); the style is picked in Settings as a card grid of mood cards (background, big letter, wave strip, string buttons), like the meter and wave looks, with English and Dutch names; the tuner's palette icon and the old style preview cards are removed. Until its own look arrives, Ancient wears the old Prehistoric palette, fonts and cave background; the Middle Ages font, background and marks are gone
- ☑ Tuner layout for every style: the meter larger and frameless on the screen background, the in-tune glow in an unclipped layer behind it, no "A4 = 440 Hz" line, no fixed borders at the screen edges, number faces with lining figures. Upright the meter spans the screen less 8 dp (dial radius 124u → 146u), mode switch at the top, strings at the bottom; one calm field under every look; tube, drum window and studio face keep their glass with a contact shadow; Classic's frame, the HUD brackets and Retro's bottom stripes went; every text style asks for "lnum". The mock-up's larger scale marks (13u majors, 12.5u numbers 27u out) and ♭ ♯ inside the box wait for the per-look finishing touches
- ☑ Each new style's palette, fonts and background, light and dark, with readable contrast for every text and mark: the mock-up's palettes copied exactly (each style its own accent hue), Ancient's real stone-wall look, Alegreya Sans (Ancient) and Bodoni Moda (Classic) number faces bundled, the mock-up's backgrounds drawn in code (quiet version behind Settings) in a cached layer, the mock-up's corners and string-button shapes (pebbles, ivory keys, hi-fi buttons, circles, neon tiles, capsules); every palette, light and dark, passes AA (4.5:1 text, 3:1 marks; scale numbers now ink 80 %, minor ticks 50 %). The buttons' inset shading and neon glow wait for the finishing touches
- ☑ Each style's own finishing touches for the six meter looks and the wave looks, still in the shared art style from Phase 13: the mock-up's needle, hub, arc and tick caps per style (Ancient's ticks drawn by hand), the larger scale (13u majors, 12.5u numbers 27u out), ♭ ♯ inside the meter (the row under it went), the mock-up's strobe and ribbon marks and periods per style, Classic's and Retro's shaded string buttons and Futuristic's neon outlines and glow. The spirit level keeps its Phase 13 tube (the mock-up's wider one touched the box's sides); its numbers are set up to 30 % smaller instead
- ☑ Every screen checked in each new style, light and dark, with TalkBack, in landscape (also compact mode) and at 2× font (UI tests); screenshots of every style for the owner; ARCHITECTURE.md updated; phone check — `TunerStylesTest` and `SettingsStylesTest` check the tuner (upright, at the owner's 1.25× text, full layout on its side), Settings and About in every style, light and dark: everything fits without scrolling and nothing overlaps or sits under the settings button, every part, row, card name and About text is wholly shown at 2× text (`assertWhollyShown`, now in `:core:testing`), the longest string names fit their buttons, and TalkBack hears every part, card and choice by name with which one is picked. A full `--all --thorough` run (about 15 minutes) heated the phone to 44.7 °C and five pixel tests ran past their 10 s limits; rerun cool (32 °C) only `CompactLandscapeStylesTest#onItsSideEveryMeterLookStaysInItsBox[ANCIENT]` (the first test of that run) still timed out, every other style passing it. On the Xiaomi 12X each style was checked light and dark, upright and on its side (compact and with the controls), at 1.25× and 2× text, with Settings and About: on its side Ancient's hand stencil and Space Age's stars covered the tune hint and strings, so on its side they keep to the strip left of the meter and the planet is sized by the shorter side. The double checks stay out of normal runs until Phase 23 (more screens are still coming). Screenshots (in tune, 20 cents flat and Settings, light and dark, per style, with a contact sheet each) in `build/phase14-screenshots/`

## Phase 15 — More instruments
Goal: tune more than a six-string guitar.
- ☑ `Instrument` model in `:core:dsp` with string counts and preset tunings: 7- and 8-string guitar, 4- and 5-string bass, ukulele (standard and low G), violin, viola, cello, mandolin
- ☑ Low notes: an 8192-sample window when the instrument goes below low E (down to B0 ≈ 30.9 Hz); ±1 cent tests from 30 Hz; CPU per frame measured on the test phone — tunings whose lowest string a semitone flat is under 62 Hz (7/8-string, basses, cello) read 28–1500 Hz; synthetic 30 Hz up within 0.52 cents (0.83 at 10 dB SNR); Xiaomi 12X: 0.74 ms CPU per frame (3.5 % of a core; 5.1 ms on a little core) vs 0.33 ms for guitar, after a cache-friendlier FFT
- ☑ Instrument picker (beside the tuning picker), saved in settings; the tuning menu shows only that instrument's tunings — "Standard ▾ Guitar ▾" on one line (names set up to 30 % smaller in wide faces, a line each at 2× text), English and Dutch names for every instrument and tuning; the mode switch now reads "Strings / Chromatic"
- ☑ String row that fits 4 to 8 strings in portrait, landscape and at 2× font — full-size buttons spread out while they fit; otherwise equal shares, shrunk alike (8 strings on a 360 dp phone: 41 dp each); names shrink to fit their buttons (also fixed Space Age's solfège at 2× text)
- ☑ phone check for each instrument with short test tones — Xiaomi 12X, 4 s laptop tones, all 10 instruments (and ukulele low G): every string the laptop can play showed the right string, note and cents (e.g. 20 cents flat read as 20 flat, 10 sharp as 10 sharp, in tune green), from C3 (130.8 Hz) up to E5; a note between strings or an octave off read honestly against the nearest string (G3 on bass: "G2, 1180 cents sharp"). The laptop speakers can't make notes under about 130 Hz loud enough at the phone (E2 and A2 failed on the plain guitar too), so B0 to A2 stayed at "Play a string" — a speaker limit, the ±1 cent DSP tests cover them; a real bass is an owner check. No false note: silence and room noise with 5-string bass, bass, 8-string and cello (also chromatic), and short 50 and 60 Hz hum bursts, all stayed at "Play a string"

## Phase 16 — Custom tunings
Goal: players can make, name and keep their own tunings.
- ☑ One combined picker on the tuner ("Guitar · Standard ▾") in place of the separate tuning and instrument menus; tapping it opens a panel from the bottom with the instruments along the top and that instrument's tunings below, the current one ticked, as the approved mock-up [docs/design/1.x/combined-picker-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/combined-picker-preview.html) (instrument first, a thin outline in the style's button shape, instruments in one sideways-scrolling row). The string-lock tip moves its corner to the new button; the one-line shrinking in `PickerRow.kt` goes. Every style, English and Dutch, TalkBack and 2× font; phone check
- ☑ Custom tunings stored in DataStore (name, instrument, one note per string); damaged data falls back to the presets (`CustomTuning`, add/update/delete with lasting ids; groundwork, nothing visible yet)
- ☑ Tuning editor: pick each string's note with ± semitone buttons, name it, save; checks for empty names and duplicates (notes B0–E6; opened from "New tuning" at the end of the panel's list; saving picks the tuning; English and Dutch, TalkBack labels and a 2× font test already in)
- ☑ Custom tunings in the picker panel below the presets, with "New tuning", edit and delete (under "My tunings", each with a ⋮ menu; Delete asks first in a dialog)
- ☑ English and Dutch texts, TalkBack and 2× font for the editor; phone check (editor, panel, menu and delete dialog on the Xiaomi 12X in English and Dutch at 2× text and with TalkBack; `MyTuningsStylesTest` in all six styles)

## Phase 17 — Reference tone
Goal: the app can play the target note so players can tune by ear.
- ☑ Tone player in `:core:audio` (`AudioTrack`): a short plucked tone at the target pitch that follows A4 calibration and stops by itself after a few seconds (4 s; every preset string within 1 cent by YIN at A4 415–466 Hz)
- ☑ Long-press a string (or a play button) to hear it; tap again to stop (a tap only stops, it doesn't lock; sounding rings in each style; TalkBack action "Play A 2")
- ☑ The tuner ignores the microphone while a tone plays, so it doesn't measure its own sound (mic stays open, readings set aside while playing + 0.3 s)
- ☑ Volume follows the phone's media volume; TalkBack labels; phone check by ear (volume keys set the media volume on the tuner; transient audio focus pauses other music; rings checked in all six styles, light and dark; listening is an owner check)

## Phase 18 — Strum all strings
Goal: strum once and see which strings are out of tune.
- ☑ Research spike: polyphonic detection of 6 strings at once in `:core:dsp` (spectrum peaks per expected string, harmonics that overlap); write down the accuracy that is reachable — `StrumDetector`: with a 341 ms frame ≤ 0.12 cent on strings with a clear partial, ≤ 2.3 on B3/E4 (under E2's harmonics); 1.9 ms per reading on the Xiaomi (ARCHITECTURE.md, *Strum check*)
- ☑ Detector with synthetic chord tests (all six strings, one or two strings off by 10–30 cents, a missing string) — joint fit of overlapping partials: an octave pair detuned against each other 7.3 → 0.44 cent; `StrumListener` gives the first reading 0.39–0.44 s after a strum, then one every 0.1 s; `StrumVerdict` flat / in tune / sharp / not heard; 2.6 ms per reading on the Xiaomi. Known limits: a soft upper octave string within 35 cents of the lower one reads up to 19 cents off, a missing D4 in DADGAD/open D reads as heard (ARCHITECTURE.md, *Strum check*)
- ☑ "Strum check" view: every string shows flat, in tune or sharp at once, in each style — a switch beside the instrument-and-tuning button; the panel takes the meter's place, less sure strings hollow; a synthetic strum from a laptop speaker read A2 −21 / G3 +15 on the Xiaomi (ARCHITECTURE.md, *Strum check view*)
- ☑ phone check with recorded strums from short test tones; owner check with a real guitar (now under *Owner checks*) — `scripts/strum-check.sh` plays synthetic strums from the computer's speaker to the Xiaomi: every string within 1 cent of what was played, missing strings caught. It found that the last reading, as the strings die away, was up to 7 cents off and that clicks and room sounds were read as strums; each string now shows the strum's readings together, weighted by loudness (ARCHITECTURE.md, *Strum check*, *Phone check*)

## Phase 19 — Metronome
Goal: a simple, steady metronome in the same app.
- ☑ Click generator with sample-exact timing (`AudioTrack`), 30–250 BPM, accent on the first beat, 2/4 to 7/8 (`ClickTrack` + `AndroidMetronomePlayer`; no drift over ten simulated minutes, beats 250 ms apart within ±0.02 ms on the Xiaomi 12X)
- ☑ Metronome screen: tempo dial, ± buttons, tap tempo, beat lights in each style; optional vibration on the beat. Mock-up: [docs/design/1.x/metronome-nav-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/metronome-nav-preview.html). **Owner decided (2026-10-14):** bar variant **B · Frameless** (for the bar, below); keep the round tempo dial; the rest of the metronome as shown: meter picker 2/4 3/4 4/4 5/4 6/8 7/8 at the top, beat lights in the style's own wave marks (beat 1 largest, group starts middle sized), a frameless dial 30–250 BPM over 300° with the meter's scale rules and the style's arc and needle, the tempo with its Italian name, − / + by 1 BPM, Tap tempo (average of the last taps, reset after 2 s), Start / Stop, "Vibrate on the beat"; lit marks in the accent, never in-tune green; one soft accent glow behind the tempo on each beat; the quiet background; opens stopped. Until the bar lands it opens from a small metronome button beside the tuner's gear (temporary); Back returns to the tuner
- ☑ Stops when the app leaves the screen (no foreground service); tempo and meter saved in settings (and the vibration switch; the screen stays on while it plays)
- ☑ Timing drift measured over 10 minutes; English and Dutch texts; phone check (`scripts/metronome-drift.sh` on the Xiaomi 12X, 120 BPM for 10 min: every beat on its exact sample, speaker clock 0.0 ppm from the system clock, at most 0.02 ms off a perfect wall-clock grid, beat reports within ±0.3 ms, told 1–6 ms after the sound, no underruns; meter buttons heard by name, Dutch "slagen per minuut"; tuner, metronome and settings through the bar on the phone)
- ☑ Bottom navigation bar with Tuner · Metronome · Settings (Material 3 `NavigationBar`), replacing the gear button; a navigation rail along the edge on a phone on its side, hidden in the compact layout; in each style, without a frame that fights the frameless meter. Mock-up first; English and Dutch, TalkBack and 2× font; phone check (variant B: icons and names on a calm field, the open tab in the accent with the style's mark; tabs heard as tabs; the gear and the temporary metronome button are gone, Settings lost its back arrow; checked on the Xiaomi 12X upright and on its side)

## Phase 20 — Wear OS companion and home-screen widget
Goal: Twangle on the watch and one tap from the home screen.
- ☑ Home-screen widget: shows the chosen tuning and opens the tuner with one tap (the widget never holds the microphone) — plain RemoteViews, in the style's colours, redrawn by the app when the tuning or look changes
- ☑ Wear OS module: a watch tuner that listens with the watch's own mic and reuses `:core:dsp` — standalone `:wear` app with the phone's app ID, `:core:audio`'s recorder, a simple note-and-cents screen; starts and listens on the Wear OS 6 emulator
- ☑ Watch UI: note, cents and an in-tune ring sized for round screens; mic released when the watch screen turns off — a ring along the edge with the cents scale at the top, the note large in the middle, a soft in-tune glow that eases in and out (the phone's `InTuneDetector`, now in `:core:dsp`), the phone's Contemporary colours; screen off or ambient lets go of the mic within a second
- ☑ Check on a Wear OS emulator; real watch test by the owner — emulator check done (6 Compose tests, mic released on sleep and back on wake); the PC's mic didn't reach the emulator, so a live note waits for a real watch, now in *Owner checks*

## Phase 21 — Native pitch detector (only if needed)
Goal: a C++ (NDK/Oboe) detector, built only if profiling shows the Kotlin one is too slow (for example for strum check or Wear OS).
- ☑ Decide with measurements from Phases 15, 18 and 20 whether it is needed; if not, close this phase — not needed (2026-10-15). `DspPhoneBenchmark` now also times the watch's chain; on the Xiaomi 12X's little cores (a stand-in for a watch CPU) it takes 2.7 ms per 21 ms hop at 16 kHz (13 % of a core) and 2.2 ms at 48 kHz (10.5 %); on a big core 0.46 / 0.33 ms. The phone's low-note window takes 0.75 ms (3.5 % of a big core) and strum check 2.6 / 5.2 ms every 0.1 s (ARCHITECTURE.md §4, *Watch CPU and the native detector*)
- ☑ Native YIN behind the `PitchDetector` interface, with the same ±1 cent tests as the Kotlin version — not needed, see the decision above
- ☑ CPU and battery compared with the Kotlin detector on the test phone — not needed, see the decision above

## Phase 24 — Show me around
Goal: a short tour on first start that points out the main parts of the tuner, one at a time, with text balloons that float on their own layer above the screen.
**Owner decided (2026-10-14):** the tour balloons live in a separate overlay layer above the whole UI instead of being squeezed into the layout, since the tour runs once at first start; under the dim layer they may cover anything.
- ☑ Preview for the owner before anything is built: mock-up of the tour in `docs/design/1.x/tour-preview.html` (each step on a tuner screen: the screen dimmed, one part lit up, a short balloon with Next / Skip; a few styles, light and dark, Dutch at 2× text, a 360 dp phone). **Owner decided (2026-10-14), round 2:** balloons without an outline, lifted by a soft shadow (Futuristic: a glow), carrying the style's mark, filled with the style's **accent**; texts as short as possible: "How far off your note is", "Lock a string", "Pick instrument and tuning", "Tune strings or any note", "Metronome and settings" (Dutch in the mock-up). The mock-up's top comment is the design the build follows
- ☑ The tour: on first start the screen dims and one part at a time is lit up (spotlight cut-out) — the note with needle/wave, the strings (tap one to lock it), the instrument-and-tuning button, the guitar/chromatic mode switch, the bottom bar (metronome, settings) — each with a short balloon and Next / Skip tour (last step: Done); shown once (a saved setting), "Show me around" in Settings starts it again; never a nuisance for experienced players (owner, 2026-10-14): one tap on "Skip tour" or Back ends the whole tour for good, and updates of an existing install never show it; balloon wholly shown at 2× font, in Dutch, on a 360 dp phone, on a phone on its side and in the two-pane layout, every style (the in-layout string-lock tip misses its corner in Dutch, Futuristic and Space Age on the Xiaomi 12X, and in Dutch in every style on a 360 dp phone; the overlay balloon must not); TalkBack; English and Dutch; phone check (done 2026-10-14: an overlay over the whole window, `TourHost` in `:feature:tuner` `tour/`; on the rail of a phone on its side the balloon goes beside it; `TourStylesTest`, `TourFlowTest`, `TourGeometryTest`; the in-layout tip stays, hidden while the tour is due or has run)
- ☑ The one-time string-lock tip becomes the tour's strings step: the in-layout balloon (`StringLockTip`, its corner/above-the-button fallback and `stringLockTipSeen`) goes; `StringLockTipStylesTest`, which checks that fallback, is replaced by the tour's tests (the landscape controls pane keeps its 56 dp clearance, which also keeps a margin when it scrolls; the old `string_lock_tip_seen` key stays in existing installs and still marks them as updates)
- ☑ Follow-up (owner, 2026-10-15: the balloons jumped around the screen): the tour goes in the reading order of the layout shown, rows top to bottom and left to right within a row, the bar or rail always last (upright: mode switch, meter, note, instrument button, strum check, strings, bar; on its side and in two panes: meter, note, then mode switch, instrument button, strum check, strings, then rail or bar); the meter and the note are separate steps ("How far off you are", "The note you play") and the strum check switch has its own ("Strum to check every string"); chromatic mode has 4 steps; each pointer is centred on its own element with its tip 2 dp from it; the bar step lights only Metronome and Settings and points at the pair's middle; a balloon goes to the other side when its preferred side would cover another control and the other side is clear; the highlight is stronger (owner, 2026-10-15: hardly visible in dark and light): a darker dim (dark: background 50 % black at 80 %; light: background 60 % ink at 80 %) and a soft, steady accent halo round the spotlight's edge (28 % dark, 20 % light, fading over 12 dp) (`tourOrder`, `placeBalloon`, `TourGeometryTest`, `TourStylesTest`, phone check)
- ☑ A reception tip that is set smaller to fit ("Too loud, move the phone back" at 1.25× text) no longer moves the note by 1–2 px; `TunerContentTest.aShrunkTipMovesNothing…` (now `@Ignore`d) switched back on (cause: with Android 14's non-linear font scaling Compose keeps an sp line height in proportion to the font size, and a smaller font is enlarged by more, so the shrunk line came out 1 px taller; now the tip's line keeps the measured height of a full-size line, the text centred in it. Both tests run again and also check the tip really was set smaller and is wholly shown)

## Phase 22 — More languages
Goal: Twangle speaks the world's major languages before it goes public.
- ☑ `LocalizationTest` checks every supported language, not only Dutch; the in-app language list (Android 13+) shows them all (one list, `twangle.languages` in gradle.properties, feeds both apps' `localeFilters` and the test)
- ☑ Western European: German, French, Spanish, Italian, Portuguese (Brazil) (phone and watch apps, all texts; UI tests in these languages still to run, in the fit-check item below)
- ☑ Asian: Chinese (Simplified), Japanese, Korean, Hindi, Indonesian (phone and watch apps, all texts; CJK and Devanagari from the phone's own fonts; UI tests in these languages still to run, in the fit-check item below)
- ☑ Russian, Turkish and Polish (phone and watch apps, all texts; Cyrillic falls back to the phone font in four styles, and Ancient and Futuristic mix faces in Turkish and Polish titles, see the fit-check item below)
- ☑ Arabic, with the screens mirrored right-to-left (string row, settings, wave and dial checked in RTL): texts, settings and panels mirror; meter, wave, strum check, string row, tempo dial, beat lights and the A4 slider stay low-left/high-right; Arabic-Indic digits everywhere except octaves in note names (phone checked in Arabic; UI tests in Arabic still to run, in the fit-check item below)
- ☑ Note names that match local habit: German, Polish and Russian use H for B, and French, Italian, Spanish, Portuguese, Turkish and Arabic start on Do-Re-Mi (only the default; the notation setting still lets players choose). Settings has *Automatic* (follows the language) plus a German option; German TalkBack says Cis/Des/H; the watch follows its own language (phone checked in German and French; watch not checked on a device)
- ☑ In-app language picker in Settings: Phone's language first, then every language in twangle.languages in its own name; works on Android 8–12 too (AppCompat per-app language, the same setting as phone Settings on Android 13+; phone checked: Polish and back, texts and H for B follow, kept after a restart; its UI tests in `SettingsContentTest` run in the overnight run; not checked on an Android 8–12 phone)
- ☑ Long words and other scripts fit at 2× font in every style (UI tests); phone check in a few languages. **Runs overnight, started by the owner** (see *Waiting on the owner*); also fix the Turkish/Polish letters missing from Ancient's and Futuristic's title fonts (words mix two fonts) and check Futuristic's and Space Age's letter spacing on Chinese and Japanese (new `@EveryLanguage` checks: tuner, Settings, About, metronome, tour and bar in all 16 languages at 2× text, upright and on its side, every style, all pass after fixes; Polish and Turkish titles in Ancient and Futuristic now in Alegreya Sans and Michroma, no mixed faces, guarded by `StyleFontCoverageTest`; no extra letter spacing in Chinese, Japanese and other scripts the faces lack; phone: Japanese, Polish and Turkish in Space Age on its side)

## Phase 25 — Meter looks, round 2
Goal: every meter look shows the note its own way, so they no longer look alike (approved mock-up: [docs/design/1.x/meter-looks-round2-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/meter-looks-round2-preview.html), owner 2026-10-15). Six looks: Classic, Light arc, Spirit level, Eclipse, Arrows, Light bars. UI tests run in the overnight run (see *Waiting on the owner*).
- ☑ Classic gets the "CENTS" label from Studio meter; Rolling scale, Comet and Studio meter go (a saved one opens as Classic)
- ☑ Light arc becomes a fan of little lights that opens toward the note, and Spirit level a big straight vial with the bubble at its middle height; neither has numbers any more
- ☑ New looks Eclipse, Arrows and Light bars, with their names in every language (the arrows' shimmer from the mock-up left out, so a steady note draws nothing new)
- ☑ The on-device UI tests of the six looks pass (`MeterLooksTest` and the settings and tuner layout tests). **Runs overnight, started by the owner** (see *Waiting on the owner*) (2026-10-16, every style; Eclipse's soft halo past its box allowed in `CompactLandscapeStylesTest`, as `MeterLooksTest` already did)

## Phase 26 — Pre-launch fixes
Goal: fix what the full pre-launch review (2026-10-15: code, UX and wording, release readiness, and a walkthrough on the phone) found, so v1.0 ships without known bugs or loose ends. Items that depended on the owner's pre-launch decisions say what was decided (see *Waiting on the owner*). Visible changes get a quick phone screenshot; their UI tests join the overnight run.
- ☑ Release basics: the watch's release build is signed with the same upload key as the phone (it comes out unsigned today, and Play rejects that); version 1.0.0 (phone versionCode 10000, watch 1_010_000), set in one place for both apps; CI also builds the release (`assembleRelease bundleRelease`, unsigned there) so R8 or signing breakage shows up (`twangle.version` in gradle.properties; both release APKs and bundles verified signed with the upload key)
- ☑ Licences: About credits Okio (Apache 2.0), Protocol Buffers (BSD 3-Clause, which needs its licence text in `assets/licenses`), JSpecify and Guava's listenablefuture, which ship inside the app via DataStore and AndroidX; the licence dialog no longer shows build details (script names, file paths) (credits checked against the release dependency tree; the Noto Sans Symbols note no longer names a script or file; JVM tests guard both; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Remove the temporary "When in tune: breathe gently / hold perfectly still" setting, keeping the owner's pick (**decision 1: hold perfectly still**), with its saved key, strings and tests (the wave always holds still on the note; an old saved `wave_breathes_in_tune` is ignored; JVM tests for both; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Metronome: the tempo dial only reacts to touches on its ring, not its middle (tapping "BPM" now jumps to 30 or 250; JVM tests for `bpmAtTouch` and `dialDegrees`); it keeps playing when the phone turns or dark mode switches (it stops on any configuration change today); it stops when headphones are unplugged; audio focus as decided (**decision 4: click over other music, no longer pause it**) (no audio focus any more; a call, or the phone ringing, still stops it, read from the phone's audio mode; `TempoDialTest` and `CallModeTest` on the JVM; phone: plays on through a rotation; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Microphone robustness: notice when the mic is silenced mid-session (a call, another app, the privacy switch) and show the "microphone busy" message instead of "Play a note" forever; a run of empty reads or writes fails instead of spinning; opening the mic tries the next sample rate if the first fails; JVM tests with a fake recorder for these paths (Android 10+ reports silencing through an `AudioRecordingCallback`; 2 s of exact zeros, a muted or blocked mic, also counts as busy; 50 empty reads or writes in a row fail; `ChunkReaderTest`, `MicOpenerTest`, `BlockingTransferTest` and the pipelines' tests on the JVM; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Microphone permission: dismissing the first prompt keeps "Allow microphone" (Settings only after a real "don't ask again"); losing the permission while the app is open falls back to the prompt; the Settings path text matches Android 11+; the Tuning item opens or is disabled while the mic is denied (today it does nothing) (a refusal is remembered in the settings, so a dismissed dialog keeps "Allow microphone" and only a real "don't ask again" leads to Settings; the Settings text says "allow it while using the app" from Android 11, "turn on Microphone" before; the Tuning item opens the panel over the prompt; JVM tests for the rules, the saved flag and the panel's tuning; checked on the phone: dismiss, refuse twice, restart, Tuning without the mic, permission taken back; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Light themes: no white strip behind the gesture bar at the bottom (MIUI draws the navigation-bar scrim; make it transparent with gesture navigation) (no scrim with gesture navigation, read from the phone's navigation mode since MIUI counts its gesture bar as tappable; navigation buttons keep the system's scrim; JVM tests for the rule; phone: tuner, metronome and settings in light and dark with gestures, and settings with buttons in both; no UI test covers the system bars)
- ☑ Tuner clarity: show the current tuning's name (e.g. "Guitar · Drop D"); resting states no longer look in tune (strum-check markers green on the centre line, Classic's green centre, Eclipse's full disc); a small check mark when in tune with the wave display too, so colour-blind players see it; the tuning panel opens scrolled to the selected tuning (quiet "Guitar · Open E" above the strings, a tap opens the panel; every meter look's centre muted without a note, Eclipse's moon gone and sun grey at rest, strum check's centre marks and band neutral until heard or in tune; the check mark under the wave; `RestingCentreTest` on the JVM, new `TunerContentTest` cases; phone: Classic and Eclipse at rest, the wave's check with a test tone, strum check at rest, the panel reopened at Open E, upright and on its side; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Discoverable reference tone: the tour's strings step says "Tap to lock, hold to hear" (NL "Kies om vast te zetten, houd vast om te horen"); "Playing D3" shows while it sounds; the strum-check button gets a label or tooltip ("Playing E3" in the hint line while a tone sounds, in the player's note names and every language, TalkBack "Playing E 3", over a tip; a long-press on the strum check switch shows a "Strum check" tooltip, no label, so the row keeps its room for the tuning's name; phone: both upright, a long-press still doesn't switch strum check; new `TunerContentTest` cases; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Settings tidy-up: clearer Display options ("Below the note: numbers / wave" instead of "Needle"), the in-tune effect explained in one line, the "Tuning" group renamed "Pitch and notes" so it doesn't clash with the bar's Tuning item; a less crowded Look section; wallpaper colours on or off for a fresh install (**decision 2: stay on by default**); the language list fits on screen ("Bahasa Indonesia"; Language row gets a chevron like its neighbours); "442 Hz" never breaks across lines; the style, meter and wave preview cards honour "Remove animations" (Look split into "Look" (style, light or dark, wallpaper colours) and "Tuner screen" (meter, in-tune effect "How strongly the meter glows when a note is in tune", "Below the note: Numbers: how many cents off / Wave", wave look, layout on its side); wallpaper colours stay on by default, unchanged; the language dialog takes at most 60 % of the screen, scrolls with a line at the edge that has more and opens at the picked language; no-break space between number and Hz in all 16 languages (`HertzStringsTest`); the look cards hold still with "Remove animations" (`rememberAnimationsOff` moved to `:core:designsystem`, `HoldStillTest`); phone: Settings scrolled through, the language list, the cards still with animations removed; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Wording and small UX: Dutch strum texts say "Kies een snaar" (not "Tik op een snaar"), "Rondleiding" for the tour, "Effect bij zuiver", "Tempo tikken"; German "Effekt, wenn gestimmt"; About's privacy line mentions the phone's own backup; "on this device" instead of "on this phone"; TalkBack separators come from string resources (Arabic comma); the tour's last step drops "Skip tour"; the compact landscape layout hints that a tap shows the controls; the signal-bars button gets a 48 dp touch area (privacy line in all 16 languages: settings stay on this device, and in Android's own backup if it is on, as `allowBackup` allows; the microphone texts say device too; `tuner_spoken_separator` ("، " in Arabic, "、" Japanese, "，" Chinese); "Tap to show the controls" at the bottom for 4 s after the compact layout appears, then it fades, hidden from TalkBack; the bars' button 48 × 48 dp around their 24 dp slot, nothing moved; phone: the compact hint and its fade, About in Dutch; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Small robustness: strum check keeps working after 12+ hours open (counter overflow); unison strings in a custom tuning (or the editor refuses duplicate notes); one settings read error no longer freezes settings at the defaults for the session; the tuning editor's save can't be cut off halfway by a rotation; the editor asks before throwing away unsaved edits (the strum timer counts in a Long, a strum still noticed 13 hours on; unison strings are supported: strum check reads a pair as one course and both strings show its reading, where before they only came out heard because the refine passes are even; a failed settings read shows the defaults only if nothing was shown yet and is tried again after 0.5 s, doubling up to 30 s; Save adds and picks the tuning in one write and runs in a ViewModel, so a rotation neither cuts it off nor keeps the editor open; "Discard changes?" (Discard / Keep editing) on close, Back or a widget tap when the draft has edits, in all 16 languages, NL "Wijzigingen weggooien?" (Weggooien / Verder bewerken); also the watch's mic prompt, found by the mic-permission item: a dismissed first dialog now keeps "Allow microphone" and only a real "don't ask again" leads to settings, following the phone's `MicPermissionRules`, now in `:core:audio`; and, found on the phone, every screen's saved state now survives turning it: the navigation frame put the screen at a different place in the composition for bar and rail, so turning lost the editor's draft (and brought back the one from the last time on its side); JVM tests for each; phone: the discard question, and the draft and question through four turns; watch not checked on a device; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Cleanup: remove dead code (`NoteMapper` helpers only tests use, stale comments, orphaned doc comments); R8 keep rules also cover `MeterLook`, `InTuneEffect` and `Meter`; prune the four stale worktrees under `.claude/worktrees`; README brought up to date (status, metronome, strum check, custom tunings, reference tone, widget, watch, 16 languages), as the base for the store text (`NoteMapper` keeps `midiOf` and `frequencyOf`; `map`, `nearestNote`, `centsOff`, `centsBetween` and `NoteReading` moved to the DSP tests; three orphaned doc comments put back on their functions or dropped; the meter-frames benchmark finds the renamed "Numbers" display again; the four worktrees held only merged work and are gone; release build checked with R8)

## Phase 27 — Tempo from music
Goal: the metronome can listen to music playing nearby (from a speaker, a band, or the phone itself) and set its tempo to match, so a player can click along with a song or backing track. Added by the owner on 2026-10-15, before v1.0. Twangle can't hear other apps' audio inside the phone (Android allows that only with a screen-recording prompt, and most music apps block it), so it listens through the microphone, like the tuner. Audio stays in memory only, as everywhere in Twangle.
- ☑ Design first: a mock-up of the metronome screen with a "Listen" button beside Tap tempo, what it shows while listening and when it has found a tempo, and how the player accepts it. Owner approves before building (approved mock-up [docs/design/1.x/metronome-listen-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/metronome-listen-preview.html), its top comment is the spec; **Owner decided (2026-10-15)** after five rounds: Listen beside Tap tempo as two equal fixed-size buttons, − / + in the dial's bottom corners; a found tempo is suggested in a balloon (½× / 2×, Use, Not now) and nothing changes until Use, a stopped metronome stays stopped; while listening a subtle trace runs round the Listen button's edge and a small ring on the dial marks the tempo it is settling on; a new press effect on every button, lit on touch-down and fading over ¼ s, with a crisp flash for Tap tempo; clicks in step with the song not in scope)
- ☑ Tempo detector in `:core:dsp` (pure, allocation-free): an onset-strength envelope from the microphone frames and a tempo estimate from its periodicity (e.g. autocorrelation as in Scheirer 1998 / Ellis 2007), whole BPM within 30–250, steady enough to show without jumping, and a sensible pick between half and double tempo; synthetic tests (click tracks and drum-like patterns at known tempos, with noise) within ±1 BPM (`OnsetTempoDetector`: click tracks 30–250, drum grooves 70–200, swing, loose timing, a bass line, 8–96 kHz all within ±1 BPM, most within 0.1, after 14 s; first tempo by 3–5 s, steady by 7–10 s; noise and silence never show one; also tells the beat moments, within 6 ms, and the half/double choice; 0.1 ms per mic chunk on the JVM)
- ☑ Listen on the metronome screen: asks for the microphone the same way the tuner does, holds it only while listening (stops on its own once the tempo is steady, after about 15 s, or when the screen is left), pauses the metronome's own clicks while listening, and sets the tempo as the mock-up says; with the mock-up's new layout (− / + at the dial's bottom corners, Tap tempo and Listen as two equal fixed-size buttons) and its new press effect on every metronome button, built as one shared Modifier in `:core:designsystem`, with Tap tempo's crisp flash; translated in every language; JVM tests with a fake recorder, UI tests in the overnight run (built 2026-10-15: `MetronomeViewModel` listens through its own `AudioRecorder` with `OnsetTempoDetector` on `Dispatchers.Default`, the clicks paused; the shared press effect is `Modifier.pressFeedback` in `:core:designsystem`; 15 new strings in all 16 languages; `MetronomeListenTest` (12 JVM cases with a fake microphone and the real detector) and `PressFeedbackTest`; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ The same press effect on every other button of the app (tuner, tuning panel and editor, settings, About, tour balloons, dialogs), replacing Material's ripple; each style, light and dark; UI tests in the overnight run (owner asked for this, 2026-10-15) (built 2026-10-15: `TwangleTheme` turns Material's ripple off and gives every row and plain clickable the press light (`PressIndication`, tint only, no sink); Material's buttons became `TwangleTextButton`, `TwangleButton`, `TwangleIconButton` and `TwangleFilledTonalIconButton`, same look and 48 dp touch area, with `pressFeedback`; the string buttons, instrument chips, strum switch, tuning name and look cards also sink (`pressSink`); the navigation items light without a sink; watch and widget unchanged; `PressFeedbackTest` on the JVM, the new `PressEffectTest`; phone: held presses in two styles, light and dark; UI tests deferred to the overnight run)
- ☑ Phone check with real music from a speaker at a few tempos; the owner's live check is under *Owner checks* (2026-10-15 on the Xiaomi 12X, drum grooves of 14 s from the laptop speaker: 120 BPM found as 120, 160 as 160, a swung 100 as 100, 80 as 160 with eighth-note hi-hats (½× gives 80); a quiet room gave "No steady beat heard"; Use, Not now and the clicks coming back checked in Futuristic dark, Contemporary light and Ancient dark)

---

## Music tools, before v1.0
**Owner decided (2026-10-16):** there is no hurry to release, so the music tools (Phases 29–33) are finished first and go into **v1.0**, not a later version 1.1 as planned earlier that day. Each tool stayed hidden in the Play build behind its own switch (Phase 28) until it was finished and had passed the owner's check; then it went live (Phase 34, 2026-10-17), with the privacy policy and the store-listing plan updated to match. Phase 35 (the "more tools" button) and Phase 36 (focus mode) were done alongside.

Rules for every phase here: no internet, no account, audio never saved, every text translated, TalkBack and 2× text from the start, nothing added to the tuner screen's clutter. A rough mock-up for the owner comes before each new screen or mode. Each phase's live test goes under *Owner checks* when it lands.
