**Where they go in the app** (owner agreed, 2026-10-16): the tools that listen stay modes inside the tuner, under the "more tools" button; the Metronome tab becomes **Practice**, holding the Metronome, Chords and the Capo helper; the bar keeps four items. The reasons are in [docs/architecture/ui.md](docs/architecture/ui.md) (*Navigation bar*, *Practice*).

---

## Phase 28 — Hidden until 1.1
Goal: new tools can land on `main` without changing what v1.0 users see.
- ☑ One switch per after-v1.0 tool (intonation check, chord naming, pitch trail, Practice tab with chords, capo helper), on in debug builds and off in release builds, decided in one place in `:app` and passed down (through `AppContainer`), so a hidden tool leaves no trace in a release build: no mode, tab, setting, tour step or text on screen
- ☑ Tests: a release-variant unit test checks every switch is off there; a debug test checks they are on; a UI test can turn one off to see the v1.0 screen
- ☑ Phase 23's store screenshots and the Baseline Profile see the v1.0 app (switches off), whatever the debug build shows: `ToolSwitchesOffRule` for the screenshot test; the profile's builds are non-debug, which `ReleaseToolSwitchesTest` now checks in them too
- ☑ docs/architecture/performance.md (*Release build*) describes the switches and how a tool goes live: its switch turns on for release (or goes), in Phase 34

## Phase 29 — Intonation check
Goal: tells whether a guitar plays in tune up the neck. Play the open string, then the same string at the 12th fret; Twangle compares the two and says to move that string's saddle back (12th fret sharp) or forward (flat), or that it's fine. One string at a time, with the existing pitch detector.
- ☑ `:core:dsp`: the comparison (the 12th fret should sound exactly one octave, 1200 cents, above the open string) with a tolerance of a few cents worked out in the phase, and a verdict per string; synthetic tests for sharp, flat, fine and a wrong note at the fret (`intonation/Intonation`: 3-cent tolerance, about 1 mm of saddle; over 50 cents is not the octave; every verdict right on every instrument's strings, offset within 0.38 cents)
- ☑ Rough mock-up of the mode (choosing a string, the two steps, the verdict) for the owner to pick from (`docs/design/1.x/intonation-preview.html`; owner picked option A on 2026-10-16: a switch like the strum check, with the small icon switches gathered behind one "more options" (^) button so the tuner doesn't fill up with icons as tools are added; millimetres shown only where their accuracy can be guaranteed)
- ☑ The mode inside the tuner, next to strings, chromatic and strum check, behind its switch (Phase 28); every text translated, TalkBack and 2× text; JVM and UI tests (UI tests overnight); phone check with synthetic tones (a "more tools" (^) button gathers the strum check and the 12th-fret check where the build has more than one tool, v1.0's strum switch otherwise; millimetres on guitars only, to 0.5 mm; synthetic plucks through a fake microphone on the Xiaomi: A3 8 cents sharp says "move the saddle back, about 3 mm"; the laptop speaker didn't reach the phone that day; every style and every language run overnight)
- ☑ Owner check added: with a real guitar, check each string's verdict matches what a guitar shop would say

---

## Phase 30 — What chord is this?
Goal: strum a chord and Twangle names it (e.g. "A minor"), building on the strum check's six-strings-at-once detection, pointed at chords instead of tuning.
- ☑ `:core:dsp`: chord naming from a strum, the common chords first (major, minor, 7th, major 7th, minor 7th, sus2, sus4), with synthetic chord tests like the strum check's, open and barre shapes, and no name when the strum isn't a clear chord (`ChordDetector`: 41 shapes, no wrong name in 1,640 synthetic strums, no name for 483 of 484 sounds that are no chord)
- ☑ Rough mock-up of the mode for the owner to pick from (`docs/design/1.x/chord-mode-preview.html`; owner picked B on 2026-10-16: the ^ menu's "Chord name", big symbol with words, the chord's notes with the bass filled, the last three chords; design in docs/architecture/ui-tuner.md, *Chord name view*)
- ☑ The mode inside the tuner behind its switch (Phase 28): the chord's name in the player's note names, translated, TalkBack and 2× text; JVM and UI tests; phone check with synthetic chords ("Chord name" in the ^ menu for guitars, ukulele and mandolin; settled by `ChordSettler`: 4 of 6 readings, 1.5 s hold, faded, "Not a clear chord"; synthetic Am and C/E named on the Xiaomi through the fake microphone)
- ☑ Owner check added: strum a dozen real chords and check the names

- ☑ Rough mock-up for the owner to pick from (docs/design/1.x/more-tools-preview.html): owner picked (2026-10-16, round 2) the hammer and screwdriver icon (Material Symbols `handyman`), icon only, no word
- ☑ The `handyman` icon in place of ^, same 44 dp button, in every style, light and dark, upright and on its side, 2× text, TalkBack (still "More tools"); the tour's step points at it; UI tests; phone check (24 dp glyph in the 44 dp button; off and on, light and dark, and the menu checked on the Xiaomi)
- ☑ docs/architecture/ui-tuner.md (*More tools*) updated

## Phase 32 — Practice tab with chord and scale charts
Goal: finger diagrams for the chosen instrument and tuning, own tunings included (a DADGAD player sees DADGAD shapes), and scales on a fretboard; no microphone needed. The Metronome tab becomes Practice.
- ☑ Rough mock-up of the Practice tab, a chord chart and a scale on the fretboard, for the owner to pick from (`docs/design/1.x/practice-preview.html`; owner picked A, D, G, I on 2026-10-16: a segmented switch like the tuner's, an upright chord box with fingers in the dots, one shape at a time, the whole neck with note names; design in docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md, *Practice tab*)
- ☑ The Metronome tab becomes Practice behind its switch (Phase 28): the bar reads Tuner · Instrument · Practice · Settings, a small switch at the top picks the tool, and it opens on the tool used last (saved); with the switch off it stays the Metronome tab as now (`:feature:practice`; Metronome · Chords, the Chords side a placeholder until the next item)
- ☑ `:core:dsp`: playable chord shapes for any instrument and tuning (within a comfortable fret span, open strings where they fit), and scales (major, minor, pentatonic, blues) on any tuning; pure and tested (`ChordShapeFinder.shapesOf`, `ScaleFinder.notesOf`; the first shape is the chord books' first for every common chord in standard tuning, ukulele and mandolin; every chord on every preset checked)
- ☑ Chords: pick a root and a chord type, see one or more finger diagrams; TalkBack reads a shape in words (string by string, fret and finger) (`ChordsScreen`: twelve roots and seven types in the player's note names, the upright chart with fingers in the dots, ‹ 1 of 6 › and a swipe, following the Instrument item's tuning, own tunings included; root and type saved; translated into all 16 languages, fits at 2× text and on its side; scales come as a Chords · Scales choice inside the tool)
- ☑ Scales: pick a root and a scale, see it on a fretboard; TalkBack reads it in words (a Chords · Scales choice inside the Chords tool, the root shared; five scales; the whole neck 0–12 sideways as the mock-up, every note named, the root in the accent, upright only where that is clearly bigger such as a tablet; the instrument's tuning, own tunings included; choice and scale saved; read string by string; translated into all 16 languages, fits at 2× text and on its side)
- ☑ Every text translated, 2× text, every style light and dark; JVM and UI tests; phone check (2026-10-17: every Practice, Chords and Scales text checked in all 16 languages, chord types, scale names and TalkBack phrases included; the real app on the Xiaomi in all six styles, light and dark, upright and on its side, Chords and Scales; two-line scale names kept 3 dp clear of their outline, which Classic's and Retro's letters touched; at 2× text on its side the scales' start half scrolls by about half a button row, accepted and explained in docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md; overnight run 2026-10-17: Dutch scale names broke inside a word at 2× text, now broken at the hyphen, and Classic's La♭maj7 was cut off on a 320 dp phone, now set a little smaller)
- ☑ Owner check added: look up chords and scales in standard tuning and in a tuning of your own, and play them

## Phase 31 — Pitch trail
Goal: a line that draws the note over the last few seconds, so a held note, a bend or vibrato shows how steady it is; also for singers and violinists.
- ☑ Preallocated history of readings (no allocation per frame), drawn in the draw phase only, like the strobe (`PitchHistory`, 5 s by default, fed by `TunerViewModel` on the DSP thread; the drawing comes with the mode)
- ☑ Rough mock-up of the trail in two or three styles for the owner to pick from (owner, 2026-10-17: option A of [docs/design/1.x/pitch-trail-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/pitch-trail-preview.html), a band in the meter's place, the line scrolling right to left over 4 s, sharp up, flat down)
- ☑ The mode inside the tuner behind its switch (Phase 28), in every style, light and dark; TalkBack describes it in words; JVM and UI tests; frame times measured on the phone, like the meter looks (`scripts/meter-frames.sh`) (2026-10-17: "Pitch trail" / "Toonlijn" in the more-tools menu, for every instrument, staying on in chromatic mode; the line in the meter's place in each style's colours; TalkBack says "steady, in tune", "wavering, 10 cents sharp", "bending up"; on the Xiaomi 120 frames a second with no late frame and less main-thread work than the Classic needle in the same run, `scripts/meter-frames.sh PITCH_TRAIL`; phone check with the synthetic sweep in the real app and the real microphone in the debug build; the other styles in the overnight run)
- ☑ Owner check added: hold a note, bend a string and play vibrato, and check the line shows it clearly

## Phase 36 — Focus mode
Goal: the full layout by default, upright and on its side, and one big meter as a choice the player makes. **Owner decided (2026-10-17):** the setting "Full layout on its side" turns around into **Focus mode**, off by default. Today a phone turned on its side goes compact on its own, which surprises anyone who turned it by accident, and people with auto-rotate off never see the big meter at all. Not a music tool, so no tool switch: it ships in v1.0. No mock-up: the owner found the plan clear enough.
- ☑ Focus mode on: the whole app stays on its side (`SENSOR_LANDSCAPE`, so it works with auto-rotate off and still flips with the phone), not only the tuner, so the screen doesn't turn on every tab switch; the metronome, Practice and Settings use their side layouts. Large screens may ignore the request (Android 16+), where the window's shape decides as before (switched off with auto-rotate off, it first turns back upright: the Xiaomi otherwise stayed on its side)
- ☑ In focus mode the tuner shows today's compact layout (big meter and note), but the navigation rail with its Instrument item **stays**, so the meter is a little narrower (about 80 dp less); a tap still brings the tuner's own controls (mode switch, strum check switch, strings, more tools) for 5 s, and the "Tap to show the controls" hint stays
- ☑ Focus mode off (the default): the full two-pane layout on its side, the single column upright, as with "Full layout on its side" on today
- ☑ The setting: `keepTwoPaneLandscape` replaced by `focusMode` (default off; the app isn't released, so no carrying over of old values), "Focus mode" with a summary like "Turns Twangle on its side with one big meter, easy to read from a distance", in every language; TalkBack and 2× text
- ☑ Tests: `TunerLayoutTest`, `TunerScreenTest` and `CompactLandscapeStylesTest` (the rail now shown) and the Settings tests updated; a test that focus mode asks for the side orientation and lets go when switched off; phone check upright with auto-rotate off
- ☑ Docs: docs/architecture/ui.md (*Navigation*, *Landscape and wide screens*), the old design to history.md; README.md, PRIVACY.md (the setting's name, then `scripts/publish-privacy.sh`) and docs/store-listing.md (the big meter is now a setting, screenshot 8) (the policy published on 2026-10-17)
- ☑ Owner check added: turn Focus mode on with auto-rotate off, read the meter from a distance, switch tabs, then switch it off

- ☑ Style backgrounds reworked to abstract patterns, picked by the owner from [docs/design/1.x/style-backgrounds-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/style-backgrounds-preview.html): Ancient rock layers, Retro supergraphic, Contemporary silk lines, Futuristic glowing grid fading away (no horizon line), Space Age halftone; Classic unchanged
- ☑ Ancient's lettering calmer: Forum (antique Roman letters, with Polish, Turkish and Russian) instead of Caesar Dressing, picked by the owner from [docs/design/1.x/ancient-font-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/ancient-font-preview.html)
- ☑ Privacy policy brought up to date (every stored setting: instrument, own tunings, metronome, app language, landscape layout; the tour instead of the string-lock tip; the remembered microphone refusal, on the phone and in the watch's own small file, so "stores no settings" no longer holds for the watch; the wave's "when in tune" choice is gone; also the metronome's Listen and the widget) in English and Dutch, and published at a public URL (**decision 5**, owner 2026-10-16: a small separate public repo just for the policy, the code stays private until after release): https://klaboukes.github.io/twangle-privacy/, kept up to date with `scripts/publish-privacy.sh`
- ☑ The contact email (owner, **decision 5**: twangle.tuner@proton.me) in the policy's *Contact* section (English and Dutch, then `scripts/publish-privacy.sh`)
- ☑ Watch app as decided (**decision 3**): if it launches now, a real-watch check, the Wear OS form factor opted in, Wear screenshots and its own track; otherwise it follows in an update (owner, 2026-10-16: it follows in an update after v1.0; v1.0 ships the phone app only)

The Play Console steps done after the split, moved here on 2026-10-24 once 1.0.2 was on the closed track (the open ones stay in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md)):
- ☑ Developer account ready (owner, with Claude's help): identity and Android-device verification, and a public developer contact email (**decision 5**). Owner, 2026-10-21: personal account, identity and device checks passed
- ☑ The policy URL and the contact email entered in the Play Console (once the developer account is ready). 2026-10-22: the policy URL in *App content*, the contact email and website sent with `scripts/release.sh listing`

### Owner checks (live tests)
- ☑ Phase 3: tested on at least 2 physical devices with a real guitar (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)

- ☑ Phase 12: with a real guitar, check the tips feel right: none while playing normally near the phone, "Play a bit louder" when playing very softly from across the room, "Too loud, move the phone back" when strumming hard right next to it (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 14: try each of the six styles on the phone, light and dark, upright and on its side, and say if any colour, letter or background picture feels wrong (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 16: make a tuning of your own ("New tuning" in the instrument and tuning panel), tune a real guitar to it, then change it with Edit and delete it (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 17: hold each string of a real guitar's tuning and tune by ear to the tone; check it sounds like a plucked string, at a comfortable loudness, and fades out by itself (owner, 2026-10-14: good enough for now)
- ☑ Phase 18: with a real guitar in a quiet room, switch on strum check (the strings button beside "Guitar · Standard"), strum all six open strings and check each one reads like the tuner does when you play it alone; then detune one or two strings a little and leave one out (mute it). Try it in drop D too (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 19: play along with the metronome for a few minutes with a real guitar, at a few tempos and meters: check it feels steady, the accents help you find beat 1, and the clicks are pleasant at a comfortable loudness (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 22: native speakers look over the new translations where possible (owner, 2026-10-16: no native speakers at hand; trusts the translations, and users can report anything that reads wrong)
- ☑ Phase 25: tune a real guitar with each of the six meter looks, light and dark, and say if any feels hard to read or too busy (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Ticks and clicks: with no string ringing, tap the desk, click a pen and let a clock tick next to the phone: the needle stays still. Then tune a real guitar and check a plucked string still shows promptly and holds steady while you tap; the G string stays on G3 (it used to jump to E4) (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 26: with the metronome playing, unplug wired/Bluetooth headphones (it stops) and take a phone call (it stops) (owner, 2026-10-16: fine)
- ☑ Phase 27: play a few songs from a speaker (slow, medium, fast, and one with a loose feel) and let the metronome listen: the tempo it finds matches the song, and clicking along feels right (owner, 2026-10-16: fine; only slow jazz is a bit hard for it to pick up)
- ☑ Phase 29: with a real guitar (a debug build), turn on the 12th-fret check from the hammer-and-screwdriver button above the strings and check every string: tune it, play it open, then pressed at the 12th fret; check each verdict (fine, or move the saddle back / forward, about how many mm) matches what a guitar shop would say (owner, 2026-10-17: passed)
- ☑ Phase 30: with a real guitar (a debug build), pick "Chord name" from the hammer-and-screwdriver button above the strings and strum a dozen chords you know, open and barre (for example E, A, D, G, C, Am, Em, Dm, E7, G7, Cmaj7, Am7, Dsus4, F and Bm as barre chords, and C with E in the bass): each shows its right name about a second after the strum and stays while it rings, the name doesn't flicker, and a muddled strum says "Not a clear chord" instead of a wrong name (owner, 2026-10-17: passed)
- ☑ Phase 31: with a real guitar (a debug build), pick "Pitch trail" from the hammer-and-screwdriver button above the strings. Hold a note for a few seconds, then bend a string up and let it back, then play vibrato: the line runs flat along the middle, in the "in tune" colour, while the held note is in tune, rises and falls with the bend, and wavers with the vibrato, clearly enough to see how steady your hand is; it breaks when the note stops. Switch to Chromatic and sing or hum a note: the line stays and follows it (owner, 2026-10-17: looks good)
- ☑ Phase 32: with a real guitar (a debug build), open Practice and pick Chords at the top. In standard tuning look up a dozen chords you know (for example C, G, D, A, E, Am, Em, Dm, F, B7, Cmaj7, Dsus4) and play the first shape of each, then step through a few others with ‹ ›: each is comfortable to hold and sounds the chord named, and the notes under the chart are the ones you play. Switch to Scales and play A minor pentatonic, G major and E blues along the neck: every dot is a note of the scale and the root dots are where you expect. Then pick a tuning of your own (or DADGAD) in the Instrument item and look up a few chords and a scale again: the shapes and the neck follow that tuning (owner, 2026-10-17: passed)
- ☑ Phase 33: with a real guitar (a debug build), open Practice and pick Capo at the top. Work out the capo for two songs you know that are played with a capo: pick the shapes you play under "Shapes I play" and move the capo with ‹ › until "Sounds in" shows the song's key; the table should list the chords you finger and what they sound. Put the capo there and play both songs along with a recording: they sound in the right key. Tap a chord in the table: it opens in Chords with its shape on your tuning (owner, 2026-10-17: passed)
- ☑ **Phase 45, the metronome's clicks:** open Practice → Metronome and tap **Sound**, the first button under the dial: a panel opens with the four sounds (wood block, cowbell, claves, rim click) and "Vibrate on the beat". While stopped, a tap on a sound plays one bar of it; while playing, the new sound is heard from the next beat; Back or a tap beside the panel closes it. Play along at a few tempos with each click, from the speaker over a guitar and in headphones: each is easy to hear, beat 1 stands out, and none is tiring after a few minutes (owner, 2026-10-26: the sounds are good)
- ☑ **Phases 36 and 40, focus mode:** with auto-rotate off and the phone upright, tap the four-corners button right above the tools button: the whole app turns on its side and the tuner fills the screen (no tabs, no clock bar), with the note small in the top left corner and the corners-pointing-in button in the top right. Play a string and read the meter from a few steps away. Tap the screen: the controls and tabs come back for a few seconds, and the button above the tools button (corners pointing out) brings the big meter straight back. Switch tabs and back: the screen doesn't turn. Turn the phone the other way round: the app follows. Tap the top-right button: focus mode goes off and the app turns back upright. Turn it on again and swipe Back from the edge (maybe twice): it switches off the same way and the app stays open (owner, 2026-10-26: passed)
- ☑ **Phase 48, the drone under Scales:** in Practice → Scales tap ▶ and play a scale over the drone for a few minutes, from the speaker and in headphones: steady, no clicks, in tune with the tuner, a new root follows smoothly, and it stops on leaving Scales (another tool, another tab, the screen off). Try the four sounds, the notes and *Lower* in the panel behind the sound's icon (the flute organ, the pad and *Lower* are known to be quiet on the speaker) (owner, 2026-10-27: passed)
- ☑ **Phase 55, Ear training:** with a real guitar, a few minutes of each exercise (Practice tab → tool button → Ear training; the three icons pick Play it back, Intervals and Chord type), from the phone's speaker and in headphones: Play it back hears right and wrong notes reliably and never takes the question for the answer; the sounds are clear (a strummed chord about as loud as a single note, nothing crackling); the buttons are easy to tell apart (with all twelve intervals and all twenty chord types chosen in *What is asked* too); and nothing flickers (owner, 2026-10-28: "I tested live. Sounds OK")
- ☑ **Phase 46, stiff strings:** with a real guitar and a pick, play each open string hard and watch the needle: it shows the note within a fifth of a second and settles smoothly, never jumping to another note. On the low E, A and D expect it to start several cents sharp and come down over about two seconds, on the G, B and high E to settle within half a second: that is the string itself, whose overtones start sharp and settle (measured on your own guitar in Phase 46's recordings), not the tuner. Then run the 12th-fret check on a guitar a shop has set up: every string `FINE` (owner, 2026-10-29: done)
- ☑ **Phase 62, the metronome's clicks between the beats and the speed trainer** (2026-10-31; only a guitar and headphones, with the debug build from Claude, as the speed trainer is off in release builds until this passes): play along with eighths, triplets and sixteenths (Options → *Between the beats*), first through the phone's speaker, then in headphones; then practise a passage with the speed trainer (Options → *Speed trainer*, for example from 80 to 120, +5 every 4 bars). It passes if it is easy to follow, the beat still stands out from the clicks in between, nothing gets tiring after a few minutes, and there is no crackle at 250 BPM in sixteenths. Then Claude turns the speed trainer on for release (`ToolSwitches.speedTrainer` in `app/.../BuildToolSwitches.kt`) and the website's "Coming soon" note becomes its own section (owner, 2026-10-31: the clicks and the speed trainer are fine; the trainer turned on for release the same day)
- ☑ **Phase 20, a Wear OS watch:** install Twangle on it and tune a real guitar: the note and cents follow the string, the ring glows softly in tune, and the microphone icon disappears when the screen goes dark or dims to always-on (the watch app follows in an update, decision 3) — the owner's Galaxy Watch 5 with a guitar (2026-10-30 and 2026-10-31, Phase 61): note, cents and glow follow the strings; the microphone lets go 0.2 s after the screen goes dark, and also as it dims to always-on (switched on for the check only). Found on the way: waking from always-on onto the watch face opened the microphone behind it for some 0.4 s; fixed the same day (the screen coming on counts only with the tuner on it)

## Phase 40 — Focus mode, full screen
Goal: in focus mode the meter is as big as the screen allows. **Owner (2026-10-19):** "Focus mode should be all about displaying the meter as big as possible. The other information on screen should be much smaller or even absent", and asked Claude to suggest the rest. The owner approved the rough mock-up [docs/design/1.x/focus-mode-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/focus-mode-preview.html) the same day.
- ☑ The plain tuner goes full screen in focus mode: no rail, no status bar and no navigation bar while its controls are away (a swipe from the edge shows the system bars for a moment); a tap brings back the controls, the rail and the bars for 5 s, as before. The other tabs keep their side layouts with the rail, and the whole app stays on its side, so the screen doesn't turn on a tab switch
- ☑ The meter fills the screen at its 2:1 shape: about 750 × 375 dp on the Xiaomi 12X, against about 490 dp wide before. Only the note stays, small (48 dp) in the top start corner, green in tune; without a note the hint, and a reception tip under it. No cents, frequency, string row, wave or signal bars. "Tap to show the controls" moves to the top end corner
- ☑ With a tool on (strum check, 12th-fret check, chord name, pitch trail) its panel stays beside its own text and buttons, also full screen
- ☑ Leaving focus mode without Settings (owner, 2026-10-19): an exit button (the four-corners "exit full screen" icon, no word) in the top end corner of the controls a tap brings up, and Back while the tuner is in focus mode; both switch focus mode off, and the app turns back upright. "Exit focus mode" translated into every language
- ☑ Turning focus mode on from the tuner (owner, 2026-10-19): a "full screen" button (four corners pointing out, no word) right above the "more tools" button and drawn like it (the bare icon in the top corner was hardly visible, in Space Age least of all), where the exit button is too; the Settings switch stays. The tour has a step for it, "Full screen, one big meter" (4 of 8), left out while focus mode is on. Texts in every language
- ☑ Tests: `TunerContentTest`, `CompactLandscapeStylesTest` and `FocusModeOrientationTest` for the new layout, the full-screen report and the rail and status bar on the phone; the store screenshots' picture 8 (pitch trail in focus mode) crops no rail any more
- ☑ Docs: docs/architecture/ui.md (*Navigation bar*, *Landscape and wide screens*), the old design to history.md, lifecycle.md, README.md; the owner check under *Owner checks* updated

## Phase 41 — Releases through the Google Play API
Goal: one command sends a release, the store texts and the pictures to Google Play, so the owner no longer drags files in or pastes 16 languages by hand. Owner, 2026-10-21: before Phase 23's store-listing step, which it then does; Phase 23's Play Console forms and the policy URL don't wait for it. It uses the Google Play Developer API through the Gradle Play Publisher plugin (Triple-T, version in the catalog) and runs on the PC only: the app itself doesn't change (no `INTERNET` permission).
- ☑ Owner, with Claude guiding in the browser: a Google Cloud project with the Google Play Android Developer API switched on, a service account and its JSON key, saved next to the upload key in `~/.twangle-keys/` (never in the repo); the service account invited in the Play Console (*Users and permissions*) for Twangle only, with permission to release and to edit the store listing. Done 2026-10-22: project *Twangle Play* (`twangle-play`), service account `twangle-uploader@twangle-play.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, key `~/.twangle-keys/play-service-account.json`; in the Play Console for Twangle only: view app information, release to production and to testing tracks, manage testing tracks, manage store presence
- ☑ Gradle Play Publisher in `:app`, reading the key from `~/.twangle-keys/` as the upload key is read; without it (CI, other machines) nothing changes and nothing fails. It takes the texts, release notes and pictures from `fastlane/metadata/android/`, still the one place they live (`StoreListingTest` keeps guarding them). Version 4.1.1 (4.x is the line for AGP 9); the key is `~/.twangle-keys/play-service-account.json`; `syncPlayResourcesFromFastlane` copies the fastlane files into the plugin's layout in `build/` at task time
- ☑ `scripts/release.sh upload`: the signed bundle and its notes in every language to the internal track; while Twangle is still a draft on Play, as a draft release the owner starts with one click. `upload completed` once the app is out of draft; checked up to Google's sign-in with a made-up key, the real upload waits for the key
- ☑ `scripts/release.sh promote closed|production` and `rollout <percent>|halt`: the same build to another track, a staged rollout raised step by step, or halted. The closed test is Play's track `alpha` (the one the Play Console makes first); production starts at 20 %; both promotions go from the internal track, the closed one as a draft by default like `upload`; checked up to Google's sign-in with a made-up key
- ☑ `scripts/release.sh listing`: title, short and full description, screenshots (`scripts/store-screenshots.sh`), icon and feature graphic for every language. Also Play's app details (default language en-US, the contact email and the policy page as website, decision 5), which Play replaces together; refuses until every language has its eight screenshots (Play would show the English ones), checks them and `StoreListingTest` first, and sends the screenshots as 24-bit PNG (Play takes none with alpha); the icon and feature graphic go with en-US only, which Play shows for every language; checked up to Google's sign-in with a made-up key
- ☑ docs/release.md and the `/release` skill updated: Claude asks the owner before every upload, promotion and listing change, since each changes what people get or see; the owner still checks the internal build on the Xiaomi before it is promoted
- ☑ Tried for real: the store listing sent for every language, and a build on the internal track as a draft (2026-10-22: the listing in all 16 languages with 8 screenshots each, the icon, feature graphic and app details, read back from Play; the first try failed, as Play takes a language's pictures only once it has that listing, so `listing` now sends the texts first (#317); Twangle 1.0.1 (versionCode 10001) on the internal track as a draft, beside 1.0.0 that the owner had put there by hand)

## Phase 45 — A choice of metronome clicks
Goal: the metronome can sound in more than the wood block, so it can be heard over a loud amp or a band and stays pleasant through a long practice. The owner (2026-10-25) chose four sounds, wood block, cowbell, claves and rim click, picked in the metronome itself, not in Settings; a rough mock-up, [docs/design/1.x/metronome-sound-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/metronome-sound-preview.html), decides how. Every click keeps the accents as today (beat 1 higher and louder, the group beats in between) and the peak under 0.8; the clicks are 80–180 ms long, still under a beat at 250 BPM (240 ms), so `ClickTrack` keeps one voice and timing, beat lights and vibration don't change. In order:
- ☑ **Hear them first:** three or four candidates made in `ClickSounds`' way (a few decaying partials, no recordings, so nothing to licence), e.g. a soft electronic beep (a sine with a quick decay), a sharp high click that cuts through an amp, and a low, round knock for players who hear high sounds poorly. Rendered as short WAV files (a bar of 4/4 at 100 BPM each, then silence, so nothing loops) into `docs/design/` with a small page to play them side by side with the wood block. **The owner** listens on the phone's speaker and in headphones and picks the two (or three) that join the wood block *The owner's choice (2026-10-25), after several rounds: four instruments, each clearly its own, picked with a Sound button in the metronome: today's wood block (the default), the cowbell, the claves and the rim click. Round 1 (soft beep, sharp click, low knock) sounded "like a cheap keyboard"; round 2 (real instruments ringing 0.2–0.4 s in a little room) was too much instrument; round 3 made clicks to keep time by (a sharp attack, short, the same every beat, beat 1 hit harder and an octave up), but tuned to nearly the same notes they sounded like one instrument. Now each lives in its own register and keeps its nature: the cowbell a choked, clanging metal bell (tone 560 Hz, beating partials to 3 kHz, 40 dB down at 132 ms), the claves a glassy rosewood tone (2.2 kHz, 100 ms), the rim click a dry, noisy crack with a short drum thump (64 ms), the wood block as it is (880 Hz, 36 ms). Beat 1 is +5.1–5.4 dB at the peak and an octave up (claves: a fifth); the clicks are 80–180 ms long, under a beat at 250 BPM (240 ms), so `ClickTrack` keeps one voice. Samples, alone and under a strummed guitar, with the measurements: `docs/design/click-sounds-preview/` (open `index.html`; the same eight files are on the Xiaomi in Music/Twangle-clicks). Made by `ClickCandidatesReport` and `PercussionSynth` in `:core:dsp`'s test sources (`./gradlew :core:dsp:benchmark --tests '*ClickCandidatesReport*'`), not in the app.*
- ☑ **The clicks in `:core:dsp`:** `ClickSound` with the four sounds, the wood block first (the default), then the cowbell, the claves and the rim click, synthesised in the app from the recipes in `PercussionSynth` (test sources), moved into main code as needed; each rendered once per sample rate as today, allocation-free to play; `ClickSoundsTest` for every sound: its actual length (the click under a beat at 250 BPM), the fade ending on exactly 0, no clipping, the accents told apart by pitch and level, and loud enough next to the wood block (the same RMS within 1 dB) *`ClickRecipes` and `PercussionSynth` are main code now, and the report writes the samples from them. At 48 kHz `ClickTrack` gives every 16-bit sample of the approved samples exactly (the floats too, checked while moving them); at other rates the struck sounds are resampled from 48 kHz, so they stay the same (the rim click up to 0.4 dB quieter, its crack peaking between samples). `ClickTrack.sound` switches from the next beat. 242 KB at 48 kHz for all four, each rendered when first used.*
- ☑ **Saved and played:** the choice in `SettingsRepository` (`metronomeClickSound`, wood block when unset), `MetronomeViewModel` hands it to the player, and a change while it plays is heard from the next beat; `AndroidMetronomePlayerTest` and `MetronomeViewModelTest` *Also the preview the Sound panel needs: `MetronomeViewModel.previewSound` picks and saves a sound; stopped, one bar of it plays once (`isPreviewing`), ended by the next pick, Start, Listen or leaving the screen; playing, it only switches the sound. Every `MetronomeBeat` says its `sound`.*
- ☑ **Picked in the metronome:** the owner picked option A of the mock-up on 2026-10-25: a Sound button, first in the row (Sound · Tap tempo · Listen; it replaces Vibrate), its icon the click picked, opening a panel with the four sounds and the "Vibrate on the beat" switch; the icons from round 2 of the mock-up (the owner's picks: wood block A3, claves A5). A tap on a sound plays one bar of it while stopped (never looping) and switches from the next beat while playing. Names in every language, TalkBack says each name; steady sizes in every language at 2× text (`MetronomeScreenTest`, `MetronomeStylesTest`); the metronome doc *Done 2026-10-26: `SoundButton` and `SoundPanel` (`SoundParts.kt`); four line icons in the row icons' style (the rim click's drum clipped round its stick), ic_vibrate's two marks beside the icon while vibration is on; "Sound" always under the button; the panel on the surface colour with a soft lift and no border, the picked sound in the container colour with an accent outline, above Sound upright (start edges in line) and beside it towards the dial on its side, scrolling only where the room is short; Back or a tap beside it closes it. TalkBack: "Sound: Cowbell" (", vibrates on the beat"), the panel by name, the sounds a radio group. "Sound" and the four instruments' common names in all 16 languages; the old short "Vibrate" label and ic_vibrate went. `MetronomeScreenTest`, `MetronomeStylesTest` in all six styles light and dark (the panel at 1×, 1.25× and 2× on a 360 dp phone and the Xiaomi, upright and on its side, Dutch at 2×; 30 passed in the six styles); :feature:metronome 34, :app 33 UI tests passed. On the Xiaomi in Contemporary dark (wallpaper colours) and Retro light, upright and on its side, and Futuristic dark upright; the panel is on the raised surface colour, as plain `surface` was nearly the background with wallpaper colours. The privacy policy now names the click sound among the saved settings.*
- ☑ **Owner check** added under *Owner checks*: play along at a few tempos with each click, from the speaker over a guitar and in headphones: each is easy to hear, beat 1 stands out, and none is tiring after a few minutes

## Phase 43 — UI and UX polish (the review of 2026-10-24)
Goal: the rough edges Claude found reviewing v1.0.2 on the Xiaomi (every tab, the tools, the tour, focus mode and the store pictures) are smoothed before v1.0 goes public. Most important first; each item is one increment, and the owner picks where an item offers a choice (a rough mock-up in one style, as usual). Each lands with its UI tests, every language where words change, and the architecture doc of the screen it touches. After a few have landed, `/release patch` sends them to the closed testers.
- ☑ **Green stays for "in tune" under wallpaper colours:** with "Wallpaper colours" on (the default, decision 2 of Phase 26) a green wallpaper makes the accent green too, so the picked segment, the open tab, the metronome's Start and the strum check's markers all look "in tune" (seen on the Xiaomi, 2026-10-24: accent and in-tune colour nearly the same green). The six styles keep green for in tune on purpose (`StylePaletteTest` holds the accents out of the greens); the wallpaper scheme has no such guard (`colorSchemeFor` in `TwangleTheme.kt`). When the wallpaper's accent comes within about 40° of the in-tune hue, the accent falls back to Contemporary's own blue (the rest of the wallpaper scheme stays), light and dark; the widget follows since it uses the same scheme. A JVM test with a green wallpaper scheme; a phone check with a green wallpaper. The setting stays on by default. *Done 2026-10-25: `keepingInTuneApart` in `TwangleTheme.kt`, measured in OKLCH hue (HSV put Material's usual green 43° from the in-tune green); `WallpaperColorsTest`; on the Xiaomi's own green wallpaper the toggle, the open tab, the picked segment and Start are Contemporary's blue, light and dark, the background keeps the wallpaper's green tint.*
- ☑ **No check mark beside a number that says "flat":** in tune holds until 7 cents (Precise: 3), so the pill can read "7 cents flat" with the check mark; store screenshot 8 shows exactly that. While the check shows, the pill says "In tune" (translated into every language; TalkBack says it already) and the cents go to the frequency line or go; in the wave display, where the pill holds only the check, nothing changes. `TunerContentTest`, `NoteDisplay`; the store screenshots made again in the next overnight run *Done 2026-10-25: the needle's pill says "In tune" (`tuner_in_tune`, TalkBack's words, capitalised per language) while the check shows; the cents go rather than move to the frequency line (calmer: the needle shows them, and the line doesn't change length in and out of tune); the pill keeps the width of its widest text. `TunerContentTest` (`inTunePillSaysInTuneInsteadOfTheCents`, `inTunePillSaysItInDutch`, `leavingTuneBringsTheCentsBackWithoutMovingThePill`); on the Xiaomi a short E4 tone 4 cents flat read "In tune" over 328.9 Hz. Store screenshots: next overnight run.*
- ☑ **Metronome: words under the three buttons, and a beat you can see:** Tap tempo, Listen and Vibrate are icons only (72 × 52 dp, the name only in a long-press tooltip), and the beat lights are small (48 dp) and far above the dial with an empty band between. Short labels under the three buttons (the tooltip's words, one line, set smaller where a language needs it, the buttons keeping one size), and the beat lights larger and nearer the dial, using that band (or on the dial's arc), so the beat reads from a music stand. Every style, Dutch at 2× text, upright and on its side (`MetronomeStylesTest`, `MetronomeScreenTest`); the metronome doc *Done 2026-10-25: "Tap tempo", "Listen", "Vibrate" under the buttons (a new short `metronome_vibrate_label` in every language, as "Vibrate on the beat" didn't fit; one size for all three, set smaller down to 8 dp where needed; hidden from TalkBack, the tooltips gone); upright the beat lights stand right over the dial and take half the spare height, 48 up to 80 dp (80 on the Xiaomi, the marks 1.67× larger), the empty band now above them under the meter picker; nothing moves when they flash. `MetronomeStylesTest` in every style (36 passed), every language at 2×, `MetronomeScreenTest`; on the Xiaomi, stopped and playing.*
- ☑ **Chromatic mode's empty lower third:** without the strings the lower third of the screen is empty and the focus-mode button floats alone above nothing. A rough mock-up (Contemporary) with two ideas for the owner: (a) the focus button in one place that exists in both modes, beside the mode switch, say, as the owner found the bare top-corner icon hard to see in Phase 40; (b) chromatic mode keeps the controls row (the instrument button, which switches to Strings mode on a pick, as today from the panel) and shows the cents and frequency larger where the strings were. Build the owner's pick; `TunerContentTest`, `TunerStylesTest`, `CompactLandscapeStylesTest`, the tour's `FOCUS` step moved with the button *Done 2026-10-25, the owner's pick A1 ("the Strings and Chromatic mode should look the same, buttons should not flip to other screen positions"): focus mode's button at the end of the mode switch's row, top right, its outline at the 16 dp text margin, in both modes in the same place; chromatic mode still shows no instrument or tools button. The switch stays centred between two 52 dp slots, its labels shrinking to fit (Dutch and Russian at 2× text on a 360 dp phone wholly shown); the signal bars, when on, moved to the row's start, across from the button, so the switch stays centred with them too. The switch's labels now give up padding before they shrink and keep one height, so the switch no longer changes size on a tap at 2× text. On the Xiaomi both modes look alike, the button top right. Among focus mode's controls the same button there takes them away; the full-screen tuner's exit button keeps its own corner (Phase 40). ## Phase 47 — Practice: one tool button, Scales a tool of its own, more scales (owner, 2026-10-26)
Goal: the chord chart and the scale neck get the room the controls take today. Practice → Chords stacks four rows before the chart: the tool switch, the instrument and tuning line, Chords · Scales, and the chord's short name with its name in words under it. The owner never liked the switch on top, and it has room for three tools only (the Practice tab section of [docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md](docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md)), so a fourth (Scales here, ear training in Phase 54) needs another way to switch anyway; the tool list has room for more. Aim: two rows above the chart, the tool and the tuning on one, the name on the next. Replaces Phase 43's "Fewer control rows above the chord chart". Before Phase 50, as store screenshot 7 shows Practice → Chords. Each item is one increment and lands with its UI tests, every language where words change, and the Practice tab section of the doc above. In order:

For the name, a button as wide as the row with the short name large and the name in words beside it or on a second line inside it ("Am7 · A minor seventh ⌄"), the quiet line under it gone. And whether a mode's own colour note (Dorian's 6th, Lydian's ♯4, Mixolydian's ♭7, …) gets a mark on the neck, since D Dorian shows the same dots as C major but for the root. **Owner picked (2026-10-26): A, D, F**: the one tool button sharing its row with the instrument and tuning line; the name as one wide button of two lines, the short name large and the words on a second line inside it; an accent ring on the mode's colour note
- ☑ **One tool button in place of the tool switch** (pick A): "Chords ⌄" with the tool's icon at the top start, one steady width whatever the tool, sharing its row with the instrument and tuning line (the metronome, which has no tuning, shows the button alone in the same place); a tap opens a short list of the tools with icons, the picked one marked (a soft lift, no border). On every Practice tool (Metronome, Chords, Capo) in the same place (steady positions), opening on the tool used last as today; on its side at the top of the start half, where the switch is now. Tool icons drawn in the row icons' line style. TalkBack names the tool and the list; every language at 2× text on a 320 dp phone. `PracticeToolSwitchTest` becomes the new control's test; `PracticeTabTest`, `MetronomeStylesTest`, `ChordsScreenTest`, `CapoScreenTest`. Built 2026-10-26: `PracticeToolButton` (160 dp, the list a Material menu), `ToolAndTuningRow` (the tuning line drops under the button only where two lines can't hold its name, a 320 dp phone at 2× text), `PracticeToolButtonTest`; checked on the Xiaomi upright and on its side

- ☑ **The name as wide as the row:** pick D on Chords and Scales: one button as wide as the row, two lines inside it, the short name large with ⌄ and the name in words under it (the quiet line under today's button gone), one steady height whatever the name (the chart never moves), its panel as today; TalkBack unchanged. ## Phase 49 — Four more chord types, two more scales (owner, 2026-10-26)
Goal: the chords and scales guitarists ask for that Practice lacks. The power chord above all, the most played "chord" in rock, punk and metal (root and fifth, two or three strings), which many beginners learn before any full chord; then the 7♯9 (the "Hendrix chord", funk and blues rock), m9 and maj9 (the soft sound of neo-soul, R&B and pop ballads). For the scales, major blues (the brighter twin of the blues scale: country, major-key blues) and Phrygian dominant (the flamenco and Middle-Eastern sound, much used in metal). 11ths, 13ths, altered chords and the rarer scales (whole-tone, diminished, Hungarian minor) stay out, so the panels stay calm. Both grids stay full (the owner likes even rows): twenty chord types in five rows of four, fourteen scales in seven rows of two, the most used first. Each item is one increment and lands with its tests, names in all 16 languages, every language at 2× text on a 320 dp phone, and the Practice tab section of [docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md](docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md). In order:
- ☑ **Four chord types in `:core:dsp`:** `ChordQuality` gets the power chord (5: root, fifth and optionally the octave), 7♯9, m9 and maj9 (the fifth optional in the 9ths, as guitarists play them), with their symbols (C5, C7♯9, Cm9, Cmaj9) and spelling (`ChordSpellingTest`), and `ChordShapeFinder` finding playable shapes for each on every instrument and tuning, the power chord on two or three adjacent strings with the rest muted (`ChordShapeFinderTest`, `ChordShapeReport`). Whether the tuner's chord name hears them (`detected`): the power chord yes, since it is strummed so often, named "E5" and never guessed as E or Em from a third that isn't there (`ChordDetectorTest`, `ChordNamerTest`, `ChordAccuracyReport` with no other chord's figures getting worse); the other three only if the accuracy report shows they don't take names from chords already heard — done: all four heard; the 41 shapes' figures unchanged, power chords 16 / 0 / 0 as played and never E or Em, the 9ths right or their 7th chord (Am9 as Am7 when the ninth is unheard). Their words are in all 16 languages for the tuner and the Chords tool already; the panel still shows the sixteen (`ChordQuality.inPanel`) until the item below
- ☑ **The four in the Chords panel:** five full rows of four, the most used first (the power chord in the top rows), each named in words and for TalkBack in every language ("C power chord", "C seven sharp nine"); the chord name in the tuner if the item above made them heard. `ChordsScreenTest`, `ChordSpeechTest` or its equivalent, the store screenshots unchanged — done: C Cm C5 C7 · Cmaj7 Cm7 Csus2 Csus4 · C7sus4 C6 Cm6 Cadd9 · C9 Cm9 Cmaj9 C7♯9 · Cdim Cdim7 Cm7♭5 Caug (`ChordQuality.inPanel`, `ChordSpellingTest`); the tuner already named all four
- ☑ **Two scales:** `ScaleType` gets major blues (1 2 ♭3 3 5 6, written in the major key, the blue note ♭3 written flat) and Phrygian dominant (1 ♭2 3 4 5 ♭6 ♭7, the 5th mode of harmonic minor, written with the signs of the harmonic minor a fourth above: E F G♯ A B C D; its colour note the major 3rd, ringed), in seven full rows of two, still the most used first: Major blues beside Blues, Phrygian dominant beside Phrygian, the rows around them moved as needed. `ScaleTest` cases for both on a few roots (notes and spelling), `ScaleSpeechTest`, `ScalesScreenTest`; the scales part of [docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md](docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md) — done: Major · Minor · Major pentatonic · Minor pentatonic · Blues · Major blues · Dorian · Mixolydian · Harmonic minor · Lydian · Phrygian · Phrygian dominant · Melodic minor · Locrian (Lydian moved up a row so Phrygian dominant sits beside Phrygian); G major blues G A B♭ B D E, E Phrygian dominant E F G♯ A B C D with its G♯ ringed; all 16 languages at 2× on a 320 dp phone; checked on the Xiaomi

## Phase 48 — A drone under the scales (owner, 2026-10-26)
Goal: the Scales tool can sound a steady low note, the scale's root (alone, with its fifth, or with its octave), that keeps sounding while the player plays over it. It lets you hear how each note of a scale sits against the key, which is how players practise intonation (violin, cello, fretless bass, singing) and how a mode's sound is learned: D Dorian over a D drone sounds unlike C major over a C drone, though the notes are the same, so it builds on Phase 47's modes. A play button on Scales, not a tool of its own, so it follows the root and key picked there; never in the tuner, whose microphone would hear it. Made in the app like the reference tones (no recordings, nothing to licence), played only while Scales is shown (no foreground service, as the metronome). In order:
- ☑ **Hear them first:** three or four candidate sounds made the way `StringSynth` and `PercussionSynth` are (e.g. the bowed cello voice the tuner already has, a soft organ, a tanpura-like hum with its slow shimmer), each rendered as a short WAV (a few seconds, the root alone and with its fifth, fading out, nothing loops) into `docs/design/` with a small page to play them side by side, as Phase 45 did. **The owner** listens on the phone's speaker and in headphones and picks the sound (or a choice of two), and whether the fifth and the octave are offered *Round 2 ready, the owner's pick pending (2026-10-26). Round 1 (the tuner's cello held, a soft organ, a steady tanpura-like hum, a shruti box) sounded "very similar" to the owner: steady stacks of harmonics with nearly the same slope. Round 2 makes four different kinds of instrument: a **tanpura** re-plucked in its cycle (Pa, Sa, Sa, low Sa, a pluck every 1.5 s, each pluck made afresh, its jawari buzz blooming after the attack), a **harmonium** (two reeds a note a few cents apart, nasal, the bellows swelling), a **flute organ** (a stopped pipe, nearly pure, still) and a **warm pad** (five detuned sawtooth voices through a slowly opening and closing low-pass). Root alone at D3, they are far apart: centroid 987 / 726 / 180 / 307 Hz, energy above 400 Hz (a phone speaker's stand-in) 57 / 43 / 4 / 15 %, level moving 10.2 / 8.8 / 0.2 / 5.1 dB, brightness moving 490–1877 / 371–1134 / none / 167–490 Hz; the app's detector reads each within 0.4 cents on average. Each as D3 alone, with its fifth, with fifth and octave, A2 with its fifth (8 s each) and under the D Dorian scale on the steel guitar, all equally loud (K-weighted): `docs/design/drone-sounds-preview/` (open `index.html`; the same twenty files are on the Xiaomi in Music/Twangle-drones). Made by `DroneCandidates` and `DroneCandidatesReport` in `:core:dsp`'s test sources (`./gradlew :core:dsp:benchmark --tests '*DroneCandidatesReport*'`), not in the app.* **The owner's pick (2026-10-26): all four** (tanpura, harmonium, flute organ, warm pad) are offered, with every variation: the root alone, root + fifth, root + fifth + octave, and *Lower* (the whole drone an octave down). Known caveat: the flute organ and the pad (and *Lower*) are very quiet on a phone's speaker.
- ☑ **The drone in `:core:dsp`:** the picked sound rendered on and on rather than as a looped piece, so it never clicks or drifts; on pitch within 1 cent and following the A4 setting; a short fade in, out and across a change of root; allocation-free in its render loop (hard rule 2). `DroneSynthTest`: pitch, no clipping (peak under 0.8, as the clicks), no seam or click at any point, the fades ending on exactly 0 *Done 2026-10-26: `DroneSynth` (`drone/`), all four sounds ported from round 2's candidates (same centroid and share above 400 Hz within a few %), each voicing and lower; the root placed G♯2–G3 (`DroneSynth.rootNote`), lower an octave down; fade in 0.1 s, crossfade 0.4 s across any change, release ending on exactly 0. Every sound within 1 cent by YIN at A4 = 415–466 (harmonium −0.4, the rest within 0.3), all equally loud (−22.5 dB K-weighted), highest peak 0.63, no click by a 14 kHz high-pass, ten minutes steady, no allocation. Cost per 10 ms chunk on the Xiaomi 12X, dearest case: pad 1.1 ms on a big core (3.3 on a little one), harmonium 0.45, tanpura 0.32, flute 0.04 (notes-and-audio.md, *Drone*; performance.md).*
- ☑ **Played in `:core:audio`:** a `DronePlayer` through an `AudioTrack` as `TonePlayer` (`USAGE_MEDIA`, so the media volume sets its loudness), started and stopped from the main thread without blocking it (hard rule 3); `:feature:practice` gets it for playback only, never the microphone (from `:app`, as the tools get their header, or by depending on `:core:audio`: decided in the increment, the Practice tab doc says which). `AndroidDronePlayerTest` *Done 2026-10-26: `DronePlayer` (`play(rootHz, sound, voicing, lower)`, `stop()`, `isPlaying`), `AndroidDronePlayer` on its own writer thread at audio priority, the track only while it sounds, transient audio focus as the tone, stopped by unplugged headphones as the metronome; `FakeDronePlayer` in the test fixtures; `AppContainer.dronePlayer`. `:feature:practice` depends on `:core:audio`, using only `DronePlayer` (ui-other-screens.md, *Practice tab*). On the Xiaomi 12X: six tests pass, no underrun through a run of crossfades (notes-and-audio.md, *Drone*).*
- ☑ **The button on Scales:** a rough mock-up first (Contemporary), then a ▶ / ■ button of one steady size by the name or the neck; the drone follows a new root or scale at once (the fade), stops when Scales goes (another tool, another tab, the screen off) and starts again only on a tap; its sound choice in the same place if the owner wanted one. **Planned (owner, 2026-10-26):** only a ▶ / ■ button on Scales itself, defaulting to the tanpura with the fifth; a small drone panel like the metronome's Sound panel with three rows: *Sound* (tanpura, harmonium, flute organ, warm pad, in that order), *Notes* (Root / + fifth / + fifth and octave) and *Lower* (on/off), all three remembered. Known caveat to show or say: the flute organ and the pad (and *Lower*) are very quiet on a phone's speaker. TalkBack ("Play the drone", "Stop the drone"), every language at 2× text, every style; `ScalesScreenTest`; the Practice tab doc and the privacy policy if a choice is saved *Mock-up ready, owner's pick pending (2026-10-26): [docs/design/1.x/drone-button-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/drone-button-preview.html), drawn from the Xiaomi's Scales screen upright and on its side, dark and light, with one drone panel for all (Sound, Notes, Lower). A: ▶ / ■ and the sound's icon as one split button at the end of the name row, the panel dropping under it (recommended: the neck keeps its height); B: a Sound · Drone row under the neck as the metronome's (the neck a seventh smaller upright); C: ▶ / ■ alone by the name, a long press for the panel. Playing: ■ on the accent and the root's dots breathing softly. On its side the panel opens in the empty start half in every option. **The owner's pick (2026-10-26): A.** Done 2026-10-26: the split button (`DroneButton`, 120 dp, the name's steady height) at the end of the name row, ▶ / ■ following `DronePlayer.isPlaying`, the sound's icon opening `DronePanel` (one column under it upright, two columns over the start half on its side); the root in G♯2–G3 at the A4 setting, followed at once while it sounds; stopped by another tool, another tab, the background or the screen off (not by turning the phone), started again only on a tap; the screen kept on while it sounds and the roots' dots breathing (3 s); `droneSound`, `droneVoicing`, `droneLower` saved, named in the privacy policy. No hint about the quiet sounds (the owner's rule). A long scale name beside the button is set smaller ("G♯ minor pentatonic" at about 80 % on the Xiaomi) and takes three lines where two can't hold it (Russian at 2× on a 320 dp phone). `ScalesScreenTest` (16 tests incl. every language at 2×) and `PracticeTabTest` pass on the Xiaomi 12X; heard briefly on its speaker.*
- ☑ **Owner check** added under *Owner checks*: play a scale over the drone for a few minutes, from the speaker and in headphones: steady, no clicks, in tune with the tuner, a new root follows smoothly, and it stops on leaving Scales *Added 2026-10-26.*

## Phase 51 — Tunings players ask for (the comparison of 2026-10-25, reviewed 2026-10-26)
Goal: the tunings guitarists search for by name are there, and any tuning can be lowered, without a long list to scan. A comparison with eight popular tuners on Play (2026-10-25, from their store pages: GuitarTuna, Fender Tune, LikeTones, Simply Tune, Soundcorset, gStrings, DaTuner) found Twangle ahead on privacy (the only one without the internet permission; every other one's data-safety label lists data it collects), on price (the big ones put tunings, the metronome or the strobe behind ads or a subscription, the most common complaint in their reviews) and on tools (strum check, the 12th-fret check, chord name, a watch app of its own: none of the others lists them), and short on ready-made tunings: 8 for guitar against Fender's 26+ and GuitarTuna's 100+. Those counts are marketing: most of a hundred tunings are one shape shifted down (Drop C♯, Drop C and Drop B are Drop D a semitone, two and three lower; C♯, C and B standard are Standard lowered), and the big tuners charge for them. **The owner's review of 2026-10-26** ("just because other tuners have a feature doesn't make it good or useful; maybe Twangle can offer a better replacement") kept of the comparison's eight items only this phase before launch, since "Drop C" is a word people search for and the work is small: the proof on real strings joined Phase 46 (the same recordings, the same session with the owner); the 12-string, the banjo and left-handed charts went to Phase 57, after launch and narrowed; transposing instruments, the peg-direction drawing, moving to the next string by itself and temperaments went to [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md) with the reasons and the condition that would bring each back. The store listing's item had moved to Phase 50 on 2026-10-25. Each item is one increment. Visible changes get a rough mock-up (Contemporary) for the owner first, and land with their UI tests, every language where words change, and the architecture doc they touch.
- ☑ **A short list of named tunings:** **the owner chose two (2026-10-26):** Open C for the guitar (C2 G2 C3 G3 C4 E4) and the baritone ukulele (D3 G3 B3 E4), with their names in all 16 languages. Drop C, Drop B and C standard are not list entries: the next item, lowering any tuning, gives them with their common names; Drop A on the 7-string and Drop D on the bass were left out by the owner's choice (the tuning editor makes them). No group headings: the list's order already keeps Standard, Drop D, the lowered standards, DADGAD and the open tunings together, and Open C sits last among the open ones (Open G, Open D, Open E, Open C); the owner's own tunings stay first. The baritone is a third ukulele preset (after Standard and Low G), not an instrument of its own: four strings, the ukulele's voice and chord charts, and D3 is well inside the detector's standard window. Open C's C2 is the guitar's lowest preset and takes the low-note window, as the cello does. Chord charts, scales and the capo helper follow both like any tuning (`ChordShapeFinder` tests: baritone G 0003, C 2010, D 0232; Open C's C 000000); `TuningTest`, `InstrumentTest`, `LowPitchRangeTest`, `StrumDetectorTest`, `PickerNamesTest`; the README, the store descriptions in every language and the architecture docs updated
- ☑ **Lower any tuning:** **built 2026-10-26 as the owner chose in the rough mock-up** (`docs/design/1.x/lower-tuning.html`): one quiet "Lower by" row with an up and a down button at the bottom of the panel, under the list (not on top: most casual players rarely use it), none to 2½ steps; picking another tuning or instrument starts it not lowered (the amount is saved for the tuning picked, and editing or deleting a lowered own tuning ends it); no string below B0, so the five-string bass has no row; common names win (Drop C♯/C/B/B♭/A, C♯/C/B/B♭/A standard, Half and Full step down, Open E lowered a step is Open D), else "Open G, ½ step down"; German, Polish and Russian write B as H (Drop H). Planned as: a small "lower by" control in the instrument and tuning panel that drops the picked tuning, presets, open tunings and the player's own alike, by one to five semitones, so "Drop D a step down" or "Open G a half step down" exists without a list, and the chord charts, scales and capo helper follow for free (the shapes don't change; the low window follows as for any tuning). Lower only: tuning up is rare and hard on strings, and the capo helper covers playing higher. Where the result is a tuning with a common name, the button and the picker say that name ("Drop C", not "Drop D −2"; Standard lowered one or two is today's "Half step down" and "Full step down"); otherwise the tuning's name with the shift ("Open G, ½ step down"). A rough mock-up (Contemporary) decides where the control sits in the panel, how a lowered tuning is named, and whether the shift stays when another tuning is picked. `Tuning.lowered(semitones)` in `:core:dsp` with tests (notes, spelling, the common names, the lowest note), saved in `SettingsRepository`, the widget's tuning name, `TunerContentTest`, `MyTuningsStylesTest`, every language at 2× text; the settings and tuner docs, and [PRIVACY.md](PRIVACY.md) and its Dutch copy where they list the saved settings

## Phase 52 — Check on a real tablet (Lenovo Tab M11)
Goal: Twangle looks and works right on a real tablet before v1.0, since Play offers it to tablets too. Owner, 2026-10-21: the owner has a Lenovo Tab M11 and plugs it in later. The layouts already pick their arrangement from the space (`tunerLayoutFor`), and the UI tests already include a tablet-sized screen, so this checks the real thing: the tablet's own density, microphone, speaker and navigation. It starts once the tablet is plugged in (see *Waiting on the owner*); until then `/roadmap-step` skips it, and it doesn't hold up Phase 53.
- ☑ Twangle installed on the tablet; its details (adb serial, Android version, screen size in dp, refresh rate, navigation) and any install quirks noted in CLAUDE.md next to the two phones (2026-10-27: no install quirks, first start clean with the microphone question, cold start 2.8 s in the debug build; `scripts/ui-tests.sh` runs on it)

What looks wrong, each for the fix item below:

Fixed 2026-10-27: the lock was moved by the check itself (every `uiautomator dump` locks the rotation where the screen is, here on its side in focus mode); with plain taps the tablet turns back upright and keeps `user_rotation` 0. Large screens are now let go at once instead of asked for upright (`largeScreen` in `FocusTurn`), which removes the letterbox behind the next item
  - ☑ **Grey band over the status bar after focus mode:** once focus mode is left without the screen turning, a solid grey bar lies behind the clock and icons on every tab (Space Age, on its side) until the tablet is turned. Right: the style's background behind the status bar, as before focus mode. Fixed 2026-10-27: asked for upright with its lock on its side, the tablet letterboxed the app, and leaving the letterbox pushed the content below the status bar; it is no longer asked (`FocusModeOrientationTest.aLargeScreenIsLetGoAtOnceWithNothingOverTheStatusBar` fails without the fix, on the tablet)

Fixed 2026-10-27: "Show me around" closes the tool as a tap on the Tuner tab does (`TourFlowTest.showMeAroundClosesTheToolFirst` fails without the fix, on the tablet)

Right: on a tablet the column grows (say up to 720 dp), the meter with it, so the tuner fills the screen as it does on a phone.
