## Phase 57 — The 12-string, the banjo and left-handed charts (built before launch, 2026-10-27)
Goal: the instruments and the mirrored charts the comparison of 2026-10-25 found missing, as the owner's review of 2026-10-26 narrowed them (Phase 51's goal tells the story). After launch (Phase 53), when reviews or the closed testers ask for them, in the order they ask; it doesn't wait for Phases 55 and 56, which are mostly the owner's steps. Visible changes get a rough mock-up (Contemporary) for the owner first, and land with their UI tests, every language where words change, and the architecture doc they touch. Until someone asks, in this order: *Built before launch instead, at the owner's request (2026-10-27, PRs #410–#413).*
- ☑ **12-string guitar, as six courses:** a 12-string can be tuned with the guitar's six strings already, since a locked string accepts its own octave (*Auto* in [docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md](docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md)), so both strings of a course read against it. The instrument gets six course buttons like the guitar's, not twelve: in standard tuning the lower four courses carry a string an octave up, the top two are in unison. The octave rule applies to the course from the first pluck (today an octave read from scratch is its own note), the reference tone plays the pair (the plucked voice an octave apart, `StringVoices`), and the chord charts, scales and capo helper are the guitar's. The strum check and the chord name stay off for it (twelve strings' overtones overlap too much) and the picker says so. `Instrument`, `TunerInterpreter` tests for the octave rule, the names in every language, `StringRowStylesTest`, every language at 2× text; the README's feature list and the tuner doc *Done (option A of `docs/design/phase57-preview.html`): a small "8" on the lower four courses, the octave string its course's from the first pluck (`Tuning.octaveCourses`), the pair's tone an octave apart within 1.6 cents at 0.3 s; the 12th-fret check kept, with millimetres.*
- ☑ **5-string banjo:** open G (g D G B D, the short fifth string at the row's start) and double C and D tuning if the owner wants them, with a plucked voice of its own (`StringVoices`). For the tuner first: the chord charts and scales don't know a short fifth string that starts at the fifth fret, so Chords, Scales and the capo helper are not offered for it (as the Practice tools already handle an instrument they don't cover) until a later item teaches them that string. `Instrument`, the names in every language, `StringRowStylesTest` and every language at 2× text; the README's feature list *Done (option A of `docs/design/phase57-preview.html`, double C and double D both in): the short string a small 12 dp gap apart (`Tuning.shortStrings`); its own voice (`StringVoices.BANJO`, bright, a quick fall); a quiet line in the chart's, the neck's and the capo table's place; the strum check and the chord name kept (the strum check reads every banjo tuning within 0.12 cents), no 12th-fret check (the short string's octave is at the 17th fret).*
- ☑ **Left-handed charts:** a switch in Settings, off by default, that mirrors the drawn necks, the chord charts, the scale neck and the capo helper's shapes, so they match a guitar held the other way. The tuner's string row stays as it is: it is a list, not a picture of the guitar, and keeps left to right even in Arabic (buttons keep their place). `ChordsScreenTest`, `ScalesScreenTest`, `CapoScreenTest` with it on, TalkBack unchanged; the settings and Practice docs, and [PRIVACY.md](PRIVACY.md) and its Dutch copy where they list the saved settings *Done (option A of `docs/design/phase57-preview.html`): a "Left-handed" switch at the end of "Pitch and notes" (`leftHanded`, saved, named in the privacy policy); the chord chart and the scale neck (upright and sideways) drawn as their mirror image, the "5fr" label kept at the left; TalkBack's words unchanged. The Capo helper draws no shapes (a table; a row opens the shape in Chords, mirrored there), so `CapoScreenTest` needed no change. `ChordsScreenTest` and `ScalesScreenTest` compare each drawing with its mirror image.*

## Phase 54 — The Twangle website, small, before launch (owner, 2026-10-27)
Goal: a small public site, up before the production rollout, that says what Twangle is, explains each tool for players (and, for the curious, how it hears), and answers the common problems, so a review or an email can be answered with a link. It grows from the privacy page Play already links to. The same promise as the app: free (GitHub Pages, no server), **no tracking, no cookies, no contact form, nothing loaded from another site** (no web fonts from Google, no embedded videos, no scripts from elsewhere; the app's own bundled fonts, which their SIL licence allows), plain pages that work without JavaScript, read well on a phone, and follow its light or dark setting; only the contact email (twangle.tuner@proton.me). The app keeps no `INTERNET` permission (hard rule 7): it only opens the site in the phone's browser. English and Dutch first; the other languages after launch (Phase 56). The site explains ideas and walks through each tool rather than every button, so it stays true as the app changes. In order:
- ☑ **The address:** the free `https://klaboukes.github.io/` for now (owner, 2026-10-27), from a public repo `Klaboukes/klaboukes.github.io` beside `Klaboukes/twangle-privacy`, whose address stays as it is; a name of Twangle's own comes later (Phase 56), so the pages link to each other with relative links and moving is only a setting
- ☑ **Rough mock-up first:** [docs/design/website-preview.html](docs/design/website-preview.html), the home page, the tuner's page and help at phone and desktop width; the owner kept its direction as it is (2026-10-27): the home page's headline, Play button and four promises first, then the instruments and the tools in three groups (tuning, listening, practice); each tool page with steps, short sections and a fold-out *For the curious* part; help as questions that fold open, grouped by topic, the contact email at the end. Its pages and `site.css` are where the site's own start
- ☑ **The frame:** the source in `website/` in this repo (Markdown pages, one small stylesheet, the fonts, images), so a change to a tool updates its page in the same pull request (a line saying so in CLAUDE.md's *Conventions*); `scripts/publish-website.sh` copies the built site to the public repo and commits and pushes only when it changed, with GitHub's no-reply email, as `scripts/publish-privacy.sh` does; a check in that script that no page loads anything from another host (only plain links leave the site). The privacy policy pages join the site or stay at their address, the owner's choice from the first item, without the address Play and the app use changing. A short `docs/architecture/website.md` and its line in ARCHITECTURE.md's *Contents*. Done 2026-10-27: plain HTML pages in `website/pages/<en|nl>/` wrapped in a shared frame by `website/build.py` (Python standard library, no Markdown converter needed), the device's own font as the mock-up chose; the check also refuses any `<script>` and any link inside the site that leads nowhere; the privacy policy stays at its own address, linked as `twangle-privacy/`; live at https://klaboukes.github.io/
- ☑ **Home page:** what Twangle does in a few calm lines, the promise (every feature free, no ads, no account, no internet permission, so nothing leaves the phone), a few screenshots from `scripts/store-screenshots.sh`, Google's *Get it on Google Play* badge (kept on the site, by Google's badge rules), and the instruments it tunes. Done 2026-10-27 in English and Dutch, from the mock-up: five screenshots as 540 px JPEGs (`scripts/website-pictures.sh`), Google's English and Dutch badges, the tool cards plain boxes until their pages come
- ☑ **How it works, one page per tool:** the tuner (strings and chromatic, cents, A4, the reference tones), the strum check, the 12th-fret check, the chord name, the pitch trail, the metronome (tap tempo, meters, the clicks), Chords, Scales with its drone, Capo, the instruments and tunings (the 12-string's courses, the banjo's short string), left-handed charts, the widget; each in plain words for players, with a short *For the curious* part where it helps (how a pitch is found, after de Cheveigné & Kawahara's YIN; why Twangle listens to the unprocessed microphone). Pictures from the app, no stock photos. Done 2026-10-27 in English and Dutch: a page for each of the twelve home-page cards (tuning, listening, practice, and *Instruments and more*: instruments and tunings, left-handed, the widget), each linking to the one before and after it; Capo's picture (store screenshot 7b) joined the site, and the site's pictures were made again from the current store screenshots
- ☑ **Help:** short answers to what players run into: the microphone refused and how to allow it again, a needle that wanders in a noisy room, a low string shown an octave off, the phone's battery saver stopping the metronome, how to reset Twangle; then the questions the closed testers asked (Phase 53). It grows from the weekly reviews (Phase 56), each new answer in English and Dutch. Done 2026-10-27 in English and Dutch (`help.html`), from the mock-up: twelve questions in four groups (the microphone, tuning, the metronome, the app), each answer true to the app's code, the ones above plus the mock-up's (another app using the microphone, recording, the wrong string, 442 Hz, the screen turning off, free) and Android's microphone-access switch; each answer can be linked to (`help.html#octave`); Help shows in the top bar and footer. No questions from the closed testers have come in yet: add them when they do
- ☑ **The privacy policy names the site:** [PRIVACY.md](PRIVACY.md) and its Dutch copy say the website collects nothing either, and that GitHub, which hosts it, sees visitors' addresses as any web host does (a link to GitHub's own statement); published with `scripts/publish-privacy.sh`. Done 2026-10-27, effective date 27 October 2026, address unchanged. The policy's own page loaded a script from cdnjs.cloudflare.com (the GitHub Pages theme's frame), so it now has a frame of its own without it (`website/privacy-layout.html`, copied by the publish script)
- ☑ **Linked from everywhere:** the Play listing's website becomes the home page (`contact-website.txt` in `app/build.gradle.kts`, sent with `scripts/release.sh listing` after **the owner**'s yes; the privacy-policy address in Play's *App content* stays); in the app one calm row in About, *Help & website*, opening the browser (the merged manifest still without `INTERNET`), in every language, with its UI test; README.md links it *Done 2026-10-27 (PR #426): in About, *Help & website* ("How each tool works, and answers to common questions") above *Rate Twangle* opens https://klaboukes.github.io/ in the phone's browser, the Dutch home page `nl/` when the app is in Dutch and the English one in the 14 languages the site doesn't have yet (their summary says "in English"); a plain `ACTION_VIEW` intent, the merged manifest still without `INTERNET`; the settings row to About says "help" too; `SettingsContentTest` (English, Dutch, German), `WebsiteIntentTest`, `WebsiteTest`. The Play listing's website is the home page (`contact-website.txt`), to be sent with `scripts/release.sh listing` (the owner's yes, 2026-10-27) from a machine that has the Play key: the desktop has none, so it waits (ROADMAP.md, *Waiting on the owner*); the privacy-policy address in *App content* is unchanged. README.md links the site and its help page.*

## Phase 55 — Ear training (owner, 2026-10-26; a tool, not a game, 2026-10-28)
Goal: one Practice tool, *Ear training*, with three exercises: **Play it back** (Twangle plays a note, the player finds it on the instrument, and the tuner hears whether it is right), **Intervals** and **Chord type** (answered with buttons). Play it back is what only Twangle can do among the free tuners, because it already hears. Intervals and chord types are what guitar teachers teach first, and they work away from the instrument, in headphones; with them, a teacher can recommend Twangle as a beginner's whole practice kit, free and without ads (Phase 56's emails to teachers). Against bloat: one tool with three exercises, nothing added to the navigation, and no fourth exercise (a scale by ear stays in [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md)). **A tool, not a game** (owner, 2026-10-28), in every exercise: no scores kept, no rounds, no levels, streaks or reminders. Questions keep coming until the player leaves; right and wrong are shown as gently as the tuner's in-tune effect; a quiet tally counts this visit only ("7 of 9") and is gone, never saved, when the tool closes; a question missed comes back a few questions later in the same visit. The player chooses what is asked (which notes, which intervals, which chord types), so a teacher can say "fourths and fifths this week"; Twangle never sets a level or says one is passed. No account, nothing leaves the phone (hard rules 7 and 9). It needs the microphone, which `:feature:practice` doesn't use by design, so it is a module of its own that holds the mic only while it is shown (hard rule 4). Built behind its own switch in `ToolSwitches`, hidden in release builds until the owner's check passes (the music tools' rule). Visible changes land with their UI tests, every language and every style. In order:
- ☑ **Rough mock-up first** (Contemporary), for **the owner** to pick: picking one of the three exercises, one question of each (the ▶ to hear it again; "Play it on your instrument" with the note the tuner hears, or the answer buttons), right and wrong, the tally for this visit, a missed question coming back, and where the player picks what is asked (e.g. the open strings, the natural notes up to the 5th fret or every note up to the 12th; which intervals; which chord types) (docs/design/1.x/ear-training-preview.html). **Owner picked (2026-10-28): A for all five:** the three exercises as three icons in a row; Play it back shows the note the tuner hears as you play; after an answer the next question comes by itself after about 1½ s; a missed question comes back with no mark; questions start at once, and a button opens the choices any time
- ☑ **The questions in `:core:dsp`:** pure and seeded, so tests can repeat a visit: a note from the player's choice within the instrument's range for its tuning, sequences of two to four notes, an interval from the player's choice (minor second to octave, up, down or together), a chord type from the player's choice of the Chords tool's twenty (sixteen when this was planned, twenty since Phase 49); never the same question twice in a row; a missed question asked again a few questions later; and the answer checks (the same note in any octave or the exact one, by the tuner's own note maths). `EarTrainingTest` (package `eartraining`; [notes-and-audio.md](docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md), *Ear training questions*)
- ☑ **The tool and its module:** `:feature:eartraining` (the CLAUDE.md modules table and ARCHITECTURE.md updated), `PracticeTool.EAR_TRAINING` in Phase 47's tool list, the microphone asked for as the tuner does and held only while the tool is shown (`WhileSubscribed`, the tuner's pipeline reused, `UNPROCESSED` as hard rule 5 says). The question's sound plays in the instrument's voice through `TonePlayer`, and listening waits until it has faded, so the speaker's sound is never taken for the answer. Tests with a fake recorder and a fake player (`EarTrainingViewModelTest`, `EarTrainingScreenTest`; on in debug builds only, `toolSwitchesFor`; [ui-other-screens.md](docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md), *Practice tab*, **Ear training**)
- ☑ **Play it back:** Twangle plays a note, the player finds it on the instrument and holds it; the tuner says right once the note is steady (the strings mode's lock rules, no flicker), or plays the question again; the tally for this visit and the missed notes coming back; then two to four notes in a row. The exercise used last and the player's choices of what is asked are kept in `SettingsRepository` like the other Practice settings (settings, not scores), and [PRIVACY.md](PRIVACY.md) and its Dutch copy add them to the *Practice* line. **Built (2026-10-28):** right once the note is held 0.3 s (the in-tune hold), a wrong note counted only after 1 s held ("Not that one", the player plays on until it is found, and it counts as missed and comes back); a dot per note for notes in a row; "Notes in a row" 1–4 and *Any octave counts* in the choices panel, as the mock-up
- ☑ **Intervals:** two notes in the instrument's voice, the player picks the interval from buttons (steady sizes, the names in every language, TalkBack); the player's choice of intervals and of up, down or together; no microphone needed, so it works without the instrument. **Built (2026-10-28):** up or down 0.7 s apart, together through `TonePlayer.playTogether` (`StringMix`, a synth per note at 1/n, ready for Chord type's strum); a button per interval chosen, two to a row, the sign large and the name small; right glows softly, a wrong pick fades and the next question waits 2½ s (1½ s after a right one); twelve chips and Up · Down · Together in *What is asked*, saved (`earIntervals`, `earDirections`), never none; the six of the mock-up (m3, M3, P4, P5, M6, P8) and up on a fresh install
- ☑ **Chord type:** a chord strummed in the string voice from `ChordShapeFinder`'s shapes for the tuning picked; the player picks its type from buttons, from a few (major, minor, 7) up to all twenty, as the player chooses. **Built (2026-10-28):** a new root each question, low string to high 40 ms apart, a 12-string's courses as pairs; a button per type chosen in the Chords panel's order, up to three in a column and more two to a row, the type large ("Major", "m7", "7♯9") with its symbol on C small, TalkBack the words; with many chosen they scroll in their own box under the question, which stays put; twenty chips in *What is asked*, saved (`earChordTypes`), major, minor and 7 on a fresh install; on the banjo the Chords tool's quiet line in its place. A strum now sounds as loud as one note (`StringMix` at 1/√n with a soft limiter; at 1/n a six-string strum was 8 dB quieter)
- ☑ **Owner check** added under *Owner checks*: with a real guitar, a few minutes of each exercise from the speaker and in headphones: Play it back hears right and wrong notes reliably and never takes the question for the answer, the sounds are clear, the buttons are easy to tell apart, and nothing flickers
- ☑ **Live:** after the owner's check, the switch on in release builds too. Done 2026-10-28, the owner's check passed ("Sounds OK"): `toolSwitchesFor` gives every build `ToolSwitches.ALL_ON` (`BuildToolSwitchesTest`), and the website has an Ear training page in English and Dutch

## Phase 46 — Stiff strings: the tuner's last few cents (the accuracy review of 2026-10-24)
Goal: the tuner and the 12th-fret check read a real, stiff string as well as they read a synthetic one. A real string is stiff, so its overtones sit a little sharp of exact multiples of the fundamental (inharmonicity, Fletcher 1964: partial k at `k·f₀·√(1 + B·k²)`), and YIN, which uses the whole waveform, is pulled sharp by them. Modelled on 2026-10-24 with `StrumChords.stiffStrum` and the stiffness the strum tests already assume (B = 1.5·10⁻⁴ for the low E, down to 2·10⁻⁵ for the high E), what the needle shows after a hard pick attack on the low E: **+8.5 cents at 0.25 s, +3.6 at 0.5 s, +1.0 at 1 s, +0.5 at 1.5 s**, sliding down as the overtones die away (the same pluck without stiffness: −0.2 to 0.0; a finger pluck, partials at 1/k², stays under 0.5 throughout). A note at the 12th fret has half the string's length and so four times its B, so the 12th-fret check, which takes 0.25–1.25 s of each note, would report a well set-up guitar played with a pick as **+4.3 cents on the low E** ("move the saddle back 1.5 mm"), +3.2 on the A and +3.1 on the G, all past its 3-cent tolerance; with a finger, under 1 cent on every string. The strum check and the chord name are tested with stiff strings (`StrumAccuracyReport`, `ChordAccuracyReport`); the tuner isn't. The owner's live checks passed, so this matters most for players with a pick and for the Precise setting (2 cents). Everything here is `:core:dsp` (hard rules 1, 2 and 6); nothing changes on screen. In order:
- ☑ **Stiff-string tests first:** `EdgeCaseTest` cases from `StrumChords.stiffStrum`'s model for every string of the standard tuning at the strum tests' B values and at three times them, plucked with a pick (partials at 1/k) and a finger (1/k²): the shown reading 0.5 s and 1 s after the pluck within 1 cent of the fundamental (`f₀·√(1 + B)`, as `StrumDetectorTest` defines it); and `IntonationTest` with the fretted note at 4× the open string's B: `FINE` on every string, the offset within the 3-cent tolerance. They defined done for the next item. Done 2026-10-28, landed with the next item: before it, the low E picked hard read +3.5 at 0.5 s (+7.6 at three times the stiffness), and the 12th-fret check gave it +3.4, "move the saddle back"
- ☑ **The refinement on the lowest partials:** after YIN has chosen the period (its threshold, its whole-period checks and its above-range check stay exactly as they are, so the octave decisions don't change), the sub-sample refinement (step 5) runs again on the frame limited to the lowest three or four partials of that note: a low-pass at about 3.5 × f₀ (a Butterworth's start-up transient is long at such a cutoff, so likely through the frequency domain instead, since `FftDifference` already holds the frame's spectrum: the band-limited cross-correlation's peak near τ, refined with the same cubic fit). Measured in this review: limiting E4 to three partials took its pick-attack bias from +2.5 to +0.5 cents at B = 1.5·10⁻⁴, with the 20 dB SNR precision still under 0.5 cent. Allocation-free; every existing accuracy test still within its limit (sines ≈ 0.1 cent, 20 dB SNR under 1, 10 dB under 5, no octave errors, every sample rate, the low range); the stiff-string tests pass; `YinBenchmark` and `scripts/dsp-phone-benchmark.sh` for the cost per frame against [docs/architecture/performance.md](docs/architecture/performance.md); the YIN section of [docs/architecture/signal-pipeline.md](docs/architecture/signal-pipeline.md) and the Intonation section of [docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md](docs/architecture/notes-and-audio.md) say how the refinement works and lose their stiff-string known limits. Done 2026-10-28: gains 1 up to 2·f₀, 0 from 3.5·f₀, applied to the spectra `FftDifference` keeps (the window band-limited, then tapered smoothly, so neither the filter nor the window's ends move a partial), at the ~100 lags around the dip. It counts in full once the note rings on, less while the window holds the attack, and not where the band has no clean dip of its own (a chord, mains hum under a low string). A hard pick on the low E now reads +0.2 cents at 0.25, 0.5 and 1 s, at three times the stiffness +0.6, every string within 0.7. The 12th-fret check reads at most +1.0 picked (the low E) and 0.5 with a finger. Sines stay within 0.13 cents and 20 dB SNR within 0.65 (single frames, 62–1500 Hz). Cost: Xiaomi 12X chain 0.33 → 0.37 ms a frame, low range 0.76 → 0.83. The drone tests now measure the drone with the whole waveform (`refineOnLowestPartials = false`), because a few partials of the harmonium's two reeds follow their beating and read about 1 cent flat
- ☑ **Real recordings in the tests:** every accuracy figure so far rests on synthetic sound (`core/dsp/src/test/resources/fixtures/` is empty). First a recorder for the test phone that is not Twangle (an instrumented test in `:core:testing` run by `scripts/record-plucks.sh`, writing 16-bit mono WAV files to the phone's storage and pulling them with adb), so hard rule 7 stands untouched. Then **the owner** (Claude says when the script is ready) plays each open string of a real guitar a few times in front of the Xiaomi, hard with a pick and softly with a finger, and in the same session a few plucks in a noisy room (talk, a TV on) for the next item, and the files go into the fixtures folder (a few MB, in git). A test runs them through `TunerChain`: the right note from the first shown frame, no octave flips, and the reading 0.5 s after the pluck within 2 cents of the reading 2 s in (the settled fundamental); `RealPluckReport` (print-only, with the benchmarks) prints each pluck's trajectory for the docs. The *Accuracy target* line of the signal-pipeline doc gets the measured figure in place of the ±3 cents target, and the pick and finger partial models in the tests are checked against what the recordings show. **Recorder ready (2026-10-28):** `scripts/record-plucks.sh` (`PluckRecorder` in `:core:testing`'s on-device tests, through the tuner's own recorder; [testing.md](docs/architecture/testing.md), *Real recordings*), checked end to end on the Xiaomi with `--test` (48 kHz, unprocessed). **Done 2026-10-29:** the owner's steel-string acoustic through the Xiaomi 12X, 30 takes (every open string twice with a pick, twice with a finger, once with a TV on across the room; ten retaken the same day), 12 MB in git. `RealPluckTest` (every quiet-room take through `TunerChain`): its own note from the first reading, 0.10–0.20 s after the pluck, and never another note; from 0.5 s on the reading only settles. The roadmap's 2 cents between 0.5 s and 2 s hold on the G and high E strings and the finger-plucked D; the low strings glide with the string itself (a hard pick on the low E +8.9 at 0.5 s, +6.9 at 1.5 s, +5.2 at 2 s; the whole waveform without the refinement +19 → +13; the picked A moves up to 6.5, the finger-plucked E and A 3–5), so the test holds the measured figure per string and the docs say why: a real string's upper partials start 6–10 cents sharp and settle 2–4 cents in the next second, and on this guitar's E, D and G partial 1 sits 3–6 cents under where partials 3 and up point. Two detector flaws found and fixed with their tests: the refinement's attack rule took a ringing string's beating level (its two planes of vibration, a window's first half up to 15 % quieter than its second) for the attack and handed a third of a finger-plucked low E's frames back to the whole waveform, 2–4 cents sharp (now full from 80 % of the energy, none under 50 %; `EdgeCaseTest`'s beating stiff strings); and the picked high E, its 2nd partial 7 dB over its fundamental, jumped to E5 for half a second 2.2 s in as the fundamental sank into the room's noise (an octave now needs frames loud enough to start a reading; `PitchSmootherTest`). The partial models: the low strings' fundamentals are 10–20 dB weaker than 1/k or 1/k² through the phone (`EdgeCaseTest` gained the weak-fundamental partials), the real inharmonicity lies between the model's and three times it on every string (the plain B twice the model), so the constants stay. `RealPluckReport` prints it all (testing.md, *Real recordings*; signal-pipeline.md, *Accuracy target* and *Edge cases*)
- ☑ **Quick and steady on real strings** (from Phase 51, moved here on 2026-10-26: it uses the same recordings and the same session with the owner, and it answers the most common complaint about the big tuners, a needle slow to catch the note or stuck): from the recordings, measure for each pluck how long until the first note shows and until the needle settles, and that the noisy-room plucks read the right note without a wandering needle. A test holds the measured figures; where a figure is worse than the synthetic one, fix it in `:core:dsp` first (hard rules 1, 2, 6), without undoing the no-flicker rules (the steady hint, the sticky string, the in-tune hold), which are part of why Twangle feels calm. The figures go into the *Accuracy target* part of [docs/architecture/signal-pipeline.md](docs/architecture/signal-pipeline.md). **Where it stands (2026-10-29,** `RealPluckReport`**):** the quiet takes show within 0.10–0.20 s (`RealPluckTest` allows 0.25); the six noisy takes (a TV 20–25 dB under the pluck) show the right note for the first 1–1.3 s, then the G and B an octave down (G2, B2: the detector, not the smoother, at levels above the gate) or the TV's pitch (A2) for 9–16 frames, and nothing after (YIN finds no pitch once the string sinks toward the TV, the gate still open); the picked low E in the noisy room is shown in only 34 of 88 frames after 0.5 s **Done 2026-10-29:** measured on all 30 takes (`RealPluckReport`): the first reading shows 0.10–0.20 s after the pluck in the quiet room (the G, B and high E 0.10–0.16, the low E 0.10–0.15, the A 0.13–0.17, the D 0.16–0.20), 0.11–0.16 in the noisy room and 0.30 on the picked A there; the needle then settles (stays within 2 cents of where it ends up) from 0.13–0.24 s on a finger-plucked G or high E, 0.4 s on a picked G or high E, 0.4–0.65 on the B, 0.9 on a picked D and 1.0–1.6 s on the A and low E, which glide with the string itself; `RealPluckTest` holds every figure with a margin. The G2, B2 and A2 the noisy takes showed were the detector reading a whole number of the string's periods as the string sank to within 8 dB of the TV (above the gate, for 7–16 frames, with its level flat or falling), and the smoother's to fix: a reading a whole number of periods away (an octave or a twelfth, up or down) is now followed after 3 frames only when it comes with an attack (a rise of 10 dB over the quietest of the last 10 frames, which every pluck makes and a TV's talk doesn't), otherwise only once it has lasted a second, and what is left of a lost note can't start a reading at a multiple of its period for a second either (`PitchSmoother`; `EdgeCaseTest` reproduces it with plucks sinking into a speech-band murmur, where the old rule showed another note on every string and the new one none). Now every noisy take shows its own note from the first reading to the end and then goes blank, 1.1–1.7 s in; the low E's "flicker" was a misreading of the first report, it shows without a break until it is lost at 1.2 s. The detector is untouched and the quiet takes read as before; the smoother's cost is a ring of ten levels ([signal-pipeline.md](docs/architecture/signal-pipeline.md), *Accuracy target* and *Smoothing*)
- ☑ **Mains hum notch for the low range** (verified for real only with the bass from *Owner checks*, Phase 15; until then synthetic): in `PitchRange.LOW` alone, where 50 and 60 Hz are inside the range (today hum 10 dB below a B0 string pulls it up to 8 cents, 2–3 cents from E1 up, and hum alone shows as G1 or B♭1), narrow notches at 50 and 60 Hz before YIN, and at their 2nd and 3rd harmonics only where the measurements show they are needed, each so narrow that the nearest notes (G1 49.0 Hz, B1 61.7, G2 98.0, B2 123.5) stay within 0.1 cent and a semitone-flat string still reads. `LowPitchRangeTest`: hum 6 dB below every low string within 1 cent, hum alone nothing, every existing low-range figure kept; the *Low notes* and *Edge cases* sections of the signal-pipeline doc updated; the guitar's standard range is left as it is (60 Hz already lies below it). Done 2026-10-28: adaptive notches (Widrow's canceller, 1 Hz wide) at 50, 60 Hz and their harmonics up to the 6th (with three, the test hum's 4th–6th harmonics still took strings an octave off), which learn only between notes and hold their estimate while a note sounds, since fixed notches narrow enough for G1 rang on a pluck beside them and moved G1, B1, G2 and B2 by 3 to 20 cents in a quiet room; they follow mains up to ±0.25 Hz off nominal, and a pitch within 0.5 Hz of 50 or 60 Hz is no note. Hum 6 dB under every low string, in tune or a semitone flat: within 1 cent from the first frame (before: up to two octaves off or nothing); hum alone: nothing, from the moment the mic opens; quiet room: G1, B♭1, B1, G2, B2 within 0.011 cents of before; a note already ringing as the low range opens reads as before too (only estimates that turn like hum are taken out). Cost on the Xiaomi 12X: low range 0.83 → 0.91 ms a frame, the standard range untouched. Known limit: it needs to hear the hum for a second or two before the note
- ☑ **Owner check added** under *Owner checks* (Phase 46, stiff strings)

## Phase 50 — Getting found, before launch (the growth plan of 2026-10-25)
Goal: guitarists who already have a tuner find Twangle and switch to it. Nearly everyone finds a tuner by searching Play for "guitar tuner", and nobody looks for a new one while the old one works, so the plan is to rank in Play search (by rating, number of reviews, installs and how many keep the app) and to be there when players are fed up with ads, paid tunings, subscriptions and needing the internet (the most common complaints in the big tuners' reviews, comparison of 2026-10-25). The message everywhere: every feature free, no ads, works offline, nothing leaves the phone. No paid advertising (it never pays back, and doesn't fit *free forever*), no buying or swapping reviews and no rewards for a rating (Google removes apps for that), no other app named in the listing or criticised by name in posts. Nothing in the app measures anything (hard rule 9): Play Console's own reports (installs and uninstalls per country, the search terms that brought visitors, store-listing conversion, ratings) and `scripts/release.sh health` and `reviews` are enough. Claude writes the texts, in every language where it helps; **the owner** posts and sends them under their own name, and does the Play Console steps. This phase is the part that must be ready before launch (owner, 2026-10-25: settled by the time v1.0 goes to production); the rest follows launch in Phase 56. In order:
- ☑ **Christmas in mind:** many guitars are gifts, so late December into January is very likely the busiest time for tuner searches (Claude checks it in Google Trends for a few languages and notes the figures in [docs/store-listing.md](docs/store-listing.md)). Aim: Twangle in production by early December, with a few hundred ratings by Christmas; if Phase 46 or other open work puts that date at risk, Claude says so, so the owner can send the version already on Play to production first. Done 2026-10-28: the busiest week of the year is Christmas week in nearly every country checked, one sharp week (24–28 December, Christmas Day itself 2–3× an ordinary day), not January; production around mid-November fits, and 1.0.2 can go first if 1.1.0 runs late ([store listing](docs/store-listing.md), *Timing: Christmas*)
- ☑ **Ratings at a good moment, the owner's choice:** today a calm "Rate Twangle" row in About opens the Play page, and the store-listing plan (Phase 38) said "no pop-up asking for a rating". Ratings weigh most in Play search, so Claude makes a rough mock-up of the alternative for the owner: Google's own rating window (Play's In-App Review), shown at most once, right after every string of the tuning has been in tune in one go (never while tuning, never on the first day), with no question before it (Google forbids filtering out the unhappy). Only if the owner picks it: first check that Google's library adds no permission (the merged manifest: no `INTERNET`, hard rule 7) and sends nothing of Twangle's own; the version in `gradle/libs.versions.toml`; behind an interface with a fake for tests (`TunerViewModel` tests for when it may show); [PRIVACY.md](PRIVACY.md) and its Dutch copy say the window is Google's; the Ratings line of [docs/store-listing.md](docs/store-listing.md) updated. Either way, the closed testers are asked to rate Twangle once it is public. Done 2026-10-28: the owner picked Google's window (B of the rough mock-up `docs/design/rating-moment.html`); the About row stays. The library adds no permission (merged manifest: only Play's hidden helper activity) and Twangle hands it nothing; it shows at most once, after 2 s of quiet once every string was in tune, past the first day; a debug install from the PC never shows it, so the first real sight of it is on a Play track ([lifecycle](docs/architecture/lifecycle.md), *Google's rating window*)
- ☑ **The store listing leads with what sets Twangle apart:** the first screenshot shows the tuner alone, calm (one string, in tune, no tools open), and the short description and the first lines of the long one say: free, no ads, no account, no internet permission, so nothing leaves the phone. In every language ([docs/store-listing.md](docs/store-listing.md), `scripts/store-screenshots.sh`); sent with `scripts/release.sh listing` after **the owner**'s yes Texts done 2026-10-29 (PR #462): every language's short description says what it is and "no ads, no account, offline" ("free" left out, as Google's store-listing guidance asks for that field), the full description's first paragraph says every feature free, no ads, no account, no purchases, no tracking and no internet permission; screenshot 1 already shows the tuner alone and calm ([store listing](docs/store-listing.md), *Title and texts*). Sending waits for the owner's yes. Store screenshots of all 27 folders made on the desktop 2026-10-29 (two folders needed a second run for the TalkBack picture: the paced reading came after the test's 3 s once, so the test now waits up to 5 s for it); the Play key is on the laptop only (*Waiting on the owner*). Sent 2026-10-29 from the laptop after the owner's yes (`scripts/release.sh listing`, mark `listing/2026-10-29-2138`): Play has all 27 store folders now, each with the new texts and a fresh set of eight screenshots made on the Xiaomi that evening; no tablet set yet (the laptop never made one, and the send then stopped without a word, fixed in PR #485)
- ☑ **Translations at night, English and Dutch by day** (owner, 2026-10-28): at 25 languages every daily text in every language would slow daily work, so a change writes its texts in English and Dutch only, each new English text with a short translator note; the overnight run's first step (`/translate`) translates the rest in parallel batches and records which English each translation was made from (`translations/`), so a reworded text is redone too; a release refuses to build until every language is complete and up to date (`LocalizationTest` in strict mode, in the release skill and `scripts/release.sh bundle`). Before the new languages below ([languages](docs/architecture/languages.md), *Day and night*)
- ☑ **A meaning review of every translation** (owner, 2026-10-28: "I want to be sure about this. Poor translations can set new users off"): the record of the item above starts from today's texts as they are, and no check so far judges whether a translation means the right thing where it shows (a look through git history on 2026-10-28 found no translation left behind by an English rewording; the tests check only presence, placeholders and fit). So, once, for all 553 texts in the 15 translated languages: first Claude writes the translator note (what it means, where it shows) above every English text that lacks one, from the code that shows it, in one pass of its own; then one helper per language, all at once, reads each translation against its English, the note, the Dutch, the language's register line in [languages](docs/architecture/languages.md) and the [glossary](docs/architecture/glossary.md), and fixes wrong meanings, the wrong register, stiff or literal wording, and the same term said two ways, keeping texts as short as the English. About 1–2 hours, no phone, nothing needed from the owner; one PR listing per language what changed and the choices a native speaker should look at; the next overnight run checks that the changed texts fit. Each language the next item adds gets the same review before it lands. Translator notes done (PR #463, 367 added; all 553 texts have one). Review done 2026-10-29: 298 texts changed over the 15 languages (nl 3, de 16, fr 34, es 12, it 18, pt-rBR 13, zh-rCN 37, ja 22, ko 21, hi 37, in 16, ru 18, tr 15, pl 20, ar 16), the store listing and glossary following the renamed terms; findings and the choices for a native speaker in [docs/translation-review-2026-10-29.md](docs/translation-review-2026-10-29.md); fit in the night languages checked by the next overnight run
- ☑ **More languages where nearly every phone is an Android and many people play guitar** (owner, 2026-10-28, after the user base analysis): the 16 languages already cover Hindi, Indonesian, Brazilian Portuguese, Turkish and Arabic. Next, one `/add-language` each and in this order: **Vietnamese** and **Thai** (big guitar and ukulele cultures, mostly Android phones, no close language in the app yet), then **Traditional Chinese** (Taiwan and Hong Kong, where phones don't fall back to the Simplified texts and show English today; starting from the Simplified texts, with the words that differ checked; a qualifier Hong Kong phones pick up too, e.g. `b+zh+Hant`), then **Bengali** (Bangladesh and West Bengal, almost all Android) and **Ukrainian**, then **Swedish**, **Norwegian** (Bokmål, `nb`), **Danish** and **Finnish** (owner, 2026-10-28: people the owner knows play there; Polish is already in). Each comes with its store listing, phone check, store screenshots and the meaning review of the item above (by a helper other than the one that translated it), and gets every release's "What's new" from then on. Left out on purpose: Filipino, Urdu and Malay (many phones there are set to English, and Indonesian is close to Malay) and Persian (Google Play doesn't serve Iran). **Spanish for Latin America:** the app's Spanish texts already show on every Spanish phone, but the store listing is only `es-ES`; Claude checks which page Play shows a Latin American phone (Spanish or English), and if it is English, adds an `es-419` listing (the Spanish one with Latin American words where they differ, e.g. *celular*), with `StoreListingTest` allowing that one extra folder. Christmas comes first: if a language would push production past early December, the ones not yet done go out in the first update after launch. Each language adds a few minutes to the overnight run's language part and to `scripts/store-screenshots.sh`. Vietnamese landed (PR #468). Thai landed (PR #469). Traditional Chinese landed (PR #470). Bengali landed (PR #471). Ukrainian landed (PR #472). Swedish, Norwegian and Danish landed (PR #473). Finnish landed (PR #474). Done 2026-10-29: the nine languages, Vietnamese (#468), Thai (#469), Traditional Chinese (#470, store folders `zh-TW` and `zh-HK`), Bengali (#471), Ukrainian (#472), Swedish, Norwegian Bokmål and Danish (#473) and Finnish (#474), so `twangle.languages` has 25; and **Spanish for Latin America**: Google doesn't document whether a Latin American phone gets the `es-ES` listing or the default English one, so the listing has `es-419` too (the Spanish texts with *celular*, *en vivo*, *Mantén presionada*; `StoreListingTest`'s `PLAY_FOLDERS`; its screenshots are made like any language's, the app showing its Spanish for `es-419`). Every full description now says 25 languages and lists them in its own words, as do README, the website's home page and the store-listing plan. Known gap: a phone set only to plain Norwegian (`no`) or Nynorsk (`nn`) gets English, since Android has no alias to `nb` ([languages](docs/architecture/languages.md)). The new languages' and `es-419`'s store screenshots are made with `scripts/store-screenshots.sh` before the listing is sent (*Waiting on the owner*)
- ☑ **Ask Google to feature it:** **the owner** sends Play Console's featuring nomination at launch (Claude writes the text: free in every language Twangle speaks by then, no internet permission, TalkBack, the listening tools, the 12-string and the banjo from Phase 57), and again with each bigger update (the watch app). Text ready in [docs/growth/featuring-nomination.md](docs/growth/featuring-nomination.md) (2026-10-29): every field of Google's form (a Play Help contact form, checked that day), the pitch, and the watch update's answers. The form wants a new app sent at least 8 weeks before the date asked for, so Christmas week (24 December) meant sending by 29 October; otherwise the owner sends it with the production rollout (Phase 53), for a date 8 weeks on

## Phase 60 — Meters that read to the cent (owner, 2026-10-29)
Goal: every meter look reads nearly as precisely as Classic, which the owner keeps going back to. Spirit level and Eclipse go (the owner's least favourite, and the least precise); **Pointers** and **Loupe** take their places; Light arc, Arrows and Light bars get sharper. Picked by the owner from two rounds of the moving mock-up [docs/design/1.x/meter-precision-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/meter-precision-preview.html) (its top comment has the numbers; turned down: *Horizon*, a tilting line, because which way to tune wasn't clear, and *Lift*, an up-and-down look). **Can be built before launch: `/roadmap-step 60`** (owner, 2026-10-29: "I will let my desktop build this").
Why Classic reads best, and so the rules for the others: its needle tip moves ~4.3u per cent, it has a tick every 5 cents to compare against, and a thin needle meets a thin centre bar (the eye lines up two thin lines very precisely). The others move 1.8–2.4u per cent, step 6–10 cents at a time or show a ~10-cent-wide soft peak, with nothing fixed to compare against. So each new or sharpened look uses: **the stretched middle** (`meterStretch`, in `:core:designsystem` `meter/`: position = sign · 50 · (1 + k)|x| / (1 + k|x|), x = cents / 50, k = 1.5; 2.5× the movement per cent at the centre, 0.4× at ±50, 50 stays 50; never on Classic, whose numbers would go uneven), **a few fixed marks without numbers**, **thin edges instead of soft blobs**, and **the in-tune window** shown where the mock-up shows it: ±5 cents, ±2 with the Precise setting (`MeterFrame` hands the window to the look, read like the other `MeterReading` values). Everything else follows the meter art language ([ui-meter-and-styles.md](docs/architecture/ui-meter-and-styles.md)): roles only, the calm field, ♭ and ♯ inside the box, one soft in-tune glow, drawn once and moved by layers, nothing allocated per frame, a resting centre never in tune. Each increment updates that doc (and `history.md` for what goes), keeps `MeterLooksTest` passing in every style light and dark, runs `scripts/meter-frames.sh` for its look, and gets a phone check. In order:
- ☑ **Pointers instead of Spirit level:** a fixed pointer above the scale's middle (tip 7u above the track, 18u wide, 24u tall, in the centre colour), the note's pointer below it pointing up (same size, ink, with the marker shadow), sliding along a straight track (±150u at ±50, stretched: ~7.5u per cent near in tune), and a band of light along the track from the middle to the note's pointer (8u high, round ends, the accent from 8 % at the middle to 80 % dark / 65 % light at the pointer). Ticks above the track at 1, 2, 3, 4 cents (6u, ink 40 %), 5 and 10 (12u, ink 80 %), 20 to 50 (9u, ink 50 %). In tune the two tips meet; the glow and colours as the other looks. Feel 1.9 Hz / 0.8. It takes Spirit level's place in the picker, and a saved Spirit level opens as Pointers. Its name "Pointers" in English and Dutch ("Wijzers"), with a translator note; Spirit level's name goes from every language. JVM tests for the pointer's place and the band (`PointersTest`, replacing `SpiritLevelTest`)
- ☑ **Loupe instead of Eclipse:** a straight scale (3u per cent, ±150u, ticks every 5 cents as on the open face) with a slim marker (3u × 44u, ink); over its middle a round magnifying glass (radius 54u) lying on the screen (the art language's glass: contact shadow, 1u rim in ink at 14 %, one sheen from the top). Through the glass everything is magnified 3× at its centre, easing to 1× at its rim (x' = R · h(x / R), h as the stretch with k = 2), so nothing hides under it: a tick per cent where they are at least 4u apart, ticks and marker up to 1.8× as long, the centre mark in the centre colour, the in-tune window as a quiet band (track colour, fading toward the glass's top and bottom; never green at rest). Under the glass ~9u per cent. Feel 1.8 Hz / 0.8. It takes Eclipse's place in the picker, and a saved Eclipse opens as Loupe. Its name "Loupe" in English and Dutch ("Loep"), with a translator note; Eclipse's name goes from every language. JVM tests for the glass's mapping (continuous at the rim, 3× at the centre) and the marker's place (`LoupeTest`, replacing `EclipseTest`). Xiaomi 12X: 122 frames/s, 2 late of ~2,570, 3.61 ms main thread a frame (Classic 3.77)
- ☑ **Light arc, sharper:** the same 17 rays, evenly spaced, but in stretched cents, so the first ray lights at about 3 cents, then 6, 10, 14, 20, 27, 37 and 50; tiny ink dots just past the outer lights mark 5 cents (1.8u, 55 %) and 10 cents (1.3u, 32 %); the fading trail 0.12 s instead of 0.22. `LightArcHeatTest` and the ray tests follow. The dots sit on the rays nearest 5 and 10 cents, as on the mock-up; the in-tune window's edge gets a strong dot too, so with Precise the first ray is marked as well. Xiaomi 12X: 122 frames/s, 1 late of ~2,580, 3.74 ms main thread a frame (Classic 3.72)
- ☑ **Arrows, sharper:** the chevrons light at 2, 5, 10 and 20 cents, each filling over its own step (full at 5, 10, 20 and 40), instead of one per 10.5 cents; one lit arrow means "almost there". `ArrowsTest` follows. Xiaomi 12X: 122 frames/s, 0 late of ~2,580, 3.62 ms main thread a frame
- ☑ **Light bars, sharper, with a clear centre:** the 41 bars in stretched cents (one per cent near the middle); the peak narrower (σ 2.6 stretched units, 3.6 as the note is reached); the bars of the in-tune window a little taller (8u) and tinted 14 % toward ink, blending toward "in tune" as it is reached. The centre bar stands out: 5.6u wide, always taller than the peak ((16 + 64 · peak)u each way), 85 % of the way to the accent, with a 3.2u dot in the centre colour 89u above and below the middle; reaching the note, only the centre bar and its dots turn fully "in tune" (the lit bars beside it at most 35 %). `LightBarsTest` follows. Drawn at 91 % of the mock-up's heights, so the dots (just outside the box on the mock-up) stay inside it, where the Settings cards clip; the centre bar, its dots and the peak's light are muted at rest, as every look's centre; with Precise the window is the centre bar and one each side (the next stands for 2.1 cents). Xiaomi 12X: 122 frames/s, 0 late of ~2,580, 3.70 ms main thread a frame (Classic 3.77)
- ☑ **What shows the looks:** README.md's list of meter looks; any store screenshot, website picture or Settings card test that shows Spirit level or Eclipse by name (the store screenshots are retaken at the next overnight run, so only their scripts change). Only README named them: no script, website page, store text or test did, and every language's strings already lost the old names with their looks (#489, #490)

## Phase 59 — Chord charts and scales for the banjo (owner, 2026-10-27)
Goal: the 5-string banjo from Phase 57 gets the Practice tools the guitar has, so a beginner can tune and then learn the first chords (G, C, D7) without leaving Twangle. Today Chords, Scales and the Capo helper show a quiet line in their place for it (`ShortStringLine`, gated on `Tuning.hasShortString`), because the charts and the shape finder treat every string as starting at the nut, and on a guitar a string outside the chord is muted. The banjo's short fifth string starts at the 5th fret, and players let it ring as a drone. Visible changes land with their UI tests, every language where words change, both hands (Phase 57's left-handed switch), and the Practice doc. In order, Capo last: *Capo for the banjo went to [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md) when the phase closed (2026-10-30), until players ask: its short string needs a capo of its own, so the helper's sums differ; until then Capo keeps its quiet line for the banjo.*
- ☑ **Rough mock-up first** (Contemporary, one row per idea), for **the owner** to pick: a banjo chord chart (G, C, D7) with the short string drawn starting at the 5th fret, open as a drone or fretted above it; whether the open short string always rings, or only where its note belongs to the chord (and is otherwise fretted or marked unplayed); and the Scales neck with the short string (docs/design/banjo-charts-preview.html). **Owner picked (2026-10-30):** chart A (the short string starts at its own small nut at the 5th fret, the chart shows frets 1–5, the neck only four strings wide above it, the short string's o or × by its peg); drone B (the open short string rings only where its note is a chord note, else it is fretted above its start on a chord note within reach, else ×); Scales neck B (the short string drawn full length but faded or dashed below its start, no notes there); TalkBack A ("short G 4 string", "short F sharp 4 string" on double D); left-handed: both mirrored, no choice
- ☑ **The shape finder knows the short string:** in `:core:dsp`, a tuning's strings can start at a fret (the banjo's fifth at 5), so no shape frets that string below its start; the drone rule the owner picked decides when it rings open. `ChordShapeFinderTest`'s every-tuning checks stop leaving out `hasShortString` tunings (every shape sounds the chord on open G, double C and double D, within the hand's reach), plus banjo cases: the common first shapes come out first (G open, C, D7). Done: `Tuning.startFretOf` / `midiAt`, the drone rule B in the finder; the every-tuning checks cover the banjo (only chord notes, every chord note, within reach, the short string never fretted at or below the 5th, open exactly where its note is in the chord, × only where no chord note on it is in reach); first shapes G 00000, C 02012, D7 x0214 and nine more as banjo books show them (Em 02002, Am x2212, F x3213 …); two 9ths are out of reach on open G (B♭m9, D♭maj9)
- ☑ **Chord charts for the banjo:** `ChordChart` draws the short string from its own start fret (mirrored with the left-handed switch), Chords offers the banjo and its line goes. `ChordsScreenTest` with the banjo, both hands, TalkBack naming the short string; every language at 2× text. The owner's picks: chart A (the short string from its own small nut at the 5th fret; frets 1–5 shown so the start is in view; the neck four strings wide above it; its o or × by its peg), TalkBack A ("short G 4 string": its open note and the word "short"), both hands mirrored. Done: a banjo chart has five fret rows (`ChordShape.chartRowsFor`) and its strings run on past the last; the short string's own nut and peg sit on the 5th fret's line wherever that is in the chart (the last line from the nut, inside a chart from the 3rd to the 6th fret, above one from the 7th, where it runs the whole way, as under D's barre 77777), its o or × in the row above, by the peg; TalkBack "short G 4 string" (`spokenStringWords`, for Scales too); Chords' quiet line removed from every language, Ear training's banjo line now says "Chord type doesn't know the banjo's short string yet" (the night languages translate the new and reworded texts); checked on the Xiaomi with G, C, D7, D and D♭ in both hands. The chord's sound moved to its own item below: Chords plays no sound
- ☑ **Scales for the banjo:** `ScaleNeck` (upright and on its side) draws the short string from the 5th fret, with no notes below it; Scales offers the banjo and its line goes. `ScalesScreenTest` with the banjo, both hands. The owner's pick: neck B (the short string drawn full length, faded or dashed below its start, still no notes there), mirrored for the left hand. Done: `ScaleFinder.notesOf` gives a short string its open note, then only frets above its start, counted on the neck (G major on open G: g a b c d at 0, 7, 9, 10, 12); the neck draws it solid from the 5th fret and faded and dashed (45 %) from the nut to there, its open note's dot in the 5th fret's space; TalkBack "short G 4 string, open G, root, fret 7 A, …" ("short F sharp 4 string" on double D); Scales' quiet line removed from every language; checked on the Xiaomi upright and on its side in both hands, the drone playing on the banjo
- ☑ **The banjo chord's sound (the owner decides how):** this phase planned that "the chord's sound plays its five strings in the banjo voice", but Chords has no ▶ and plays nothing; the one chord strum in Practice is Ear training's Chord type, which still refuses the banjo (`ChordTypeQuestions`, `ChordsUnavailable.SHORT_STRING`). Either Chord type takes the banjo (its strum from the first shape, five strings in the banjo voice, the short string ringing by rule B), or Chords gets a ▶ of its own (a new control: a rough mock-up first), or this item goes. **Owner picked A (2026-10-30):** Chord type takes the banjo. Done: `ChordTypeQuestions` asks on every banjo tuning, each chord strummed from Chords' first shape in the banjo voice, the strings in the tuning's order, so the short string first where the drone rule lets it ring (G 00000 all five, C 02012, D7 x0214 four); a chord with no shape on its root (open G's B♭m9 and D♭maj9) is never asked, as on any tuning; Ear training's banjo line and `ChordsUnavailable.SHORT_STRING` removed from every language; checked on the Xiaomi, Play it back and Intervals on the banjo too

## Phase 62 — The metronome for practice: subdivisions and a speed trainer (owner, 2026-10-31)
Goal: the two things a practice metronome is expected to have and Twangle's lacks. Today it clicks once per beat; players keep strumming patterns and picking even, and feel triplets and swing, with softer clicks between the beats. And the usual way to learn a fast passage is to start slower and let the metronome speed up bit by bit. Both stay calm and plain: a tool, not a game, nothing scored or kept but the settings. Not tied to launch: `/roadmap-step 62` can run any time. **Switches:** subdivisions are an option of the metronome and need none; the speed trainer is something the player picks on its own, so it is a tool with its own switch in `ToolSwitches`, off in release builds until the owner's check passes. In order:
- ☑ **Where they go, a rough mock-up first** (Contemporary, one row per option, as `docs/design/1.x/more-tools-preview.html`): the metronome screen keeps its row of three buttons (Sound · Tap tempo · Listen, no fourth) and its dial, so the subdivision choice and the speed trainer must find room elsewhere, e.g. the subdivision beside the meter picker or in the Sound panel, and the trainer from a long press on Start or a panel of its own. **The owner** picks — *Round 1 ([docs/design/1.x/metronome-practice-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/metronome-practice-preview.html)) put both in the top row; the owner then asked for a calmer screen that groups all five (Sound, Tap tempo, Listen, subdivision, trainer) and brings Start and − + back under the dial. Round 2 ([docs/design/1.x/metronome-layout-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/metronome-layout-preview.html)): the owner picked **II** (2026-10-31): one row under the dial, Tap tempo · − · Start · + · options; the options panel holds the four sounds, the note values ("Between the beats"), Vibrate on the beat, Listen and the speed trainer (its switch, and its row opens the trainer's settings in place of the panel's contents with a ‹ back arrow, same size and spot; turning it on the first time goes straight there, filled in). What is on shows as small icons on the status line under the beat lights; while the trainer climbs it reads "↗ 95 → 120 · +5 in 2 bars", a small ring on the dial marks the goal, and tapping that line opens the trainer's settings. The top row keeps only the Practice tab's "Metronome ⌄"*
- ☑ **The new layout (II), nothing new in it yet:** the row under the dial (Tap tempo · − · Start · + · options) and the options panel with the sounds, Vibrate on the beat and Listen, moved from today's three big buttons and the top row's small ones; Listen's status line shows what is on; every control keeps its place, steady sizes at 2× text, on the phone and on the tablet; TalkBack names; `MetronomeScreenTest` and the other metronome UI tests follow the new places *Done: the dial holds only the tempo; the row's buttons 52, 48, 72, 48, 52 dp, icons only; the options panel (`OptionsPanel`, rows given as content: Sound, Vibrate on the beat, Listen) opens above its button; vibration shows as a mark on the status line (`StatusMark`), and listening has Stop on the line. The website's metronome page says where things are now (published with this phase's website item).*
- ☑ **Subdivisions in `:core:dsp`:** none (the default), 2 (eighths), 3 (triplets), 4 (sixteenths); in 6/8 and 7/8, where a click is already an eighth, only none and 2. Every click sound gets a fourth, softest accent for the clicks between beats, told apart from the plain beat by level and pitch. `ClickTrack` places them sample-exact by the same integer formula as the beats (no adding up of rounded intervals), and a tempo or meter change still takes effect from the next beat. At 250 BPM in sixteenths a click comes every 60 ms, shorter than today's 80–180 ms clicks, so the in-between click is short enough (or cuts the one before) to keep `ClickTrack`'s one voice; allocation-free. `ClickTrackTest` (every click of ten minutes on its ideal sample, each subdivision, odd tempos) and `ClickSoundsTest` (the new accent: no clipping, ends on 0, quieter than the plain beat). *Done: `Subdivision` (NONE, TWO, THREE, FOUR) and `ClickTrack.subdivision`, the softest click `BeatAccent.SUBDIVISION` (a fourth lower, 3.4–6.8 dB under a plain beat at the peak); a click the next catches up with fades out over 3 ms before it, so one voice stays. No change heard yet: nothing sets a subdivision.*
- ☑ **Subdivisions saved and shown** (the options panel's "Between the beats" row, its icon on the status line when not none): the choice in `SettingsRepository` (none when unset), handed to the player by `MetronomeViewModel`, heard from the next beat while playing; the beat lights and "Vibrate on the beat" stay on the beats, Listen and Tap tempo unchanged; TalkBack names each choice; steady sizes at 2× text; `MetronomeViewModelTest`, `MetronomeScreenTest` *Done: four note-value icons under the sounds (none, eighths, triplets, sixteenths), one size as the sounds'; a pick while stopped plays one bar, while playing it comes in on the next beat; in 6/8 and 7/8 two to a beat is drawn and named sixteenths and triplets and sixteenths are dimmed in place, the saved choice kept for the next meter that offers it (the one marked picked is what plays). Its note shows on the status line before vibration's. The website's metronome page describes it (published with this phase's website item).*
- ☑ **The speed trainer** (in the options panel as the owner picked: the switch, the settings in place with a back arrow, the status line and the goal ring on the dial; its `ToolSwitches` switch hides the row): a start tempo, a goal tempo and a step ("+5 BPM every 4 bars", the step and the number of bars each picked from a short list); the tempo rises on a bar's downbeat, the dial following, and holds at the goal. Changing the tempo by hand, Tap tempo or Listen ends it; Stop and Start begin again from the start tempo. Its settings saved; pure logic in `:core:dsp` with tests (each step on its downbeat, never past the goal), `MetronomeViewModelTest`, `MetronomeScreenTest` *Done: `SpeedTrainer` (start and goal on a 5 BPM grid, +1/+2/+5/+10 every 1/2/4/8 bars) climbed by `ClickTrack` itself on the audio thread, so each step is on its downbeat to the sample (the ViewModel only follows the beats); switch `ToolSwitches.speedTrainer`, on in debug builds and off in release builds (`toolSwitchesFor`) until the owner's check passes. Turned on the first time it opens its settings filled in from the tempo (to +20, +5 every 4 bars). The website's metronome page says it is coming.*
- ☑ Texts in English and Dutch (the night languages by `/translate`); the metronome part of [strum-and-metronome.md](docs/architecture/strum-and-metronome.md) and [ui-other-screens.md](docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md); the website's metronome page (`website/pages/en` and `nl`), then `scripts/publish-website.sh` after the merge *Done along the way in #516–#520 and checked complete: every new text in English and Dutch with a translator note (`LocalizationTest`), both docs describe layout II, the subdivisions and the trainer as built (the trainer's screen tests and the phone check added), the website page says where things are, the clicks between the beats and a "Coming soon" note for the trainer (off in release builds); published after the merge. The page's screenshot still shows the old layout until the next store-screenshot run.*
- ☑ Check on a test phone: each subdivision at slow and fast tempos in every meter, in the speaker and in headphones (no clicks lost, no crackle at 250 BPM in sixteenths); the trainer climbing to its goal; the metronome's UI tests *Done on the Xiaomi 12X (2026-10-31) through the real player at low volume (`MetronomeSubdivisionCheck`, on demand): every subdivision in every meter at 60 and 250 BPM, no beat missed and no underrun in any of the 40 runs; two minutes of sixteenths at 250 BPM (2,000 clicks), no underrun, beats within ±1.4 ms of 240 ms; the trainer 200 → 250 in sixteenths, each step on its downbeat and holding at 250; in the app the trainer climbed 95 → 125 to "At the goal" and Stop went back to 95; no crash. UI tests: `:feature:metronome` 46 tests, `:core:audio` 29, all passed. Headphones need the owner: moved into the owner check.*
- ☑ **The clicks between the beats in the beat lights, one shape in every style** (owner, 2026-10-31): a small dot for each click between the beats, lit as it sounds (PR #523); then every style's beat lights became today's Contemporary marks, a rounded bar per beat and the dot, the style changing only the colours and the number face (Phase 19's wave mark per style went to the history). *Pictured on the Xiaomi in all six styles, light and dark, with eighths, triplets, sixteenths and 7/8.*
- ☑ **Owner check** added under *Owner checks*: play along with eighths, triplets and sixteenths, and practise a passage with the speed trainer: easy to follow, the beat still stands out, nothing tiring after a few minutes *Added, with listening in headphones and no crackle at 250 BPM in sixteenths; once it passes the speed trainer goes on for release.*

## Phase 61 — The watch: quick to open, felt on the wrist, a finer look (owner, 2026-10-30)
Goal: the watch tuner stays a plain, useful tuner (owner, 2026-10-30: "useful features", not advanced ones; don't blow up the app), for the update that brings the watch app to Play (decision 3). A watch tuner is worth it only if it opens faster than finding the phone, and the watch can tap the wrist, which a phone can't. Picked from the mock-up [docs/design/watch-ideas-preview.html](docs/design/watch-ideas-preview.html) (cards 1 and 6); its other ideas are in [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md), *More for the watch*. Not tied to launch: `/roadmap-step 61` can run any time. The owner's real-watch check (*Owner checks*, Phase 20) comes first, as it may change or add items.
- ☑ **A finer look, a bigger marker:** the marker on the ring larger (today 2.6× the ring's line, about 14 dp across on the Watch 5), and a little more finish to the ring and the in-tune glow, in the same calm style and colours — the owner asked for mock-ups after all and picked C, a needle about 30 dp long pointing in from the ring over a tick every 5 cents with ♭ and ♯ at the scale's ends ([docs/design/1.x/watch-look-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/watch-look-preview.html)), and C1, the phone's ribbon wave small under the cents ([docs/design/1.x/watch-wave-preview.html](docs/design/1.x/watch-wave-preview.html)), which moves by the phone's own `WaveMotion` (moved to `:core:dsp` for it). Also the owner's find: the cents line no longer shifts sideways as the number changes (a fixed slot for the number). Checked live on the Galaxy Watch 5 with a guitar (A2 −13, G3 −2, E2 0: needle, pulses, still in tune) and by 8 UI tests on the emulator
- ☑ **A Twangle tile:** a Wear OS tile beside the watch face with Twangle's name and one Tune button that opens the tuner, in the watch's Contemporary colours. The tile never listens: the microphone starts only once the tuner is open (Hard rule 4) — the name, the mark in the accent and a Tune edge button; checked on the Wear OS emulator (no recording while the tile shows, one once the tuner opens; an open tuner comes to the front). Found on the way: the screen keeper listened to the tuner in the background, so the mic stayed open behind the watch face until Android silenced it and the tuner came back saying another app had the mic; fixed, and coming back after a failure now tries again by itself
- ☑ **A watch-face shortcut:** a complication (the small round slot a watch face offers) with Twangle's icon that opens the tuner with one tap — the mark with "Tune" in a short-text slot ("Twangle" was cut to "TWAN…"), the mark alone in an icon slot, the launcher icon in a small-image slot; checked on the emulator's Utility face
- ☑ **Feel it in tune:** one soft tap on the wrist the moment the note comes in tune, when the ring starts to glow; once per tuned note, again only after the note leaves tune or changes (as the phone's `InTuneHaptic`). The watch's microphone must not hear the motor: the reading stays steady across the tap (a JVM test on the pipeline, then the real watch). Whether the watch needs a way to turn it off is decided with the owner after trying it — built: the system's click at 0.6 strength (the plain click where the motor can't), by the phone's rule (`InTuneTickRule`, now in `:core:dsp`); the pipeline looks away for about 190 ms as the note comes in tune, so the buzz can't move the reading (JVM: a 150–250 Hz motor buzz mixed in at the tap, every string, up to twice the note's level: still in tune, within a cent); taps checked on the emulator, which has no motor. On the Galaxy Watch 5 with a guitar (2026-10-31) the system's click wasn't felt at all, nor an 80 ms pulse while playing, so the tap became one 150 ms pulse at full strength, the shortest the owner felt through the guitar's vibration (the look-away grew to about 320 ms, the JVM test's buzz with it); then one tap per tuned string. No off switch (owner, 2026-10-31)
- ☑ Check on the Galaxy Watch 5 with a real guitar (and the Wear OS emulator): the new look, the tile and the shortcut open the tuner, the tap comes once per tuned note; texts in English and Dutch (the night languages by `/translate`); the watch's UI tests; the watch part of [ui-other-screens.md](docs/architecture/ui-other-screens.md) brought up to date — the owner, 2026-10-31: the Twangle tile's Tune and the shortcut (on Info Modular; the owner's Simple Basic face has no slots for other apps) open the tuner, one tap per tuned string; the texts were translated (#512); the watch's 20 UI tests on the real watch: 18 pass, the 2 screenshot tests skip as meant (the 8 screen tests need the screen kept on for the run, as the watch sleeps after 15 s)

## Phase 63 — One look for the same control on every screen (the consistency review of 2026-10-31)
Goal: each screen looks right on its own, but the tools were built one at a time, and the same kind of control now looks or works differently from screen to screen. The review went through every tab, Practice tool, sheet and Settings page on the Xiaomi (Retro, light) and read the code. The fixes keep the owner's rules: buttons keep their place and size, no new hint text, calm. No new tool, so no new switch. Plan:
- ☑ **A rough mock-up first** (Contemporary, `docs/design/consistency-preview.html`, opened in Firefox). It shows, before and after, the choices below that change a look: how a menu marks the chosen tool, the options button and what it opens, one face for the chord, scale and key pickers, one look for step buttons, the rule for "pick one of a list", and Ear training's exercise switch. The owner picks; the picks go in [ui.md](docs/architecture/ui.md) under a new *Shared controls* section (one button, one stepper, one picker, one list choice, and when each is used) (2026-10-31: the owner skipped the mock-up, "no mockup, just build what you would recommend", and left the picks to Claude; they are in ui.md *Shared controls*, and the items below follow them)
- ☑ **One round button that follows the style:** the metronome's buttons and the instrument button take the style's shape (round in Contemporary and Space Age, the style's small shape elsewhere: square in Classic, eight-sided in Futuristic). Chords' ‹ › (`PagerButton`), Capo's ‹ › (`StepButton`) and Ear training's ▶ (`ReplayButton`) are always circles, each its own copy of the same code, so in Classic a square instrument button sits above round ‹ ›. One shared button in `:core:designsystem`, used by all of them; the metronome's `buttonLook()` moves there too. Checked in all six styles (2026-10-31: `StyleButton` in `:core:designsystem`; Ancient keeps the instrument button's pebble, and the tuner's more tools, strum check and focus mode buttons take the same shape, so the row above the strings stays one kind of button)
- ☑ **Both tool menus mark the chosen tool alike:** Practice's menu marks it with a tinted pill, the tuner's "more tools" menu with green text and a ✓. One way for both, as picked in the mock-up. On the tuner nothing names the tool that is on, only the ⊞+ button looks picked: TalkBack hears just "More tools", and the menu item doesn't tell TalkBack it is on (`checked=false` while the 12th-fret check was on). TalkBack hears the tool that is on in both places. The buttons stay where they are (2026-10-31: the tuner's menu rows as Practice's, the one that is on in the container colour with the 2 dp accent outline, no green, no ✓; TalkBack hears "More tools, 12th-fret check on" and each row as a switch, on or off, since picking the one that is on leaves none; the more tools, strum check and focus mode buttons are now the shared `StyleButton`, same 44 dp size and place)
- ☑ **One options button:** the same sliders icon is a framed button in the style's shape on the metronome, which opens a small floating panel, but a bare, small icon in Ear training's corner, which opens a sheet ("What is asked"). Ear training's gets the metronome's face, size and picked look while open; whether both open the same kind of panel is the mock-up's pick (2026-10-31: one `OptionsButton` in `:core:designsystem` for both; Ear training's keeps its sheet, its place and its TalkBack name, the ▶ under it unmoved)
- ☑ **One picker face for chord, scale and key:** Chords shows a wide box with a small "C ⌄" over "C major", Scales a wide box with a big bold "C major ⌄", Capo a "Shapes I play" caption over a small "C ⌄" field. One face for all three (the name's size, the ⌄, the box, the line under it), as picked in the mock-up (2026-10-31: one `NamePickerButton` in `:core:designsystem`, the full name in words on one line, semi-bold, "C major ⌄", one 56 dp height, set smaller where it doesn't fit; Chords now shows "A minor seventh ⌄" instead of "Am7" over the words; Capo's box as wide as its table with "Shapes I play" above it and its list in words; TalkBack names and what each opens unchanged)
- ☑ **One look for step buttons:** "one less / one more" comes in four looks: the metronome's − + in framed buttons, Chords' and Capo's ‹ › in circles, Settings' A4 − + with no frame, the tuning sheet's "Lower by" ⌃ ⌄ with no frame. One look (the shared button above), the arrows kept where they mean pages (‹ ›) and − + where they mean a number (2026-10-31: one `StepperButton` in `:core:designsystem`, 36 dp faces in 48 dp touch areas, for A4, "Lower by" (now − +, − on the left in every language) and the speed trainer; the metronome's tempo uses it on 48 dp faces; TalkBack names unchanged. The tuning editor's − + per string, filled tonal buttons, are left for the review below)
- ☑ **Pick one of a list, one rule:** Settings uses radio dots, the sheets (tuning, "What is asked") a tinted row with ✓, the metronome's options separate chips, and two-way choices are sometimes a joined bar (Strings/Chromatic, Ear training's exercise, Notes in a row) and sometimes separate chips (2/4…7/8, Sound, Between the beats). Apply the mock-up's rule where a screen differs (2026-10-31: one `ChoiceRow` in `:core:designsystem` for the tuning sheet, "What is asked", Settings' choices and its language list: the picked row in the container colour with the 2 dp accent outline and a ✓, no radio dots; Ear training's "Notes in a row", a count, is now separate faces as the metronome's values (`ChoiceFace`, moved from the metronome); Strings/Chromatic and the exercise stay joined bars; TalkBack still hears radio buttons)
- ☑ **Icons that say what they are:** the tuner's *Chord name* and Practice's *Chords* share the chord-grid icon: Chord name gets its own (e.g. the chord's name over a strum). Ear training's exercise switch shows three note symbols that are hard to tell apart, where the tuner's switch has words: words, or clearer icons with the same steady size, as picked in the mock-up (2026-10-31: Chord name's icon is "C7" over a strum's wave; Ear training's switch is the app's switch of words, "Play back · Intervals · Chords" / "Naspelen · Intervallen · Akkoorden", one steady width, set smaller where they don't fit, TalkBack hearing the whole names)
- ☑ **Small fixes:** (with two loose ends of the shared controls: the drone panel's rows are `ChoiceRow`s with the ✓, the tuning editor's − + the shared `StepperButton`)
  - The 12th-fret check says it twice: "Which string?" in big letters, then "Choose a string" right under it; keep one
  - Settings: the last group (Language, Show me around, About) has the only missing heading; the divider lines inside *Look* fall between some settings but not others
