## Phase 64 — Must-fix before the next release (the launch review, 2026-11-01)
Goal: the last full review before launch (2026-11-01: code, UX and TalkBack, production readiness, DSP, tests and security, and the look on the S26 with that day's build) found no blocker, but two ways the app can crash, a question that sounds after Ear training is left, no automatic guard on the "no internet" promise, and a privacy policy behind the saved settings. They go into the version that goes to production: `/roadmap-step 64` before the next `/release`. No switch of their own: they are repairs inside the tools (Ear training's go with its switch). In order:
- ☑ **Ear training survives losing the microphone:** `EarTrainingViewModel.hear()` (behind the `heard` flow) catches the recorder's errors as the tuner, Listen and the watch already do. Today an `AudioRecorderException` during Play it back reaches `viewModelScope` uncaught and Android closes the app: the phone locked or Home pressed within the 5 s grace (Android 10+ silences the mic and `ChunkReader` throws `MicrophoneBusy`), a call, the Assistant, Android 12's microphone switch, or the mic failing to open. Instead the exercise shows one short line that the microphone stopped (a polite live region for TalkBack) and listens again when the screen comes back (`ON_START`), as the watch's `shown()`. `EarTrainingViewModelTest` with a fake listener that throws, while listening and on opening
- ☑ **No question after Ear training is left:** hiding the screen (`ON_STOP` → `stopSound()`) also cancels `nextJob`, so the next question, due 1.5–2.5 s after an answer, no longer sounds in the background and takes the audio focus from the player's music; it is asked when the screen shows again. `EarTrainingViewModelTest` in virtual time
- ☑ **A failed save never crashes:** reading the settings is guarded (`retryWhen`, `ReplaceFileCorruptionHandler`), writing isn't: every `dataStore.edit` throws `IOException` on a full phone or a storage error, and its ~49 callers launch it bare, the first-use notes in `MainActivity.onCreate` among them, so a full phone would crash at every start. Every write in `DataStoreSettingsRepository` goes through one private helper that catches `IOException` and logs it, so the app keeps running with the old value; the tuning editor's Save says it couldn't save (new text) instead of closing as if it had. JVM test with a store whose writes throw: each setter returns normally, the editor shows its line
- ☑ **The "no internet" promise checked by the build:** today only a comment in `gradle/libs.versions.toml` says the merged manifest was checked by hand, so a library update that brings `INTERNET` along would ship unnoticed and break the README, the store text and the Data safety answer. A check on the merged release manifests of `:app` and `:wear` (e.g. a task after `process*ReleaseMainManifest` that `check` and `test` run, so every local run and the nightly CI see it) fails on any permission outside an allowlist (today `RECORD_AUDIO`, `VIBRATE`, AndroidX's signature-level `DYNAMIC_RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED_PERMISSION`, and what the watch has now), naming `INTERNET`, `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` and `AD_ID` in its message; `scripts/release.sh bundle` refuses a bundle with one (`bundletool dump manifest`). The comment in the catalog then points to the check, and performance.md's *Release build* says it
- ☑ **The privacy policy names the metronome's new settings:** PRIVACY.md and PRIVACY.nl.md, *Settings stay on your phone*, add the clicks between the beats and the speed trainer's settings (Phase 62, `metronome_subdivision` and `metronome_trainer_*`), with a new date; `scripts/publish-privacy.sh` after the merge (2026-11-01: under *Practice*, the clicks between the beats and the speed trainer's start tempo, goal, step, bars per step and whether it is on, in plain words; dated 1 November 2026. Every other key in `DataStoreSettingsRepository` and the watch's one setting were already named, nothing saved since Phase 62 is new; published after the merge)
- ☑ New texts in English and Dutch (the night languages by `/translate`); `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug`; Ear training's and the tuning editor's UI tests; on a test phone: Play it back listening, lock the phone, wait 10 s and unlock: no crash, the line shows, and it listens again; answer and press Home at once: no question sounds, the music plays on (2026-11-01: `ear_mic_stopped` and `tuning_editor_not_saved` in English and Dutch, the night languages wait for `/translate`; `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug` passes on main with #540–#542; on the Xiaomi, Ear training's UI tests 21, 0 failed, and the tuning editor's 15, 0 failed. Play it back listening, the screen off for 10 s and on again without unlocking: Android silenced the recording and Twangle let go of it, the same process alive, nothing in the crash log. The Xiaomi has a pattern lock, so the unlock, the line and listening again were checked through Home instead, which stops the screen the same way (#540), as was answering and pressing Home at once)

## Phase 65 — The launch review's sweep (2026-11-01)
Goal: everything else the launch review found, each item small. Nothing here blocks launch; the first two items belong before the next `/release`, the rest any time (`/roadmap-step 65`). Where the look changes noticeably, a mock-up comes first and **the owner** picks (in Firefox, `docs/design/`). Nothing new to switch: each change goes with its tool's switch, and the tuner's own changes need none. Left out on purpose: the meter's needle passing over "CENTS" (as on a real meter's face, by design), the icon's mustard (the owner's pick, 2026-10-15), and what the overnight run finds and fixes by itself (translations, UI tests in every style and language). In order:
- ☑ **The release process catches what it missed** (before the next `/release`): the release skill's version step keeps a version that has no `v<version>` tag yet and rewrites its notes instead of bumping again (today it would make 1.2.0 and leave 1.1.0's notes, `changelogs/10100.txt`, behind); `scripts/release.sh bundle` refuses when the Baseline Profile (`app/src/release/generated/`) was last committed before the last change under `app/src/main`, `feature/` or `core/designsystem`, unless told to go ahead, and the skill names its regeneration (performance.md, *Baseline Profile*); Play's current rule for Wear OS bundles checked (Play now seems to take them only on dedicated Wear OS tracks, `wear:internal` and so on), and docs/release.md and `release.sh upload` follow it before the update that brings the watch app — done 2026-11-01: `bump` counts from the last tag (1.1.0 is kept), `bundle` refuses an old profile (`--keep-old-profile`; `release.sh profile` checks it: today it is 124 screen commits behind, so the next release regenerates it on the Xiaomi first) and builds the watch bundle too; Play takes watch bundles only on Wear OS tracks since September 2023, so `upload|promote|rollout|halt watch` use `wear:qa`, `wear:alpha`, `wear:production` (docs/release.md, *The watch app*)
- ☑ **The store description catches up** (before the next `/release`; done 2026-11-01, the longest, German and French, at 3,966 of 4,000 characters; not sent yet): the full description lists what came since 1.0.2, none of which it names today: the banjo and the 12-string guitar, Ear training (Play it back, Intervals, Chord type), clicks between the beats and the speed trainer; in every store folder, by docs/store-listing.md's rules, `StoreListingTest` passing; sent with `scripts/release.sh listing` with the release, after the owner's yes

Fully offline.", `feature_graphic.txt`) beside three styles' meters fanned out on Contemporary dark, all 27 committed (about 6 MB). `StoreListingTest` checks every language's line and graphic; en-US and nl-NL screenshots made on the Xiaomi and looked at at Play's size
- ☑ **Code, small and safe:** the wave and strobe get the exact cents (a `() -> Float?` like `needleCents`; today the whole-cent `asShown()` value, so 0.6 and 1.4 cents drift alike); the tuner's looks seeded from the settings already read at start (`display`, `meterLook`, `waveLook` and the rest now flash their defaults for a frame or two); the settings read once in `AppContainer` (`stateIn`) instead of parsed by each of ~38 collectors on every write; Listen says the microphone is busy instead of "No steady beat heard" when the recorder fails (likely on Android 8–9 right after the tuner); a non-finite sample replaced by 0 in `FrameAssembler.push`, so one NaN can't silence the hum canceller and the low-note tuner; tuning names cut at a whole character (`BreakIterator`), not inside an emoji, in both places; `ChordShapeFinderTest`'s timing test moved out of `test` (a `*Benchmark`); the widget's and the app's scopes log exceptions instead of crashing (`TuningWidget.follow`); `mic_refused` left out of backups (a restored phone now gives up asking too early) with `dataExtractionRules` for the watch's too; the six newer saved enums in `app/proguard-rules.pro`; StrictMode logging in debug builds; `IntonationView`'s and `ChordView`'s brushes cached; the `Float?` per-frame lambdas without boxing. Tests for each where it can fail (`NaN` and clipped input in `EdgeCaseTest`, within ±1 cent with no octave flips; a damaged settings file through the real factory gives the defaults)
  - ☑ Part 1, the sound and the readings: the wave and strobe read the exact cents (a `LiveFloat`, the needle's, NaN for no note) instead of the whole cents the text shows; a non-finite sample goes into `FrameAssembler` as 0, before the hum canceller (`EdgeCaseTest`: a NaN, +∞ or −∞ every 0.1 s and input clipped 4–12× too loud, both ranges, within 1 cent, no octave flips; the NaN test fails without the fix); Listen says "Microphone is busy  Try again" when the recorder fails ("Microphone is off" if the permission went missing); `ChordShapeFinderTest`'s timing test is `ChordShapeFinderBenchmark`; `IntonationView`'s and `ChordView`'s field brushes cached (`drawWithCache`); the per-frame `() -> Float?` lambdas (cents, reception) are `LiveFloat`s that box nothing, and the signal bars' derived state an Int, not a Pair
  - ☑ Part 2, the settings and the safety nets: the tuner's looks seeded from the settings already read at start; the settings read once in `AppContainer` (`stateIn`); tuning names cut at a whole character (`BreakIterator`) in both places; the widget's and the app's scopes log exceptions instead of crashing (`TuningWidget.follow`); `mic_refused` left out of backups, with `dataExtractionRules` for the watch's too; the six newer saved enums in `app/proguard-rules.pro`; StrictMode logging in debug builds; a damaged settings file through the real factory gives the defaults — done 2026-11-01: the tuner's options all start from the settings `MainActivity` read before drawing (`TunerViewModel(saved = …)`); the settings turned into `AppSettings` once per change for every collector inside the repository (not a `stateIn` in `AppContainer`, which would lag a write); `CustomTuning.cutName` (`BreakIterator`) in the name and the editor's field; `follow` catches a failed redraw and the app's and receiver's scopes log (`loggingScope`); `mic_refused` moved to `this_device.preferences_pb` (a DataStore migration), left out by `backup_rules.xml` and `data_extraction_rules.xml`, the watch's `twangle_wear.xml` too; `Subdivision`, `Exercise`, `NoteChoice`, `NoteMatch`, `Interval`, `IntervalDirection` kept by R8, guarded by `SavedEnumRulesTest`; StrictMode logging in debug builds
- ☑ **One frame chain:** `PitchPipeline`, `WatchPitchPipeline` and Ear training's listening share one `:core:dsp` chain (assemble → gate → sustain → detect → noise floor, and setting aside our own sound with its tail), so a fix like Phase 64's lands once; only if it stays simpler than three copies, the existing tests passing unchanged — done 2026-11-01, decided after comparing them: Ear training already heard through the tuner's own `PitchPipeline`, so the copies were the phone's, the watch's and the tests' `TunerChain` (which mirrored the phone's by hand). The frame chain is now one `:core:dsp` class, `PitchChain` (assemble, in the low range through the hum canceller → gate → sustain check → YIN → mains-pitch rule → noise floor; both ranges on one ring buffer), run by all three, so `EdgeCaseTest`, `LowPitchRangeTest` and `RealPluckTest` now test the app's own chain; `PitchChainTest` on synthetic signals. Setting aside our own sound is **not** shared: the tuner (readings flow, shown as listening, strum check and chord name held off, smoothing restarted at the end), Ear training (per frame, smoother reset every frame) and the watch (its tap skipped before the gate, the reading held) differ in every rule, so one class would need an option each and be longer than the few lines each has (signal-pipeline.md, *One frame chain*). Every existing test unchanged and passing; no slower (`YinBenchmark` the same before and after, the hand-written chain 0.21 → 0.20 ms; on the Xiaomi `PitchChain` per hop 0.37 ms standard, 0.91 low, 0.49 at 16 kHz, as the copies it replaced); the tuner and Ear training checked on the Xiaomi with a laptop tone (A3 read at 0 cents, 220.0 Hz; Ear training heard it), the watch's 26 UI tests on the Wear OS emulator
- ☑ Docs brought up to date where a decision changed (ui-meter-and-styles.md, ui.md, lifecycle.md, testing.md); the website's pages for the tools whose words changed (English and Dutch), then `scripts/publish-website.sh` after the merge — done 2026-11-01: every PR of the phase had brought its own docs and website pages along (checked against each diff); left were ARCHITECTURE.md's package list (`SystemBars` gone, `LiveFloat` new), testing.md's `:app` and `:core:settings` rows (`SavedEnumRulesTest`, `TuningWidgetFollowTest`, the damaged settings file), ui-tuner.md's Ancient numbers note and the 12-string's "8" (below); `website/build.py` passes, the website's pages needed no change
- ☑ **Loose ends found on the way** (2026-11-01): on a 12-string the tick covered the "8" on the four octave courses (and a 12th-fret result did): the "8" now sits at the button's top left, across from the badge, in every style, no button changing size (`StringRowStylesTest.theTwelveStringsEightShowsBesideItsTick`, which fails with the old place); and *Contrast and edges* had lined the header up on its side through an alignment line, which left the string buttons' place for touch, TalkBack and the tests behind their place on screen in Classic, Space Age and Ancient (only the every-style run of `StringRowStylesTest` saw it): the guitar controls now hand the header the faces' width through snapshot state (`StringFacesSpan`), and that test checks focus mode's button ends where the strum switch does
- ☑ `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug`; the touched modules' UI tests; a check on a test phone in every style, light and dark, and on the tablet for the edges item — done 2026-11-01: `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug` passed; `scripts/ui-tests.sh --since-pass` ran the seven modules changed since they last passed (`:core:testing`, `:feature:tuner`, `:feature:settings`, `:feature:practice`, `:feature:eartraining`, `:core:audio`, `:app`; the metronome had passed since its last change): 496 tests, 0 failed; `StringRowStylesTest` in all six styles, 42 tests, 0 failed; `scripts/style-screens.sh` on the Xiaomi, all 12 pictures (six styles, light and dark) looked at; the app started on the Xiaomi with nothing in the crash log; the tablet and the real watch weren't connected, so they are in *Owner checks* (*Phases 63 and 65 on the tablet and the watch*)

## Phase 66 — The 2.0 plan (with the owner, before any code)
Goal: every phase below made precise against today's code, so each later session can build without redesigning.
- ☑ What is rebuilt and what is reused as is, module by module (`:core:*` untouched; which composables of `:core:designsystem`, `:feature:tuner`, `:feature:settings`, `:feature:metronome`, `:feature:practice` and `:feature:eartraining` survive, which go): [docs/architecture/v2-plan.md](docs/architecture/v2-plan.md), *Modules: kept, changed, gone* (`:core:settings` changes its Kotlin, not its storage)
- ☑ The lens box's contract written down (its size upright and wide, left flat and right sharp, the in-tune zone, the colours, the quiet state, how a look is drawn inside it) and where today's code gives the three looks: the pitch trail tool, `LightArc.kt`, the strobe wave look: v2-plan.md, *The lens* (the zone is the tuner's own window, 5 cents or 2 with Precise, not the mock-ups' placeholder 3)
- ☑ The 1.1 settings that 2.0 reads and what each becomes (a saved style, meter look, wave look, tuner display, focus mode, `dynamicColor`), so an update keeps everyone's tunings, A4 and notation and reads an old choice as the one look: v2-plan.md, *Settings from 1.1* (every key; no key renamed, no migration; a 1.1 Light arc stays the Light arc, a 1.1 strobe wave becomes the strobe, everything else the trail)
- ☑ The order of the phases below confirmed or changed, their estimates, and the test strategy: which UI tests are kept, which are rewritten, which go with the styles: v2-plan.md, *Order and size* and *Tests* (owner, 2026-11-02: the order kept but for No tour, moved up to Phase 68 and the phases after it renumbered; about 30 increments; the UI tests run in Ink alone after Phase 67)
- ☑ The overnight run, `scripts/ui-tests.sh`'s notes and the other scripts pointed at `v2` where they assume `main`; the nightly CI stays on `main` (2026-11-02: a 2.0 branch is scoped from where it left `v2`, and v2's passes and overnight records are kept apart, `build/ui-tests-passed/v2/` and `build/overnight/v2/`; the overnight run checks one branch a night, `main` when its code changed since its last clean run, otherwise `v2`, `scripts/overnight-ui-tests.sh --which`; releases, the website and the privacy page stay on `main`; #567, landed on `main` since the overnight run starts there, and carried to `v2`)
- ☑ Where the 12th-fret check and the pitch trail tool go once the trail is the meter: v2-plan.md, *The two tools* (owner, 2026-11-02: the 12th-fret check stays on the tuner behind a Settings switch, its way in mocked up in Phase 71; the pitch trail tool goes, its switch the trail look's brake)

**2.0, the design settled** (from ROADMAP.md's *2.0* part, archived with Phases 79 and 76 on 2026-11-06):
Decided on 2026-11-02, from the from-scratch design of 2026-11-01 ([IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md), *A fresh start*, with the first mock-up [docs/design/fresh-start-preview.html](docs/design/fresh-start-preview.html): the navigation, the metronome, the Practice cards, Settings, the watch) and the mock-ups of 2026-11-02: [docs/design/v2-tuner-screens-preview.html](docs/design/v2-tuner-screens-preview.html) (the tuner: six single designs, then the chosen combination with each of the three looks in the lens, and focus mode on its side) and [docs/design/v2-one-look-preview.html](docs/design/v2-one-look-preview.html) (four candidate looks, Ink chosen, and the picker sheet). Each page's top comment lists its decisions. **Listed first because it is what sessions work on now;** built on the `v2` branch until it landed on `main` on 2026-11-06. Phases 53, 56 and 58 below are the owner's launch and after-launch work on `main`.

**The design, settled (owner, 2026-11-02):**
- **One look, Ink:** blue-grey dark, near-white light, a plain sans, rounded corners; light, dark or the phone's choice (the switch stays in Settings); the phone's wallpaper colours as the one personalisation (on by default, Android 12 and up; off by default since the owner's review of 2026-11-03; amber and green stay the app's own). The six styles, the meter looks and the wave looks go; a saved one reads as the one look.
- **The tuner screen:** the top row with the gear (top left), the tab's name "Tune" in the middle and the focus mode button (top right), and the picker chip "Guitar · Standard ▾" on its own line right under it (owner, 2026-11-04, Phase 77: "each screen should just have the screen's name there"; until then the chip stood between the gear and focus mode; a phone on its side keeps it in the row's middle, where the rail names the tab); the ruler (the chromatic scale sliding under a fixed pointer: which note); the lens below it (how far: left flat, right sharp, the tuner's own in-tune zone, ±5 cents or ±2 with Precise, green in tune, quiet when the string dies); the whole screen softly tinted amber far off and green in tune (how close); one word at the bottom, Tighten · Loosen · In tune, with the cents small under it; the pegs as a plain row, the one heard ringed, tap to lock, hold to hear; the signal bars, behind their switch in Settings as today, beside the ruler's heard note. No mode switch, no big note (the ruler is the note), no cents pill, no hertz, no wave view, no strings backdrop.
- **Three meter looks, the one choice a player gets (Settings):** Classic (the default, 1.1's needle in Ink: a needle on a numbered half-circle dial), the Light arc (as today: 17 rays of 5 lights on the stretched scale), the strobe (three bands drifting flat or sharp, still in tune). The meter's box is the contract; only what is drawn inside it changes. Stop at three. The meter sits straight on the page, with no frame round it (owner, 2026-11-04, Phase 78; until then a rounded lens with a border and the pitch trail as the default look). The pitch trail is a Practice tool of its own, for singers and players without frets, behind its switch.
- **The word or the cents** (owner, 2026-11-05: Tighten / Loosen is a beginner's hint, and to an experienced player it repeats what the needle shows while the number they look at stays small): Settings' Look asks, under the Meter cards, what goes under the meter, *Words* (the default: Tighten · Loosen, the cents small under it) or *Cents* (the cents big in the word's place, nothing under it). A choice of what fills the place, not a switch that empties it, so the screen never changes shape. In tune and "Play a string" read the same in both, and TalkBack always says the direction. Done in Phase 78 (until then the word always, with no setting, 2026-11-02).
- **The string tick-off** (a tuned string's peg gets a check, "In tune. Play." once all have one) is a beginner helper: present, behind a toggle in Settings, off by default.
- **Focus mode:** one button, no switch in Settings; the same screen laid out wide on its side (the ruler across the full width, the lens wide, the word and the string small under it, the exit button in the same top-right corner), the pegs and tabs back with a tap; the turning of the whole app, Back and the tap stay as they are.
- **The one picker:** the chip (on its own line under the top row, on the tuner and every Practice tool, since Phase 77) opens "What are you tuning?": instrument pills, that instrument's tunings with their strings, Edit tunings, Any note as a switch. Instrument, tuning and chromatic in one place.
- **Navigation:** three tabs, Tune · Metronome · Practice; Settings and About behind the gear; Practice as rows (Chords, Scales, Capo, Ear training, and the pitch trail since Phase 78; big cards until the owner's review of 2026-11-04) under a "Last used" shortcut; no tour.
- **The 12th-fret check** is a tuner feature, not a Practice tool: it stays on the tuner, started from the last row of "What are you tuning?" (the owner's pick B of Phase 71's mock-up, 2026-11-03; no Settings switch), ended by the × on its panel or Back.
- **The strum check is a button above the pegs, and the chord name is Listen in Chords** (owner, 2026-11-03, after the guitar check: deciding by itself whether a sound was one string, a strum or a chord flickered). Until then: strum check and chord name happened by themselves, no mode to switch on.
- **Under the hood nothing changes:** `:core:dsp`, `:core:audio`, `:core:settings`' storage, the tunings, the reference tones, the drone, the metronome clock, the charts, Ear training's questions, no internet, no account, no ads. The watch and the widget follow the one look.
- **A new logo, Harmonics** (owner, 2026-11-04, Phase 79): one moment of a plucked string's first three harmonics, the whole in amber, the halves in blue, the thirds in green, on Ink dark ([docs/design/logo-v2-preview/](docs/design/logo-v2-preview/index.html), eight rounds). Until then 1.1's "twang to tune" on Retro's mustard.

## Phase 78 — The needle first, the meter on the page
The owner's rethink of 2026-11-04, after using v2: the pitch trail as the main meter "reminds me of a sperm cell swimming", and the meter in a lens frame sits awkwardly in the page. 1.1's Classic needle is the most recognisable and the most precise, so it becomes the default; the trail moves to Practice, where a held note (a voice, a fretless instrument) shows what it is good at.
- ☑ The needle as the meter's default look: 1.1's Classic dial (a needle on a half circle, ticks every 5 cents, numbers every 10, the in-tune zone, swinging a little past a new string like a mechanical needle) in Ink's plain finish; Settings' Meter reads Classic · Light arc · Strobe; a saved trail reads as Classic
  - 2026-11-04: `LensLook.CLASSIC` (1.1's saved name, so a 1.1 player who picked Classic gets it back), `meter/ClassicLook.kt` (`ClassicDial`, 1.1's 340 × 170 dial fitted to the box; `ClassicDialTest`), `MeterFeels.CLASSIC`; "Classic" in all 27 languages from 1.1's own translations; the trail's brake and its Settings card went, and the tuner keeps no history
- ☑ No frame: the lens's face, border and glow go, the meter sits on the page's tinted background as in 1.1 and spans the column's width (8 dp from the screen's edges instead of 20); its box (size, ♭ left and ♯ right, the quiet state) stays the contract
  - 2026-11-04: upright 0.6 times as high as wide (0.85 was for the trail), down to 0.45 on a short phone (half pushed the pegs down on a 360 × 570 dp one); on its side as before (24 dp in); the Settings cards' pictures without a frame too; the website's tuner page (English and Dutch) says needle
- ☑ The pitch trail as a Practice card behind its switch (`ToolSwitches.pitchTrail`, off in release builds until the owner's check): sing or play a held note and see the last seconds of it against the nearest note, with the note named; the website's pitch trail page (English and Dutch) follows
  - 2026-11-04: `PracticeTool.PITCH_TRAIL`, the screen and its ViewModel in `:feature:tuner` (`trail/`: the tuner's pipeline in Any note, the ruler, the trail in the meter's box made tall, the word, TalkBack's "wavering" / "bending up"), its own recorder; "Pitch trail" in every language from 1.1's translations, the card's line ("How steady is your note?") in English and Dutch for the night; the card's picture a line graph with no dot at its head; checked on the Xiaomi with a held A3 that then waved ±25 cents. The website's home and pitch trail pages (English and Dutch) put it under Practice. The store's pitch trail screenshot (number 8, focus mode with the old trail) is redone with the rest in Phase 76
- ☑ Practice's tools as rows, all one look, with "Last used" first (the owner, 2026-11-04, after seeing the fifth card: the 2 × 2 grid with a wide, low fifth card looked inconsistent; rows picked over a 3 × 2 grid with "Last used" as the sixth card); Ear training's picture centred
  - 2026-11-04: `PracticeCards` rows, one column upright and three on its side (two left the page too tall on a phone on its side), the "Last used" row a quiet shortcut above them (no outline; "Laatst gebruikt" in Dutch, the other languages at night); fits the S26, the Xiaomi, a phone on its side and the tablet without scrolling; the website's home pages (English and Dutch) follow

## Phase 74 — Strum check and chord name
Goal: a strum shows which strings are off and a fretted chord shows its name (first with no mode to switch on; since the owner's guitar check the strum check's button and Listen in Chords).
- ☑ DSP first: the strum detector and the chord detector next to the pitch detector on every frame, within the CPU budget (hard rule 2; `scripts/dsp-phone-benchmark.sh` before and after), telling a strum from one string without false alarms, against the recorded plucks and the synthetic strums
- ☑ The marks above the pegs (↑ ↓ ✓) and "4 of 6 in tune" in the word's place after a strum of open strings; a fretted chord's name in the ruler's place; the strum check and chord name tools and their switching on go (their switches stay as the brake)
- ☑ The owner's guitar check (2026-11-03): unreliable. Deciding by itself what each sound was flickered between tuning one note, checking all six strings and naming a chord; 1.1's strum check, switched on, was "pretty good and useful". The owner's decision: the strum check gets its button back, the chord name goes to Practice's Chords as Listen, and Scales gets no Listen
- ☑ The strum check's button above the pegs at their right end (beside them, after the last, on its side): a tap, then the next strum is read as a strum of the open strings, nothing decided; once it has rung out the button turns itself off and the strings are tuned one by one with the marks following them; the tuner names no chord any more. The website's strum check page (English and Dutch) and `scripts/strum-check.sh` (it taps the button) follow (2026-11-03: `StrumWatcher`'s `everySoundIsAStrum`, `TunerTool.STRUM_CHECK` back, 1.1's icon and its two texts with their translations, JVM and UI tests)
- ☑ The strum shown as 1.1's columns in the lens's place, not as marks above the pegs (the owner, 2026-11-03: the marks fell out of style and the lens showed random blips while the strum rang; option A of a rough mock-up, "stay close to 1.1"): from the button's tap until its × or Back, the strings tuned one by one inside it, each column following its string live
- ☑ The chord name as Listen in Practice's Chords: a round microphone button at the end of the name's row; strum a chord and it becomes the chord shown, its name and chart; the website's chord name page follows (2026-11-03: `ChordListener` with `StrumWatcher`'s `everySoundIsAChord` and 1.1's `ChordSettler`, the metronome Listen's way to the microphone, behind `ToolSwitches.chordNaming`)
- ☑ The tuner's unused chord display goes: `ChordInRuler` and `chordWords` (`components/ChordView.kt`), `HeardStrum.FrettedChord`, `TunerOptions.chordsHeard`, `ChordNameStylesTest`, and the `tuner_chord_*` texts in every language and the translation record (the tuner names no chord since the strum check's button); with it the tuner's whole chord path (`ChordNameInterpreter`, `TunerUiState.ChordName`, `StrumHearing.namesChords`)
- ☑ The owner's guitar check of both on the Xiaomi: tap the strum button, strum the open strings with one or two off, the columns and "4 of 6 in tune" right, the button off again once it has rung out, then tune those strings one by one and see their columns turn ✓; pluck strings one at a time without the button and see no panel; in Chords, Listen names a few chords (open, barre, a power chord) and shows their charts
  - 2026-11-05: passed (the owner)

## Phase 80 — A metronome on the watch
Goal: the watch app gets a plain metronome next to its tuner. The owner asked for it on 2026-11-05 ("in v2 the watch app only has a basic tuner"), taking [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md)'s *A silent metronome on the wrist* (card 4 of [docs/design/watch-ideas-preview.html](docs/design/watch-ideas-preview.html)) without waiting for its condition, and chose (2026-11-05): the beat as a tap on the wrist, the first beat of the bar stronger, with the click from the watch's speaker as an option; only the tempo and start/stop, four beats to the bar, no meter choice and no tap tempo; reached by a swipe from the tuner and by the tile's Metronome button. A tool of its own, so it has a switch (off in release builds until the owner's check). The phone's metronome clock (`MetronomePlayer`, `ClickTrack`) and its click sounds run on the watch as they are.
- ☑ A rough mock-up in Ink on the Galaxy Watch 5's round screen: the tempo in the middle, four beat lights round the ring, ▶ / ■, the speaker button, the swipe from the tuner (two pages), the tile with its Metronome button. Built on the owner's pick, or on Claude's recommendation if the owner says go on
  - 2026-11-05: [docs/design/watch-metronome-preview.html](docs/design/watch-metronome-preview.html): the tuner with two page dots, swiping left to the metronome and back; the metronome stopped (the tempo as large as the tuner's note, "BPM" small under it, a round ▶ under that, a small speaker button above it, off meaning taps only, four beat lights round the ring 11 % in from the edge with the first at 12 o'clock, a faint arrow at the edge for the bezel or crown); playing, live (the light steps round, the first beat's in the accent and wider, ■ for ▶, a small shake of the watch on each beat, the first stronger, short clicks only with the speaker on); the tile with Tune kept as its edge button and a wide Metronome button with the last tempo above it, the mark dropped. Built on Claude's recommendation unless the owner asks for changes
- ☑ The timing first, on the Galaxy Watch 5: the clock running with the click off (silent) and on, a wrist tap on every beat, the downbeat's stronger, steady for 10 minutes at 120 BPM with the screen kept on (a drift check like `scripts/metronome-drift.sh`); it decides how the taps follow the clock, and whether the watch's vibration keeps time well enough to build on
  - 2026-11-05: `scripts/watch-metronome-timing.sh` (`WatchMetronomeTimingMeasurement`, on demand) on the Galaxy Watch 5, 10 minutes silent and 2 minutes with the click at volume 1 of 15, 120 BPM in 4/4, the screen kept on: the clock −0.1 ppm against the watch's (audible +1.3), +0.14 ms off a perfect grid after 10 minutes, no missed beat or underrun; 1,196 of 1,196 beats tapped once each, every vibrate call 0.4–7.3 ms after its moment (median 1.2, 95 % within 1.9). The design it decided ([strum-and-metronome.md](docs/architecture/strum-and-metronome.md), *On the watch*): the click off is the same track playing silence (`MetronomePlayer.setSilent`), so the clock never changes hands; the taps follow the player's new `upcomingBeats`, told 0.1–0.3 s ahead, from a parked thread of their own at urgent-audio priority (`WatchBeatTaps`), each 12 ms before the beat, since the watch's vibrator service starts the motor 2–25 ms after the call (mean 12), the one spread no app can change; tapping as each beat was told as heard went out 5–45 ms late. Every tap at full strength, a plain beat 150 ms and the downbeat 250 ms (shorter from 172 BPM); whether that is felt as clearly stronger goes into the owner's check below
- ☑ The metronome page: a swipe left from the tuner (the microphone let go while it shows, hard rule 4), the tempo turned by the bezel or crown (30–250 BPM), ▶ / ■, the beat lights, the taps, the speaker button; the screen kept on while it plays, the clicks stopped when the app is left; the tempo and the speaker choice kept on the watch (a line in the privacy policy); behind its switch; TalkBack says the tempo and whether it plays
  - 2026-11-05: the watch app has two pages with dots, the tuner and a swipe left the metronome, as the mock-up drew it (the tempo large with "BPM" under it, ▶ / ■ under that, the speaker button above, four beat lights round the ring, the first in the accent and wider, held until the next beat); 1 BPM a bezel or crown click, live while it plays; a tap on every beat, the downbeat longer; the click off by default; the screen on while it plays; it stops when the page is swiped away, the app leaves the screen or the screen goes off. The tempo and the click choice are kept on the watch only (its own DataStore, out of the backup; the policy on `v2` says so, dated 5 November 2026, not published). Behind `WatchToolSwitches.metronome`, on in debug builds, off in release builds. TalkBack: "96 beats per minute, Playing", settable as a slider, "Start" / "Stop", the speaker a switch. Its texts in English and Dutch (the rest at night). Checked on the Galaxy Watch 5: all 43 watch UI tests, the tuner's mic let go 5 s after the swipe and taken back on return, never taken when opened on the metronome, the taps at 123 BPM in the vibrator's log (filed as touch vibration: the watch's touch-vibration setting must be on, as it is on the owner's watch), the click at volume 1, no tap after Home, no crash
- ☑ The tile's Metronome button, opening the metronome page at the last tempo
  - 2026-11-05: as the mock-up's card 4: "Twangle", a wide Metronome button (the phone's metronome icon in the accent, "Metronome", the kept tempo under it, "96 BPM") and Tune still the edge button; the mark makes room. Only with the metronome's switch on (debug builds); off, the tile as before. The tempo read from the watch's kept choices on each tile request, and a new tempo asks the watch for the tile again (no timer). Metronome opens the page stopped at that tempo, Tune the tuner; the watch app is `singleTask` now, since a tile tap with the app left open on the tuner stacked a second copy of it. TalkBack: "Open the metronome, 96 beats per minute", "Open the tuner". The tile list's preview shows the button in debug builds and the mark in release builds. Checked on the Galaxy Watch 5: the tile with 123 and, after a turn, 96 BPM; Metronome from a cold start and with the app left on the tuner (one copy), Tune with it left on the metronome, the bezel straight after; all 45 watch UI tests, no crash

## Phase 79 — A new logo
Goal: the icon, the opening screen and the pictures round the app in 2.0's look. The owner asked for a whole new logo matching Ink, not new colours; after eight rounds of mock-ups they picked *Harmonics*, round 8's A.

  - 2026-11-05: seven flat shapes (the seven overlaps' colours, as the mock-up blends them on Ink dark), about 4 KB a file; the themed icon the three harmonics in white at 50, 75 and 100 %; the watch mark the same cropped to them. Checked on the Xiaomi: the home screen's icon matches the mock-up's A, the opening screen in dark mode shows only the harmonics; the phone app's UI tests pass. Not yet seen: the opening screen in light mode, themed icons on, the watch (not connected), so they stay in the owner's look below

Icon, themed icon, opening animation and the store's icon and 27 feature graphics made again; seen on the Xiaomi through the store icon it draws from the installed app. Of the mock-up's lockups the owner liked *The name ringing* (the name on a string, its harmonics behind the letters) for the website and the store graphic

(at first a centred line under the version with "Twangle helps you bring yours in tune." after it; the owner: no need to advertise there, and it read as part of the name and icon), in English and Dutch (Dutch: "Twang is Engels voor…"; the other languages at night, told to say that the word is English), and a short *Why "Twangle"?* at the end of the website's home page, English and Dutch. ## Phase 82 — A microphone that works on every phone
Goal: Twangle hears the player on phones of brands nobody here owns, where the microphone acts differently from the Xiaomi's and the S26's. Found by the compatibility review of 2026-10-29 (its risks 1, 3 and 5, which the owner asked to fix that day); a first try was left half-written, untested and uncommitted (the `mic-fallbacks` worktree, deleted 2026-11-07), so this starts again from `main`. After Phase 81, which may change the same recorder code. No switch: repairs inside the microphone, which every listener shares. In `:core:audio`, JVM-tested with the fake microphone (`MicInput`, `ChunkReaderTest`, `MicOpenerTest`):
- ☑ **A silent input gives way to the next one** (2026-11-08: `FallbackReader` and `SkippedInputs` in `:core:audio`, `FallbackReaderTest`; the switch keeps the session's rate, and a muted microphone says busy after about 4 s instead of 2 where a phone lists both inputs): some phones say they support `UNPROCESSED` but record only exact zeros on it; today `ChunkReader` calls that "Microphone is busy" after 2 s and Try again opens the same input again, forever, so the tuner never works there although `VOICE_RECOGNITION` would. An input silent from its first sample is skipped for the next one, and kept skipped (for the app's life) only once the next one hears the room; if that is silent too, the microphone itself is muted (the privacy switch, another app) and the busy message stays as today. Tests: a fake phone whose unprocessed input gives zeros and whose speech input hears noise reads notes within one session; a fully muted fake still says busy, and the skipped input is tried again next time
- ☑ **Speech processing switched off where a phone leaves it on** (2026-11-08: `SpeechEffectsOff` behind `SpeechEffects` in `:core:audio`, `SpeechEffectsOffTest`; effects that are on are switched off and held until the recording ends, the rest left alone; the Xiaomi 12X logged `automatic gain: not on this phone; noise suppression: off; echo cancelling: off` on `UNPROCESSED`, so nothing changes there): some makers attach automatic gain, noise suppression or echo cancelling to the recording against Android's rules, which bends a string's level and its fading tail. Where Android lets an app control them (`AutomaticGainControl`, `NoiseSuppressor`, `AcousticEchoCanceler` on the recording's session), they are switched off for the whole recording and the handles let go when it ends; processing built into the microphone's own chip stays. Logged once which effects were found, so a tester's report can say
- ☑ **16-bit recording as a fallback** (2026-11-08: `SampleFormat` and `Pcm16Reads` in `:core:audio`, `Pcm16ReadsTest`; float at every rate and input first, 16-bit only once that whole search found nothing, and the format that opened tried first next time, like the rate; `sample / 32768`, so `Short.MIN_VALUE` is exactly −1 and 0 stays 0 for the silence check; one `ShortArray` per opened recording, 0 bytes allocated per chunk; the input switch and the speech effects work the same in 16-bit; on the Xiaomi `AndroidAudioRecorderTest.recordsIn16BitWhereFloatIsRefused` recorded the real microphone in 16-bit alone): where a phone refuses float recording at every rate (old Android 8–9 phones), the recorder asks for 16-bit samples and turns them into floats itself, allocating nothing per chunk (hard rule 2); `AudioConfig` and the pipelines see no difference. JVM test of the conversion (full scale to −1..1, no allocation)
- ☑ notes-and-audio.md (*Microphone*) says what the recorder now does; `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug`; `:core:audio`'s UI tests and the tuner's on the Xiaomi and, when it's plugged in, the S26 (one uses the unprocessed input, the other the speech one, docs/devices.md); the tuner reads a laptop tone on both. **Done (2026-11-08):** *Audio source selection* in notes-and-audio.md tells the whole order (format, rate, input), the silent-input switch, the speech effects and 16-bit; test, lint and assembleDebug pass; the UI tests of `:core:audio` (34), `:feature:tuner` (275), `:feature:metronome`, `:feature:practice`, `:app` and `:core:testing` passed on the Xiaomi 12X, which read a 7 s E4 tone from the laptop in tune (`UNPROCESSED`, float), crash log empty. The S26 wasn't plugged in, so its run (the speech input) waits for the next full check with it connected

## Phase 81 — The tuner doesn't hear TalkBack's own voice
*Archived 2026-11-08, built; its last item, the owner's check, is under* Owner checks *in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md).*
Goal: with TalkBack on and the phone's speaker loud, nothing that listens takes TalkBack's speech for a played note. The owner's check *TalkBack's own voice* failed on 2026-11-06 (Xiaomi 12X, 2.0.0 from Play): with nothing played the tuner showed notes while TalkBack spoke, and each spoken reading ("12 cents flat") made a new reading that was spoken in turn, a loop. 1.1 likely did the same; the check had never run. Before production: for a player who relies on TalkBack the tuner is unusable as it is.
- ☑ **Readings set aside while TalkBack speaks:** the microphone's frames are dropped while TalkBack's voice plays, plus a short tail for the room's echo, the way the reference tone's own sound is set aside (`ignoredFrames` in `TunerViewModel` and `EarTrainingViewModel`). TalkBack plays with the usage `USAGE_ASSISTANCE_ACCESSIBILITY`, and `AudioManager.registerAudioPlaybackCallback` (API 26, the app's minSdk) reports when such a sound starts and stops, other apps' sounds included; checked first on the Xiaomi and the S26 that it does for TalkBack, before building on it. The same for every listener: the tuner (its tools too: strum check, 12th-fret check, pitch trail), the metronome's Listen, Chords' Listen and Ear training's Play it back; the watch app the same where Wear OS's TalkBack reports it. Only while TalkBack (or another screen reader) is on, so nothing changes for anyone else. ◐ **The tuner done (2026-11-07):** `ScreenReaderSpeech` (`:core:audio`, `AppContainer.screenReaderSpeech`, `FakeScreenReaderSpeech` in its test fixtures) and a 0.4 s tail; the tuner, its strum check and 12th-fret check, and the pitch trail set the frames aside (checked on the Xiaomi first: TalkBack's speech is the speech engine's `AudioTrack` with that usage, one player per phrase). **Every listener done (2026-11-07):** the metronome's Listen (its tempo detector hears silence meanwhile), Chords' Listen (the chunks passed by, its strum analysis afresh after), Ear training's Play it back (set aside as for the question; no answer judged) and the watch's tuner (the reading held still, as for its wrist tap). The watch was unverified on hardware until **checked on the Galaxy Watch 5 (2026-11-07):** Wear OS's TalkBack (17.1) speaks through Samsung's speech engine (`com.samsung.SMT`) as an `AudioTrack` with `USAGE_ASSISTANCE_ACCESSIBILITY`, released once its phrase has played, its earcons a `SoundPool` with the same usage, and it turns touch exploration on (`dumpsys audio`, `dumpsys accessibility`), so the watch's setting-aside follows it as on the phone (TalkBack turned off again after)
- ☑ **A reading spoken once per note** (so a missed sound can't loop): TalkBack reads a reading when a played note settles, and again only when it changes enough to matter (in tune, or Tighten ↔ Loosen) or a new note is played, never on each small change; the watch's "calmly" rule is the model. **Done (2026-11-07):** a reading's key is the note and the side its word says (`SpokenReadingKey`, `:core:dsp`; the word is already held steady near the note, so a reading wobbling across it is one situation, where the cents' sign made three), plus a tip or the pitch trail's motion said with it; a situation is said once it has lasted 400 ms, and while it lasts TalkBack keeps the text it was given, cents included (until now the cents alone were said again every 2 s). The phone and the watch alike. A TalkBack player now hears "E 2, 12 cents flat, Tighten" once and then "E 2, in tune", not the cents on the way. Found on the Xiaomi with TalkBack on and nothing played: the waiting line and a tip that a soft sound in the room brings up ("Play a bit louder") were each said as they flipped, every few seconds, TalkBack's voice clearing the tip it had just said; now a text with no note that was one of the last two said is held for a touch, not announced
- ☑ **Tests:** unit tests for setting frames aside (a fake playback source: speech starts, stops, the tail) in every listener; a UI test that with a screen reader on the tuner's spoken reading doesn't change on its own. **Done (2026-11-07):** the unit tests in every listener (`ScreenReaderSpeechTunerTest`, `HeardStrumFlowTest`, `PitchTrailViewModelTest`, `ScreenReaderSpeechListenTest`, `ChordListenerTest`, `EarTrainingViewModelTest`, `WatchPitchPipelineTest`); the pacing in `SpokenReadingThrottleTest`, `SpokenReadingKeyTest` and `WatchSpokenReadingTest`; the UI tests `TunerContentTest.theSpokenReadingDoesNotChangeOnItsOwn` and, on the watch, `WatchTunerTest.onlyTheCentsMovingIsNeverSaidAgain` (passed on the Galaxy Watch 5, 2026-11-07, with all of `:wear`'s UI tests)
- ☐ **The owner's check *TalkBack's own voice* again, all three parts** (*Owner checks* above): only this is left; the phase is archived once it passes. Claude's spot check on the Xiaomi (2026-11-07, TalkBack on, a 9 s G3 tone drifting 30 → 12 cents flat from the laptop): the reading was said once ("G 3, 29 cents flat, Play a bit louder"), not again as its cents moved; with nothing played TalkBack stayed quiet after its own start-up words. After the tone, stray readings of the room's sounds ("E 4, 221 cents flat") were said every 5–10 s, each a new note; one came 0.55 s after TalkBack stopped speaking, so watch for a reading right after each spoken one: if it happens, the 0.4 s tail after TalkBack's voice (`ScreenReaderSpeech.TAIL_SECONDS`) may be too short

## Phase 85 — A tuning guide without words
*Archived 2026-11-08, built; the owner's eyes-closed check is under* Owner checks *in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md), and* What blind players say *went into Phase 56's* Blind and low-vision musicians.
Goal: a player who can't see the screen tunes by feel or by ear while the string rings, not by waiting for TalkBack's sentences. Asked by the owner on 2026-11-07, after Phase 81: TalkBack needs about 1–1.5 s per phrase (measured on the Xiaomi that day) while a plucked string lasts 2–4 s, so a spoken "12 cents flat" is out of date by the time it ends; both hands are busy (one on the peg, one plucking), so nothing can wait for a swipe. Speech stays for what changes slowly (the note heard, Tighten or Loosen, in tune, Phase 81's pacing); the guide adds what changes fast: how far off and which way, continuously. Two rules: the direction is always obvious (the meter's rule: how far alone is not enough), and the guide never fools the microphone, so no steady tone ever (it would put the very note being tuned into the mic just when the reading must be cleanest). An option inside the tuner, so it goes with the tuner's switch (CLAUDE.md, *Every tool has a switch*). After Phase 82. In order:
- ☑ **Heard and felt first, as a mock-up** (mock-up made: [docs/design/tuning-guide-preview.html](docs/design/tuning-guide-preview.html), PR #709; picked 2026-11-07, the owner leaving every choice to Claude, from what blind players say: vibration is praised, noise is the main complaint about phone tuners: **vibration only**; **single taps to tighten, double to loosen**, quicker as the string nears pitch, one long buzz in tune; **a row *Vibration guide* in *While tuning*** after *Vibrate when in tune*, **on by itself while a screen reader is on** until the player sets it; no Accessibility group in Settings: each such option sits where it belongs, lifecycle.md) ([docs/design/](docs/design/), opened in Firefox): a string drifting into tune, played with each guide side by side: vibration pulses getting quicker as the string nears in tune and one long buzz when in tune (on the page as a soft low thump, since a PC can't vibrate), and short ticks that do the same with their pitch for the direction (a low tick: too low, tighten; a high tick: too high, loosen) and a short chime when in tune. For the vibration the mock-up offers ways to say the direction (e.g. single pulses to tighten, double to loosen; or a longer pulse one way). **The owner picks** the guides, the direction cue and how the guide is turned on (a row in Settings' *While tuning*, next to *Vibrate when in tune*; on by itself while a screen reader is on, or not)
- ☑ **The vibration guide** (2026-11-07; `VibrationGuide` in `:core:dsp`, the setting `vibration_guide`; ui-tuner.md, *Vibration guide*: one 25 ms tap or two 90 ms apart, every 700 ms 30 cents off easing to 300 ms at the in-tune window's edge, a 375 ms buzz in tune in place of the click; on the Xiaomi the taps came in that pattern from a laptop tone drifting into tune, and the motor's hum wasn't heard: a 4 s buzz alone showed no note and in tune came as quickly with the guide on, so nothing is set aside): the pulses' rate follows the reading's cents (calm far off, quick close, the long buzz once in tune, then quiet while the string holds), in `:core:dsp` as a pure rule from cents to the next pulse (tested on synthetic readings) and played through the phone's vibrator as *Vibrate when in tune* does (`InTuneHaptic`); nothing while no note is heard, and it stops the moment the tuner stops listening. The vibrator's own hum in the mic checked on the Xiaomi: if the tuner hears it, its frames are set aside like the reference tone's
- ☑ **The tick guide: dropped for now** (2026-11-07, with the picks above: noise is what blind players complain of, and a tick from the speaker goes into the very microphone that must hear the string; in [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md), *A tick guide*, with its condition). As it was planned: the same rule played as short ticks through `AudioTrack` (`:core:audio`, a click like the metronome's, no allocation per tick), each tick's frames and a short tail set aside the way the reference tone's are (`ignoredFrames`), so the tuner never reads its own tick; checked on the Xiaomi that a played string still reads within the same time with the ticks on
- ☑ **On the watch:** the same guide as wrist taps (the watch already taps on in tune, Phase 61; the watch sits on the hand that turns the peg), behind the watch tuner's switch in `WatchToolSwitches`, plainly: one setting, no more (owner: the watch stays plain). **Done (2026-11-07;** ui-other-screens.md, *The vibration guide*): the phone's rule (`VibrationGuide`) with the watch's own lengths, `GuideTiming.WATCH`, since Phase 61 found the wrist doesn't feel short pulses while playing: 150 ms taps (one to tighten, two 100 ms apart to loosen), groups every 1.5 s far off easing to 0.8 s at 5 cents, quiet within the last 5 cents, one 600 ms buzz in tune in place of the in-tune tap. The watch's microphone hears its motor, so each pulse's frames are set aside (the reading holds still). Played as media vibrations, so the watch's touch-vibration setting can't silence it. One small round switch in the arc's opening under the cents, TalkBack "Vibration guide" / "Stemmen op gevoel", on by itself while TalkBack is on, kept on the watch (privacy policy updated). The watch tuner has no switch in `WatchToolSwitches`, so the guide has none either. On the Galaxy Watch 5 a laptop tone drifting into tune gave taps of 150 ms about a second apart, quiet within 5 cents, then one 600 ms buzz (`dumpsys vibrator_manager`, usage MEDIA; a sharp tone gave pairs); the reading held steady across them, and with nothing played for 35 s nothing was read and nothing tapped (a room sound the watch reads as a note gets its taps, as it gets a reading). `WatchGuideTest`, `WatchTunerViewModelTest`, `VibrationGuideTest`; on the watch `WatchTunerTest` (the switch, its TalkBack name in English and Dutch, its place) and `WatchInTuneTapDeviceTest` (no in-tune tap with the guide on, the real motor's pulses), all 51 of `:wear` passing. Not yet felt on a wrist with a guitar: the owner's check
- ☑ **Its texts and pages** (2026-11-07: the Settings texts in English and Dutch with the guide, the watch's with its switch; the website's tuner page a section *Tune by feel* / *Stemmen op gevoel*, the watch page its switch, the help page *Can I tune without looking at the screen?* (`help.html#without-looking`), the home page's TalkBack line the guide, in English and Dutch, published; docs/architecture's lifecycle.md, ui-tuner.md, ui-other-screens.md and ui.md's *Accessibility*): the setting's name and its TalkBack words in English and Dutch (the other languages at night), the tuner's page and the help page on the website (English and Dutch) say what the guide does, docs/architecture's tuner and accessibility files describe it; `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug`, the tuner's UI tests and the phone check
- ☐ **The owner's check, eyes closed:** TalkBack on, the guide on, phone on the table or in a pocket; tune all six strings without looking, first in a quiet room, then with music playing: each string reaches in tune at least as quickly as with speech alone (the bar: a blind Roadie user tunes a whole electric guitar by vibration in under 25 s, and a player on stage has 10–20 s between songs, AppleVis 2026), the direction is never mistaken, and the guide never makes the tuner show a note by itself
- ☐ **What blind players say** (after launch, with Phase 56's *Blind and low-vision musicians*): one question in that post, speech, vibration or ticks while you tune, and what they answer adjusts the guide or goes to [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md)

## Phase 86 — A high-contrast look for low-vision players
*Archived 2026-11-08, built; its last item, the owner's check, is under* Owner checks *in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md).*
Goal: a player who sees a little (blurred sight, a narrow field, the phone held close or magnified) reads the tuner at a glance. Asked by the owner on 2026-11-07: low-vision people say they keep one app over another for its high contrast. Ink reads at 4.5:1 in light and 7:1 for its muted text, but it leans on what partly sighted players lose first: grey secondary text, the soft in-tune glow, the screen's tint, the thin needle and scale marks, and the colour easing from amber to green. Twangle doesn't follow the phone's own contrast setting yet. A look, like light and dark, not a tool, so it needs no switch of its own (CLAUDE.md, *Every tool has a switch*); the meter's rule holds in it too (the direction obvious by position, not by colour). What low-vision players say after launch is asked in Phase 56's *Blind and low-vision musicians*. In order:
- ☑ **What low-vision players say first** (2026-11-08; the mock-up's *What low-vision players say*, with sources: high contrast is pure black and few strong colours, not a dark mode of greys (Perkins; Apple: grey on black is harder for low vision); no one scheme suits all (an RP player found light backgrounds intolerable, another reads black on yellow, a dark screen glares in daylight); those who need contrast set it for the whole phone (Apple; Samsung's high-contrast theme passed 15 million downloads), so Twangle follows the phone; tuners for them advertise big, bold, high-contrast displays; colour isn't relied on (inversion turns green pink). Phones: Android 14+'s contrast level (`UiModeManager.getContrast`, 0 to 1, with a listener; Ink doesn't follow it yet), high-contrast text on every Android (outlines text itself), Samsung's *Visibility enhancements* (its theme can't be read by an app), colour inversion and bold text everywhere; the Xiaomi 12X is Android 13 / HyperOS 1.0, so no contrast level, only high-contrast text (adb, 2026-11-08). Reddit was refused by the built-in browser this time), as Phase 85's research did (AppleVis, Accessible Android, low-vision forums; Reddit through the built-in browser, which Claude in Chrome can't open): which apps they keep for their contrast and what exactly they praise (white on black, yellow on black, thick lines, big digits, no thin type), and what phones offer by themselves (Android 14's contrast level, Samsung's high-contrast theme and fonts, Xiaomi's, colour inversion), so Twangle follows the phone where it can and asks only where it must
- ☑ **Seen first, as a mock-up** (mock-up made: [docs/design/high-contrast-preview.html](docs/design/high-contrast-preview.html), PR #718; picked by the owner 2026-11-08: **black and white**, white on black with dark and black on white with light, following the existing *Light or dark*; no yellow variant for now (in [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md), *Yellow on black*, if players ask); **a switch of its own in Settings → Look**, "High contrast", beside *Light or dark*, not a fourth choice in it; **on by itself** while the player hasn't set it, when the phone's contrast is high (Android 14+) or its high-contrast text is on, as the vibration guide follows TalkBack; **the watch not changed**: the watch stays plain) ([docs/design/](docs/design/), opened in Firefox; rough: the tuner, one Practice tool and Settings): the high-contrast look beside Ink light and dark: a pure black (or white) background, text in one ink with no grey, the needle and scale marks thicker, in tune shown by a clear mark and the word rather than by the glow and the tint, the note and the word as large as the screen allows; a yellow-on-black variant to compare. **The owner picks** the look and how it is chosen: a fourth choice in *Light or dark* (*Same as the phone · Light · Dark · High contrast*) or a row of its own in Look, and whether it comes on by itself when the phone's own contrast is high (`UiModeManager.getContrast`, Android 14+) or the phone's high-contrast theme is on
- ☑ **The look built** (2026-11-08; ui-meter-and-styles.md, *High contrast*, and lifecycle.md, *High contrast*: `paletteFor(dark, highContrast)`, pure black or white with one ink, the green `#3DDC84` / `#005A28` and the amber `#FFB648` / `#7A4000` kept, every text and meaningful line 7:1 or more (`HighContrastPaletteTest`); `StyleTokens.highContrast` with thicker lines (2×; Classic's needle 2.3×), nothing faded, no glow, tint or shadow, in tune as a check mark before the word; the switch `high_contrast` in Look following the phone's contrast level and high-contrast text until set (on the Xiaomi's Android 13 the high-contrast text turned it on, the player's flip held after); every screen through the theme, the meter, ruler, pegs, beat marks, chord chart, scale neck and dialogs drawn for it, focus mode included (the big meter in high contrast, upright and on its side), and the widget; the watch unchanged): a high-contrast `Palette` in `:core:designsystem` beside Ink's (`StylePalettes.kt`), 7:1 or more for every text and every line that carries meaning (the palette contrast tests, testing.md), the needle, scale marks and pegs drawn thicker in it, the glow and the tint left out or made plain, the in-tune green and the far-off amber kept apart from each other and from the accent as `keepingInTuneApart` does; on every screen (the tuner, the metronome, the Practice tools, Ear training, Settings, About) and the widget; the watch only if the owner wants it there (the watch stays plain)
- ☑ **Its texts and docs** (2026-11-08, with the look: "High contrast" and its line in English and Dutch, the other languages at night; a switch row, so TalkBack says its name, line and on or off with no words of its own, and the in-tune mark is a drawing beside the word TalkBack already says; ui-meter-and-styles.md, lifecycle.md and testing.md; `./gradlew test lint assembleDebug`, the scoped UI tests, the phone check in dark, light and at 2× text): the choice's name and its TalkBack words in English and Dutch (the other languages at night), ui-meter-and-styles.md (*Palette*) and lifecycle.md (*Light or dark*)
- ☑ **Its page and its place in the full check** (2026-11-08: the help page's *The screen is hard to read* / *Het scherm is moeilijk te lezen* (`help.html#high-contrast`), published; the full check's third stage, the six screen modules again with `scripts/ui-tests.sh --high-contrast` (`LocalHighContrastByDefault` from `:core:testing`, `HighContrastPassRule` for `:app`), its light and dark in one pass, about 12 minutes, so about 45 in all (testing.md, *Every screen light and dark*); its first run found three tests that assumed Ink (Classic's green needle in tune, the banjo's faded short string, the bar's accent mark), now aware of the look; on the Xiaomi at low brightness the tuner, the metronome and the widget on the home screen in white on black and black on white: the ruler's and the tempo ruler's outermost names and marks faded to grey at the ends, now whole or not at all in high contrast (`rulerEdgeFade`, `tempoEdgeFade`, with unit tests), and the number under the tempo cursor steps aside instead of turning grey): the website's help page in English and Dutch (what the look is, that it follows the phone's contrast, where the switch is), then `scripts/publish-website.sh`; the UI tests in the new look (the full check, which runs Ink light and dark, gains it as a third pass, `scripts/full-check.sh` and the full-check skill); the phone check at low brightness (7:1 is the aim there)
- ☐ **The owner's check** (*Owner checks* in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md), *The high-contrast look at a glance*): on the Xiaomi at low brightness, the phone at arm's length (or through the phone's magnifier): tune a string and start the metronome; the note, the direction and in tune read at a glance; then the same outside in daylight; and whether the picked one in a row of choices (the metronome's 4/4, a grey fill in this look) stands out enough

## Phase 87 — More languages, after 2.0
*Archived 2026-11-08, built: 36 languages. Still to do before the next `scripts/release.sh listing`: each new language's store screenshots, the phone set (`scripts/store-screenshots.sh`, made right after this phase) and the tablet set (the same script with the Lenovo tablet connected); the release itself waits for a full check, whose `/translate` and language checks now cover all 36.*
Goal: the biggest groups of people who can't read Twangle in any of its 25 languages get their own. Asked by the owner on 2026-11-05, after counting that the 25 languages reach roughly two-thirds of the world's people (about 55–60 % who can read). It waited for 2.0 to reach `main` (2026-11-06), so each language is translated once, against 2.0's texts. Urdu, Persian, Filipino and Malay stay out, as decided on 2026-10-28 and confirmed on 2026-11-05 (Phase 50's *More languages* in the archive: many phones in Pakistan and the Philippines are set to English, Indonesian is close to Malay, Google Play doesn't serve Iran). Each language is one `/add-language` (its texts in the phone app, the watch and the widget, its store listing, the note-name default, TalkBack's note names, digits, right to left, fonts, the phone check at 2× text, its store screenshots, the meaning review by a helper other than the one that translated it) and gets every release's "What's new" from then on. Every language added makes the night's `/translate`, the full check's language checks and the store screenshots a little longer (about 2 minutes a language for the screenshots). Before each one, check that Android phones offer it as a phone language and that Play has a store listing for it; a language with neither goes to [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md) with that reason, not into the app. The website in these languages is Phase 56's *The website in more languages*, not this phase. In this order, the most readers first:
- ☑ **Tamil, Telugu and Marathi** (`ta`, `te`, `mr`, India, about 80–95 million each): for players who read neither Hindi nor English; each is its own `/add-language`; Marathi shares Hindi's script (Devanagari), so Hindi's guitar loanwords are the place to start, and Android may write its numbers in Devanagari digits. Done 2026-11-08 (#723, #724, #726): Tamil, Telugu and Marathi, each with its store listing; Marathi's numbers are Devanagari digits, like Bengali's
- ☑ **Swahili, Amharic and Hausa** (`sw`, `am`, `ha`, East and West Africa): Swahili for Kenya and Tanzania, Amharic (its own Ethiopic script) for Ethiopia, Hausa for Nigeria and Niger only if Android and Play both offer it (see the check above). Done 2026-11-08: Swahili (#727) and Amharic (its Ethiopic letters from the phone's font), each with its store listing. Hausa left out: Android has it (`ha-GH`, `ha-NE`, `ha-NG`), but Google Play has no store listing language for it (Play Console's 86 listing languages, https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9844778, go from Gujarati to Hebrew); it waits in [IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md)
- ☑ **Burmese and Khmer** (`my`, `km`, Myanmar and Cambodia, almost all Android): both scripts stack letters above and below the line and put no spaces between words, so line breaks need the care Thai's got (languages.md, Thai); for Burmese, check that the phones there show standard Unicode Burmese, not the older Zawgyi encoding some still use. Done 2026-11-08 (#733, #734): Burmese in Unicode with Myanmar digits, Khmer with Western digits (Android's default for it), each with its store listing; both break lines only between words
- ☑ **Romanian, Czech, Greek and Hungarian** (`ro`, `cs`, `el`, `hu`, Europe, about 10–25 million each): check that the app's Nunito, which may be cut down to the letters it needs (`scripts/subset-font.py`), keeps ș ț, ě ř ů and ő ű; Greek has no letters in Nunito, so it comes from the phone's font like the other scripts. Romanian done (2026-11-08, #729), Czech done (2026-11-08, #730), Greek done (2026-11-08, #731; Nunito's five Greek maths letters, Δ Σ Ω μ π, are cut from its character map so Greek words stay in one font), Hungarian done (2026-11-08, #732; note names with H as Hungarian players write them, TalkBack says them the Hungarian way, Cisz, Esz, B, H; ő ű are in Nunito). Each with its store listing
- ☑ **The listings and docs say 36** (2026-11-08, the wrap-up PR): every Play listing's full description names all 36 languages in its own language and order (Greek and Tamil give the number with their own and English, as all 36 names would pass Play's 4000 characters), and its meter line names Classic, the needle, where it still named the pitch trail (a Practice tool since Phase 78); README, the website's home page (published), the store-listing and featuring docs, and the add-language skill's font step (Nunito sets Latin and Cyrillic, other scripts come from the phone's font, Greek's stray letters cut) brought up to date
